# Session Log

One entry per session. Append-only. Old entries archive at phase audits, never delete.

Format:
```
## Session N (Package P)
- Produced: [what shipped]
- Decisions: [D-numbers from decisions.md, or "none"]
- Tests: [pass/fail summary]
- Reflection: [one or two sentences. What was learned, what should change.]
```

---

## Session 1 (Package P1)
- Produced: Repo initialized. Charter committed at /charter/. Log structure at /log/sessions.md. Archive placeholders at /docs/archive/. README, CLAUDE.md, .gitignore, .env.example, Makefile stub at root. Conventional commits configured via .gitmessage.
- Decisions: D11 (scaffold grows incrementally).
- Tests: None. No code yet.
- Reflection: Directory layout was unspecified in the prompt and got resolved mid-session — the brief referenced `/charter/` while the working tree had `docs/charter/`, reorganized to match the brief before close. `docs/session-prompt.md` was written to the repo earlier and removed at close; canonical session structure now lives in CLAUDE.md plus the Claude.ai prompt. Charter content survived intact otherwise. Next session: align `docs/README.md` with the new layout (still references the old `docs/charter/` tree).

## Session 2 (Package P1)
- Produced: compose.yaml at repo root with postgres (pgvector/pgvector:pg17) and redis (redis:7-alpine), both with healthchecks and named volumes. .env.example extended with Postgres/Redis vars; local .env seeded from it. Makefile gained up/down/logs/ps/psql targets, help updated. Stale docs/README.md removed.
- Decisions: none (D11 reaffirmed by scoping S2 to Postgres + Redis only).
- Tests: `make up` → both services `(healthy)` within ~3s. `CREATE EXTENSION vector;` succeeds, `\dx` shows vector 0.8.2. `make down` removes containers, network, and reports nothing running.
- Reflection: pgvector and healthchecks came up clean on the first try — pg_isready and redis-cli ping are well-suited to Compose's healthcheck contract, no tuning needed. The `.env.example` wiring is already showing one mild strain: POSTGRES_USER/PASSWORD/DB are duplicated between the env block and the psql target's shell expansion (`$$POSTGRES_USER`), which is fine here but will multiply when LiteLLM, Langfuse, and Keycloak each want their own DB credentials. Worth watching whether per-service `env_file:` blocks or a secrets pattern emerges before P3's database-per-tenant work, rather than letting the flat .env grow to dozens of vars.

## Session 3 (Package P1)
- Produced: mkcert localhost cert generated into `./certs/` (gitignored); Caddyfile at repo root with `auto_https off`, `admin off`, a `/health` route returning `ok`, and a 503 catch-all. compose.yaml gained a `caddy` service (caddy:2-alpine) on port 443 mounting the Caddyfile and certs read-only, plus `caddy_data`/`caddy_config` named volumes. Makefile help line updated to mention the proxy. README gained a Local HTTPS section. P1 closed.
- Decisions: none (Caddy choice was decided pre-S3 in Claude.ai; reasoning captured in the session brief, not promoted to a D-number since it is a tooling choice rather than an architectural commitment).
- Tests: `make up` → caddy + postgres + redis all up, postgres and redis `(healthy)`. `curl https://localhost/health` → `200 ok` over HTTP/2 with no `-k` (mkcert CA in system trust store, trust chain valid). `curl https://localhost/` → `503` with the catch-all message, confirming unrouted requests fail visibly. `make down` removes all three containers and the network.
- Reflection: The placeholder catch-all did its job — sending a request to `/` returns the explicit "no backends configured yet" 503 rather than a confusing 404, which is exactly the failure mode worth preserving once P2 adds Keycloak. The Caddyfile is not yet showing strain: a single `localhost` site with two `handle` blocks is well within Caddyfile's sweet spot. The first mild pressure point will be when P2 adds Keycloak as the first real upstream: the catch-all must remain (still useful for unknown paths) while a `handle /auth/*` block is inserted ahead of it, and the cert path will need to keep working after the upstream is added — neither is structural strain, just a reminder that the ordering of `handle` blocks is load-bearing in Caddy and worth a brief comment when the second block lands.

## Session 4 (Package P2)
- Produced: Charter — D15 (Qwen 2.5 7B default dev model), D16 (bounded contexts on hexagonal with import-linter), D17 (query APIs for reads, event bus for state changes), D18 (shared Redis dev / config-swap path for prod), bounded-contexts principle under Architectural, P2 current-package block. Compose — five new services pinned to SHA256 digests (langfuse-db, clickhouse 24.12, minio chainguard, langfuse-web 3.172.0, langfuse-worker 3.172.0); existing redis shared with Langfuse via DB 1. Caddyfile — `/langfuse/*` handle_path ahead of catch-all with explicit ordering comment. .env.example — Langfuse-related placeholders; .env (gitignored) — generated secrets, ENCRYPTION_KEY in hex, INIT keys in `pk-lf-`/`sk-lf-` shape. schema.md untouched (Langfuse is an isolated subsystem with its own Postgres + ClickHouse + MinIO).
- Decisions: D15, D16, D17, D18.
- Tests: `make up` brings all 8 containers healthy in <30s on the warm path (langfuse-web flips healthy ~15s after start once Next.js Ready). `curl https://localhost/health` → `200 ok`; `curl https://localhost/` → `503`; `curl https://localhost/langfuse/api/public/health` → `{"status":"OK","version":"3.172.0"}`; `curl -I https://localhost/langfuse/` → `HTTP/2 200`. INIT bootstrap verified: `GET /api/public/projects` with the generated keys returns the `quorum` org and `quorum-dev` project. End-to-end ingestion: POST a `trace-create` to `/langfuse/api/public/ingestion` returned 201; after ~8s the trace was retrievable via `GET /api/public/traces/<id>` (POST → web → Redis DB 1 → worker → ClickHouse → queryable). `make down` removes all containers and the project network. Browser-driven login at `https://localhost/langfuse/` not run from CLI — left for operator confirmation.
- Reflection: Langfuse 3's realised deployment footprint is six services: langfuse-web, langfuse-worker, langfuse-db (Postgres for metadata), ClickHouse (trace data plane), Redis (shared with the existing Quorum instance via DB number per D18), and MinIO (S3-compatible event and media blob storage). The trace store moved off Postgres into ClickHouse in v3. This is consequent to D7 (self-hosted Langfuse 3); the alternative paths (Langfuse Cloud, different trace store) contradict either local-first or D7 itself. Footprint accepted. Watchpoint for Phase 1 close audit: if self-hosted observability tooling becomes a meaningful drag on operator time during Phase 1, that is data feeding the Phase 2 pivot decision. The original S4 prompt drafted by Claude.ai assumed a two-service Langfuse deployment, which Claude Code caught at the pre-write topology check; logged as a prompt-quality finding for future vendor-deployment prompts. Path-based routing under `/langfuse/*` worked at the HTML level but failed in the browser: Langfuse is a Next.js app whose `/_next/*` asset URLs only respect a basePath set at build time via `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH`, which would require building the image from source and would defeat the upstream-digest pinning landed this session. Switched to subdomain serving on `langfuse.localhost` instead; mkcert reissued with both SANs, Caddyfile gained a second site block, `*.localhost` resolves to 127.0.0.1 on macOS natively. This sets the pattern for future UI services (Keycloak as `auth.localhost`, Quorum app UI as `app.localhost`); UI services on subdomains, APIs on `localhost` paths. Headless `LANGFUSE_INIT_*` worked as documented — org/project/user/keys created on first boot with the .env-supplied values. Two other non-documented issues: Next.js binds to the container hostname interface by default rather than `0.0.0.0`, so `HOSTNAME=0.0.0.0` had to be added to both langfuse services; and busybox wget races IPv6 resolution of `localhost` against an IPv4-only listener, so the healthcheck uses `127.0.0.1` explicitly. Both are noted in the compose commit message. Flat `.env` strain is now visible — the file gained 18 Langfuse-related vars in a single session and is approaching the point where per-service `env_file:` blocks would be more legible than one growing file; the right answer is still to wait for S5's `platform/config/` and Pydantic Settings rather than introduce an interim structure that gets ripped out a session later. Shared Redis behaved cleanly via `REDIS_CONNECTION_STRING=redis://redis:6379/1` (Langfuse 3 supports the connection-string form alongside the discrete HOST/PORT/AUTH triple); no key-namespace collision risk observed since Langfuse uses prefixed keys (`bull:`, `langfuse:`). Charter writeup of D16/D17/D18 surfaced no need for adjustment before S5; the decisions feel proportionate to where the codebase actually is. schema.md correctly left untouched — Langfuse owns its own data plane.

## Session 6 (Package P2)
- Produced: Pre-write reconciliation against Ollama, LiteLLM, and Langfuse vendor docs reaffirmed D15 (Qwen 2.5 7B still the right default; Qwen 3 has shipped but the 7B-class slot is filled by `qwen3:8b`, ~14% larger memory cost, and tool-calling fidelity benchmarks are mixed rather than unambiguously better — substitution not warranted, evaluation candidate logged for the eval harness landing later). Five code commits plus the supply-chain commit and a charter-and-log close commit. Compose: `ollama` service (`ollama/ollama:0.22.0` pinned to multi-arch index digest, `ollama_data` volume at `/root/.ollama`, `OLLAMA_HOST=0.0.0.0:11434` so the daemon listens on something other than container loopback, `mem_limit: 12g`, healthcheck via `ollama list` because the image ships neither curl nor wget); `litellm` service (`ghcr.io/berriai/litellm:v1.83.10-stable` pinned, `/app/config.yaml` mounted from `ops/litellm/config.yaml`, healthcheck via Python urllib against `/health/liveliness`). Make: `pull-model` (idempotent, model name resolved from `zephyr.config.InferenceSettings.default_model`); `derive-env` (writes `.env.derived` from `zephyr.config.ObservabilitySettings`, gitignored via `.env.*`, regenerated on every `make up`); `smoke-llm` (resolves master key + model + verify URL through `ops/smoke_config.py`, runs the chat completion via `docker compose exec caddy wget` so no host port binding is needed for LiteLLM); `up`/`down`/`logs`/`ps`/`psql`/`pull-model` all switched to a `COMPOSE := docker compose --env-file .env --env-file .env.derived` variable. Config: `InferenceSettings` gained `litellm_master_key` (no default, fails Pydantic validation if `.env` is unset); `ObservabilitySettings` renamed `otel_collector_endpoint` → `otlp_endpoint`, default points at `http://langfuse-web:3000/api/public/otel/v1/traces`, gained `langfuse_project_id`, gained derived properties `otlp_basic_auth_header` and `otel_headers_env_value`, gained `validation_alias` mappings for `LANGFUSE_INIT_PROJECT_*_KEY` so Langfuse 3's headless-bootstrap env vars feed both Langfuse-side init and Quorum-side observability config without duplicating secrets. `ops/` introduced this session (`__init__.py`, `derive_env.py`, `smoke_config.py`, `litellm/config.yaml`). Supply chain: 7 new HIGH-severity findings on the new images (Ollama: Go stdlib + jsonparser inside the daemon binary; LiteLLM: PyJWT, orjson, setuptools, picomatch in shipped Node tooling) — all of the same shapes already covered by S5's exception categories, so `.trivyignore` got 7 entries and `docs/security/exceptions.md` got Categories B and C "Affects" lists extended. The `make scan` target needed no logic change — its image list is dynamically discovered from `compose.yaml`, so adding the two services automatically extended scan coverage.
- Decisions: none (D15 reaffirmed; D27 enforced by code; no new D-entry).
- Tests: `uv run pytest` → 32 passed (no test count change; the existing config tests cover `InferenceSettings` instantiation through the new `litellm_master_key` validation path). `make lint` → 3 import-linter contracts kept. `make scan` → 0 across 10 images with the documented exceptions; pip-audit reports no Python-side findings. `make sbom` → exits 0 with the deferral message (still S7). Stack health: 10 services, all healthy. End-to-end through LiteLLM: chat completion returns valid OpenAI-format with token counts (35 input, 6-8 output, 41-43 total) — the trace lands in Langfuse within ~4s with the GenAI semantic-convention attributes populated (`gen_ai.system="ollama_chat"`, `gen_ai.request.model="qwen2.5:7b"`, `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens=35`, `gen_ai.usage.output_tokens=6-8`, `gen_ai.usage.total_tokens=41-43`, `gen_ai.response.finish_reasons=["stop"]`) plus LiteLLM-specific metadata as additional attributes. Down/up cycle: `make down` removes all 10 containers and the project network; `make up` brings them back to all-healthy in ~30s on the warm path; `ollama list` after re-up shows `qwen2.5:7b` still present (volume persistence verified). Browser interactive verification of the Langfuse UI requested from the operator at session close (the API-side query confirms the trace structure but the browser is the acceptance signal per the S4 lesson now encoded in the four-session P2 block).
- Reflection: Pre-write reconciliation paid off twice. First, Ollama's `OLLAMA_HOST` defaults to `127.0.0.1:11434` inside the container (loopback-only) and would not have been reachable from sibling services without the explicit `0.0.0.0:11434` override; the FAQ doc surfaced the default and the problem before any compose change landed. Second, Langfuse 3's OTLP endpoint is signal-specific (`/api/public/otel/v1/traces`) rather than the generic `/api/public/otel`, and Langfuse 3 currently only accepts OTLP/HTTP — gRPC ingestion is not supported. Both reconciliations match the pattern S4 established: prompts written upstream in Claude.ai assume vendor-doc shapes the prompt author hasn't re-verified, and Claude Code catches the deltas before code lands. Worth keeping in the loop. D27's portability claim held in spirit but came with friction at the LiteLLM-emit edge: LiteLLM uses LiteLLM-specific env-var names (`OTEL_EXPORTER`, `OTEL_ENDPOINT`, `OTEL_HEADERS`) rather than the OTel SDK's canonical `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_*` form, and its `_get_headers_dictionary` parser splits on `=` once but does not URL-decode the value, so the W3C-Baggage-encoded `Authorization=Basic%20<b64>` from the OTel spec causes 401s at Langfuse — the literal space form must reach the env var instead. Verified by reading the LiteLLM source after a 401 surfaced on the first smoke run. The "consume OTLP" half of D27 is rock solid (Langfuse rendered the GenAI conventions correctly), the "any OTLP-capable emitter is interchangeable" half has more friction in practice than in principle. Worth surfacing if the eval harness later tries a non-LiteLLM emitter; the friction is at the emitter shape, not at the protocol level. `zephyr/config/` held up under its first real exercise. The per-concern split (`InferenceSettings`, `ObservabilitySettings`) was the right granularity: nothing crossed awkwardly. Pydantic's `validation_alias` solved the LANGFUSE_INIT_PROJECT_*_KEY → langfuse_*_key problem cleanly, so the config field names stay clean while consuming the env-var names Langfuse already requires. Adding `litellm_master_key` with no default forces a Pydantic validation error on missing `.env` config rather than silent fall-through — correct posture for a real secret. The `.env`-plus-`.env.derived` pattern (with the latter regenerated on every `make up`) keeps the LiteLLM OTLP basic-auth header from existing as a duplicate of the Langfuse keys; it lives derived, not stored. Token throughput on this laptop (Apple Silicon): ~19 tokens/sec eval on Qwen 2.5 7B at q4 quantization, with a one-time 4.5s model load that subsequent inferences avoid (kept warm by `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=5m`). Decent for local dev — usable for smoke testing and small interactive use, not viable for production-shaped eval cycles. Data point for the Phase 1 close audit on whether local inference is genuinely the right substrate or whether Phase 2 needs to budget hosted inference for the eval harness specifically. Session did not split into S6a/S6b — the natural break point after the direct Ollama smoke was unnecessary because the model pull (13 minutes on this connection) was the only slow step; once that finished the LiteLLM and smoke work landed in under an hour. Five code commits plus supply-chain plus charter-and-log close, total seven commits this session.

## Session 5 (Package P2)
- Produced: Step 0 disambiguation: the Token discipline line was case 2 (genuinely uncommitted at S4 close) and had already been resolved by commit `63f0ff4 docs(charter): commit token discipline line carried across S4 [post-S4]` between S4 and S5; working tree was clean before S5 work began, no duplicate to discard. Charter commit `a08eeac` landed all artefacts in one commit: Security posture principles section (compliance targets SOC 2 Type II + ISO 27001, encryption posture, secrets, audit, auth, isolation testing, supply chain, security events), optionality principle bullet under Architectural, deferred-decisions reference, D19 (config interface), D20 (TLS posture), D21 (envelope encryption), D22 (audit context), D23 (auth/policy), D24 (tenant isolation tests), D25 (supply chain hardening), D26 (security events), D27 (OTel portability), `charter/deferred-decisions.md` (orchestration multi-port architecture with feature-promotion process; data-plane ownership), `charter/current-package.md` rewritten as four sessions. Code: `platform/config/` (Pydantic Settings, `QuorumSettings` base + per-concern subclasses, `SecretManagerSource` no-op stub for prod profile, profile resolution as the only sanctioned os.getenv); `platform/security/` (envelope-encryption crypto with AAD binding, HS256 dev auth + AuthError, in-tenant + role-permits-action policy with security-event hook); `platform/observability/security_events.py` (5 categories, file-backed JSON-per-line logger); `contexts/audit/` (AuditEvent value object, pure hash-chain helpers, AuditPort, NoOpAuditAdapter); `tests/contract/tenant_isolation/` (two-principal harness, three example tests, README pattern doc); `shared_kernel/types.py` (TenantId, Jurisdiction NewType wrappers); `pyproject.toml` + `uv.lock` (pydantic-settings, cryptography, pyjwt, pytest, import-linter, pip-audit); `conftest.py` at repo root (evicts stdlib `platform` from sys.modules so our package wins); `.importlinter` (3 contracts kept); `tests/_enforcement/test_no_getenv.py` (AST walker for D19); supply chain — postgres/redis/caddy pinned to digests, `make scan` / `make sbom` / `make lint` / `make test` Make targets, `.trivyignore` + `docs/security/exceptions.md` (64 deduped CVEs documented across three categories), `docs/security/scheduled-checks.md` (monthly Langfuse digest-bump cadence, first run 2026-05-29); `docs/security/checklist.md` as per-session gate; `compose.yaml` tightened to bind only Caddy:443 on the host (postgres/redis port bindings dropped, `make psql` continues working via `docker compose exec`).
- Decisions: D19, D20, D21, D22, D23, D24, D25, D26, D27. Optionality principle and Security posture as charter additions. Deferred-decisions content for orchestration architecture and data-plane ownership.
- Tests: `uv run pytest` → 32 passed (7 config, 11 security, 4 observability, 6 audit, 3 tenant isolation, 1 no-getenv enforcement). `make lint` → 3 contracts kept, 0 broken. Deliberate violation cycle exercised: probe `import pydantic` planted in `contexts/audit/domain/_violation_probe.py` flipped Contract 1 to BROKEN; removing the probe returned the suite to clean. `make scan` → exits 0 with the documented `.trivyignore` list across 8 images; pip-audit reports no Python-side findings. `make sbom` → exits 0 with deferral message. `make up` → 8 containers, all healthy (caddy lacks an explicit healthcheck and shows `Up`; postgres + redis flipped healthy in 9s after the host-port-binding tightening). `curl https://localhost/health` → 200, `curl https://localhost/` → 503, `curl https://langfuse.localhost/api/public/health` → 200. `make psql` reaches postgres via `docker compose exec`; `\dx` shows pgvector 0.8.2.
- Reflection: The four-session P2 shape held — S5 closed in one block (no S5a/S5b split needed), with the charter commit + 8 code commits + 1 housekeeping commit (host port tightening) + 1 supply-chain follow-up (trivyignore + secret-scan toggle) totalling 11 commits. The natural split point the prompt offered between code item 5 and item 6 was unnecessary because the supply chain work was load-bearing for the per-session checklist gate — the checklist is the hinge that makes S5 cohesive rather than two half-sessions. Two execution-time deviations are worth flagging. First: the project's top-level package name `platform` collides with stdlib `platform.py`, which pytest imports for its banner before our test files load; the cached stdlib import shadowed the local package and broke every `from platform.config import ...`. Resolved with a one-time `sys.modules.pop('platform', None)` inside `conftest.py`. Charter naming preserved; cost is a 20-line conftest with explanation. Second: the brief's literal Contract 2 ("forbid os.getenv outside platform/config/ via import-linter") is not expressible — import-linter operates at module level, not attribute level, and banning `os` outright would block legitimate `os.urandom` in crypto.py. Implemented instead as `tests/_enforcement/test_no_getenv.py` (AST walk over `platform/`, `contexts/`, `shared_kernel/`); the pytest path is real enforcement and can sit in CI. Worth promoting the host-port-binding rule the same way next session: a small parser test against compose.yaml that asserts only Caddy binds host ports — checklists drift, AST tests do not. Sector-specific configuration question (reflection prompt 2): D22 surfaced audit retention as the obvious sector-specific axis (HIPAA wants 6 years; SOC 2 varies by control; finance regimes want 7+); the tenant registry remains the right home, with the audit context inheriting per-tenant retention from there. Classification policy (PII/PHI categorization) is a second axis that is also tenant-and-sector-shaped. KEK-in-`.env`-for-dev posture (reflection prompt 3): the production-swap path is now legible from `platform/config/security.py` (KEK validator, comment), `platform/config/base.py` (SecretManagerSource branch under Profile.PROD), and D19/D21; the dev-vs-prod distinction is unambiguous. Trivy work surfaced one finding worth recording: Trivy's secret scanner false-positive matches inside Langfuse's bundled Next.js JS (placeholder strings shaped like GCP service-account keys); `--scanners vuln` is the right flag for this stack since the brief commits to vulnerability scanning specifically. Orchestration deferred-decisions content (reflection prompt 6) feels complete enough for P5 activation; two ambiguities surfaced while writing — "stable semantics" in the feature-promotion criterion is intentionally permissive (it would benefit from one worked example when the first cross-orchestrator feature lands), and the six-month escape-hatch retention period is a rough number that should probably tie to release cycles when those exist. Both are clarifications for activation, not load-bearing now. Dual S4 prompt-quality lessons (reflection prompt 7): both absorbed — browser interactive verification is now an explicit acceptance criterion in the four-session P2 block ("Browser interactive verification required for any UI-bearing acceptance criteria"), and the subdomain-default pattern for UI services is encoded in the same place; S6 has no UI surface and S7 will exercise both rules.

## Session 7 (Package P2)
- Produced: 11 commits — `b36d780` charter housekeeping (current-package status, CLAUDE.md additions for browser interactive verification + configuration-discovery + enforcement layer naming, D30 uv workspace topology); `01764d5` uv workspace topology and member skeletons (root pyproject declares `[tool.uv.workspace]` with members `contexts/inference`, `contexts/observability`, `contexts/audit`, `apps/api`; each member has its own pyproject with scoped runtime deps and `package = false` so imports resolve through pythonpath unchanged); `b3beb12` `zephyr/events/` domain event bus skeleton (`DomainEvent`, `EventBus` protocol, `SynchronousEventBus`); `eb941ce` `contexts/inference/` hexagonal scaffold (`Completion` value object, `InferenceError` taxonomy, `InferencePort` protocol carrying tenant_id from inception, `request_completion` use case, `api.py` D17 facade); `6eb7ce2` `LiteLLMAdapter` implementing `InferencePort` (vendor-isolated, OTel span with GenAI semantic-convention attributes, exception mapping at the boundary); `a8a34f4` `contexts/observability/` read-path scaffold (`TraceQueryPort`, `TraceRecord`/`TraceSpan` value objects, no-op Langfuse adapter under `adapters/outbound/langfuse/`); `6bd72c8` `apps/api/` FastAPI app (application factory, AuthenticationMiddleware via `app.add_middleware`, `POST /inference/completions` router, OTel tracing wired with OTLP/HTTP export, FastAPI instrumentation, multi-stage Dockerfile with non-root zephyr user, zephyr-api as 11th Compose service, Caddy `/api/*` path routing); `bf04830` import-linter contracts (3 → 14: hexagonal layers per context, cross-context independence at domain/application/adapters layers, platform isolation, domain purity, shared-kernel policed, vendor-SDK confinement for litellm/langfuse/fastapi) plus AST enforcement (`tests/_enforcement/_discovery.py` reads workspace members from pyproject as the single source of truth for the AST walk roots; `test_no_vendor_in_domain.py` joins `test_no_getenv.py`); `2b3ec96` integration tests (`test_auth_coverage.py` enumerates routes from `app.routes` and asserts each non-public path returns 401, `test_trace_propagation.py` wires an in-memory OTel exporter and asserts the parent-child span tree with GenAI attributes; lazy `app = create_app()` via `__getattr__` so module imports don't boot the OTLP exporter); `2118c68` live-stack wiring fixes (LiteLLM SDK needs `openai/` prefix to treat the gateway as an OpenAI-compatible proxy, `HTTPXClientInstrumentor` propagates W3C traceparent through the LiteLLM SDK's internal httpx so the gateway-emitted span lands as a grandchild rather than a separate trace, Dockerfile pre-creates `/app/logs` with zephyr ownership for `logs/security.jsonl` writes); `8b46492` litellm 1.83.0 → 1.83.14 (GHSA-xqmj-j6mv-4862 HIGH; surface not exploitable but the package ships in the runtime venv so `make scan` requires the bump). - Tests: `uv run pytest` → 59 passed (32 baseline + 5 events bus + 3 inference use case + 8 LiteLLM adapter + 2 observability + 6 auth coverage + 3 trace propagation = 59; the 27 new tests this session). `make lint` → 14 contracts kept, 0 broken; AST enforcement pair (no-getenv, no-vendor-in-domain) → 2 passed. `make scan` → 0 across 11 images with the documented exceptions list (litellm bumped above the GHSA-xqmj-j6mv-4862 fix threshold; pip-audit reports no Python-side findings). `make up` → 11/11 services healthy (the existing 10 plus zephyr-api). End-to-end through the API: authenticated `POST https://localhost/api/inference/completions` returns a real Ollama completion (37 prompt + 2 completion = 39 tokens) with a 32-char hex `trace_id` in the response body; Langfuse trace `f9d8192538d5092c6c5eabc35059ea10` carries 14 observations as a single tree — `POST /inference/completions` (FastAPI request span, root) → `chat qwen2.5:7b` (LiteLLMAdapter span, GENERATION, full GenAI attrs: `gen_ai.system=litellm`, `gen_ai.request.model=qwen2.5:7b`, `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens=37`, `gen_ai.usage.output_tokens=2`, `gen_ai.response.finish_reasons=["stop"]`) → `POST` (httpx-instrumented call) → `Received Proxy Server Request` (gateway GENERATION, `gen_ai.system=ollama_chat`, full input/output/total token attrs). Browser interactive verification of the trace requested from the operator at session close (criterion 10) — `https://langfuse.localhost/project/zephyr-dev/traces/f9d8192538d5092c6c5eabc35059ea10`; the API-side query confirmed the structure and the operator confirms the rendering. Auth coverage: `test_auth_coverage.py` enumerates 2 protected routes (the live app), asserts both return 401 unauthenticated, asserts unmatched paths return 401 not 404 (proving middleware sits ahead of routing), and asserts a security event lands in `logs/security.jsonl` with `category=auth_failure` on a denied request. `make down` removes all 11 containers.
- Reflection: Pre-write reconciliation surfaced two real corrections: FastAPI's middleware-on-every-route guarantee depends on `app.add_middleware` rather than the `@app.middleware` decorator (the decorator runs after routing, so 404s would skip the middleware), and OTel's `opentelemetry-instrumentation-fastapi` plus `opentelemetry-instrumentation-httpx` are both required — the FastAPI instrumentation alone gives the request span but the LiteLLM SDK's outbound httpx call won't carry the W3C traceparent header without the httpx instrumentation. The httpx propagation finding only surfaced under live verification: the unit-tested adapter span tree was correct, the FastAPI-instrumented request span was correct, but the gateway started a separate trace until httpx instrumentation landed — the two-trace failure is invisible to the unit tests because they don't run a real HTTP hop. Strong argument for the live verification step staying in every session that touches trace propagation. uv workspace topology rose to architectural significance and got D30: keeping `zephyr/` at the workspace root rather than as a member avoids further pyproject proliferation for cross-cutting code, while contexts and apps as members give the dependency-manifest layer of boundary enforcement. The candidate alternatives — every cross-cutting concern as its own member, or no members at all and dependency-groups — are recorded in D30's alternatives field. Caddy routing path-vs-subdomain raised no issues during browser verification: `/api/*` with `handle_path` works for an API surface (no Next.js basePath constraint), so APIs-on-paths plus UIs-on-subdomains is the consistent pattern from S4. Auth middleware on every endpoint passed the architectural test cleanly: `test_unmatched_path_returns_401_not_404` is the proof that middleware sits ahead of routing, and the integration test discovers routes from the live app rather than a hardcoded list, so adding a route without auth coverage will fail the test mechanically. import-linter expanded from 3 contracts to 14: the layered hexagonal contracts initially put `ports` above `application` (so application calling a port was a violation), corrected to the right order (`adapters` > `application` > `ports` > `domain`); the cross-context `independence` contract type was the right primitive once the `forbidden`-with-wildcards approach proved syntactically awkward. The vendor-SDK confinement contracts split into two per vendor (`*-confined` for non-apps trees with `allow_indirect_imports = false`, `*-confined-apps` for apps with `allow_indirect_imports = true`) so apps can wire vendor adapters without the transitive chain firing the contract — the right granularity, found by iterating once. OTel-native discipline at the FastAPI layer worked cleanly once httpx instrumentation was added; D27's portability claim holds end-to-end, with the friction concentrated at the LiteLLM SDK's HTTP transport layer (already covered by `opentelemetry-instrumentation-httpx`, but not by default — explicit instrumentation required). Configuration-discovery refinement to CLAUDE.md proved itself in the same session it landed: the AST `_discovery.py` helper reads workspace members from `pyproject.toml` rather than hardcoding, so the rename lesson from S8 is durable; renaming `contexts/inference` to anything would update the AST walk roots automatically without further code change. Total session size: 11 commits, the largest in P2 by 4. The multi-commit decomposition was right — each commit corresponded to one architectural layer plus one configuration concern, and the live-verification fixes (model prefix, httpx propagation, Dockerfile permission, supply-chain bump) landed as their own commits rather than being squashed into a single "FastAPI app" commit, which keeps the audit trail honest about what fix landed when. The directory typo (`Vadkkan` vs `Zephyr`) is still in place; no S7 tooling hardcoded the compose project name (`COMPOSE := docker compose --env-file .env --env-file .env.derived` lets the project name auto-derive from the directory), so the typo did not block session work and remains operator housekeeping.

## Session 8 (Package P2)
- Produced: Five rename commits plus the charter commit. Charter (`a5978da`) restored D17, D18, D22 in `charter/decisions.md` and the S4/S5/S6 reflection text in `log/sessions.md` to their pre-rebrand wording via `git checkout HEAD --` (the working tree at session start carried the in-place edits as uncommitted changes; HEAD was already the pre-rebrand source of truth, so byte-for-byte restoration was a single command). D28 left as originally written. D29 appended after D28 (product rename Quorum → Zephyr, post-S6, charter narrative + operational defaults + one config class name; package namespace untouched per D28). Current-facing prose updated: `charter/principles.md` bounded-contexts bullet, `charter/deferred-decisions.md` (4 forward-looking references in orchestration tools, feature-promotion process, data-plane ownership), `README.md` title and opening, `docs/security/exceptions.md` Category B rationale. `current-package.md` left untouched (no Quorum references; the S5 `platform/config/` line is correctly historical per D28). Code: `pyproject.toml` distribution renamed (`da171e4`) with `uv lock` regenerated. `QuorumSettings` → `ZephyrSettings` and `QUORUM_PROFILE` → `ZEPHYR_PROFILE` across `zephyr/config/{base,inference,observability,profiles,security}.py` and `zephyr/config/__init__.py` re-export, with docstring updates and the test fixtures in `tests/unit/zephyr/config/test_loading.py` (7 sites) and `tests/unit/zephyr/security/test_crypto.py` (3 plaintext sites) (`9f44b8c`). Operational defaults: `.env.example` Postgres triple, Langfuse INIT org/project/user values, comment text (`9aac668`). Comments: `Makefile` help line, `Caddyfile` 503 + UI-services comment, `compose.yaml` Langfuse stack header, `ops/litellm/config.yaml` trace-path comment (`28fd113`). Residue commit (commit 6 in the brief) was a no-op outside `charter/decisions.md` and was omitted per the brief's "if nothing surfaces, omit" directive. Operational reset took an extra step: an orphaned `zephyr-*` Compose project from the pre-rename working tree at `/Users/sabu/Zephyr/` still held containers and volumes (current dir is `/Users/sabu/Vadkkan/`, so the new project name auto-derives to `vadkkan` — a directory typo, operator housekeeping per the brief's out-of-scope list); old caddy was holding port 443 and blocked the new caddy until torn down explicitly via `docker compose -p zephyr down -v`. Fresh `vadkkan_*` volumes created on the second `make up`; model re-pulled into the new ollama_data volume.
- Decisions: D29.
- Tests: `git log -S "Quorum" --oneline` returns the 6 pre-rebrand commits and 5 new S8 commits (each genuinely changes the count of "Quorum" — the charter commit re-adds the historical text plus D29, the code commits remove `QuorumSettings`/`QUORUM_PROFILE`/distribution name; criterion 1's "plus zero new ones" is mechanically impossible given the work but the spirit holds: no silent additions, every change is named in a commit message). Total commit count 45 (was 40 at S6 close, +5 this session). `uv run pytest` → 32 passed (after recreating `.venv` at the new directory path; the pre-existing venv had a hardcoded `/Users/sabu/Zephyr/.venv/bin/python` interpreter that broke when the directory moved). `make lint` → 3 contracts kept, 0 broken. `make scan` → exits 0 across 10 images. `make smoke-llm` → valid OpenAI-format completion (35 prompt + 8 completion = 43 tokens). Trace landed in Langfuse at `projectId: zephyr-dev` with `gen_ai.*` semantic-convention attributes intact and rendered via `GET /api/public/traces?projectId=zephyr-dev&limit=1`. `GET /api/public/projects` → `{"id":"zephyr-dev","name":"zephyr-dev","organization":{"id":"zephyr","name":"zephyr"}}` (single org/project, clean bootstrap). `psql -U zephyr -d zephyr -c "SELECT current_database(), current_user;"` → both `zephyr`. Stack health: 10/10 containers healthy on the warm path. Acceptance criterion 5 (the residue grep) returned 11 matches inside `charter/decisions.md` only — every match is in restored historical text (D17/D18/D22), preserved D28 (per D29's "stands as written"), or D29's own body recording the rename. The criterion as literally written is incompatible with the restoration directive (the `^./charter/decisions.md:.*\`quorum\`` filter only excludes the backtick-quoted alternative line, not the legitimate "Quorum" mentions in the rest of the entries); the principled reading is "zero matches outside `charter/decisions.md`, `log/sessions.md`, and `docs/archive/`", which holds.
- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (revert cleanliness) — clean. The IDE reinit and the directory move did not complicate git: `.git` survived intact, the working tree was where the in-place rebrand edits lived (uncommitted), and HEAD itself already contained the pre-rebrand text. `git checkout HEAD -- charter/decisions.md log/sessions.md` was the entire restoration. The lesson is that uncommitted in-place edits to historical content are recoverable as long as nobody commits them; the danger window is the gap between the in-place edit and the next commit, which is exactly when a future session should catch the shortcut. Reflection prompt 2 (trigger for future sessions) — the trigger is "is this edit changing wording in a section dated to a past session?" That question is mechanically answerable: charter/decisions.md D-entries carry a session number in their title, log/sessions.md entries are headed by session number. A pre-write diff that touches lines under a `D[0-9]+:` heading or `## Session [0-9]+` heading whose number is less than the current session number is a violation candidate by construction. The right reflex is to refuse the in-place edit and surface the question to the brief author; the wrong reflex is the "one-time exception" framing, which is exactly how append-only erodes (and is now D29's first rejected alternative for the audit trail). Worth promoting to a structural check: a small AST-or-parser test that walks decisions.md and sessions.md and refuses commits where past-session content changed without an explicit "restoration" commit-message marker. Filed as a candidate for S7 alongside the host-port-binding parser test from S5. Reflection prompt 3 (when reset stops being the cheaper path) — the answer flips when there is a customer-shaped consumer of the data the volumes hold. Pre-customer, trace data in Langfuse and DB rows in Postgres are operator-replaceable; reset is cheaper than scripting a rename because the reset path is one Make target and the rename path is per-store migration logic with verification. Once a tenant exists whose data is in those volumes, the calculus inverts immediately: reset becomes data loss, and the rename script becomes mandatory. The architectural commitment under D1 (database-per-tenant) means the rename surface is bounded — per-tenant DB rename is a contained operation rather than a schema-wide rewrite — so the script does not need to be heavy when it's needed, but it does need to exist before the first tenant lands. Phase 3 (tenant registry, per-tenant provisioning) is the natural place; logged for that package, not now. Reflection prompt 4 (customer-facing surface in D29) — D29 would need a transition section: when the rename touches a name customers see in URLs (subdomains, SaaS hostname, OAuth client_id, billing entity name) or in support contracts, the rename is no longer commercially neutral and the D-entry has to record (a) what the old name will continue to resolve for, (b) for how long, (c) the deprecation cadence and the customer notification window, (d) what stays Quorum-named in invoices and SOC 2 evidence packages until the next audit cycle, and (e) whose sign-off was required. Pre-customer this is all moot; the cheap clean break D29 records is a one-time affordance of being early. Worth flagging that this affordance closes silently — there is no "first customer" event that pings the team to switch from clean-break to managed-transition framing — so the next product-rename consideration (none expected) should be gated on an explicit customer-impact check in the brief. Operational note: the orphaned `zephyr-*` Compose project blocking port 443 on `make up` was the only execution-time surprise; the lesson is that Compose project names auto-derive from the working directory and survive directory renames as orphans, so any future directory move should add a `docker compose -p <old-name> down -v` step before the new `make up`. The directory typo (`Vadkkan` vs `Zephyr`) is operator housekeeping per the brief's out-of-scope list and was not corrected this session.

Then S9 proper: `864728a docs(p3/s9): open P3, record framing D-entries, fold directory housekeeping` (charter — `packages.md` P3 line expanded, `current-package.md` rewritten as the four-session P3 block with the D13 breach acknowledgement, D32 + D33 appended, `.gitignore` archive patterns at repo root only, `project.zip` deleted as a 107KB snapshot of `b0853055...` with no references). `.env.example` and `.env` mirror the new variables; Makefile help text reflects 14 services). `1501b4a feat(p3/s9): scaffold contexts/tenancy with bounded-context structure` (full bounded-context layout: `domain/`, `application/`, `ports/`, `adapters/inbound/`, `adapters/outbound/`, `api.py`; workspace member with `package = false` and zero third-party deps; import-linter contracts grew 14 → 15 with `layers-tenancy` plus tenancy added to the three cross-context independence contracts, the domain-purity source list, and the litellm/langfuse vendor-confinement source lists). `95ced6a feat(p3/s9): add tenancy domain model with Tenant, EncryptedCredentials, TenantConnectionConfig` (`TenantId` UUID-validated value object owned by tenancy — shared_kernel.TenantId stays as the str-shaped cross-context primitive; `EncryptedCredentials` with wrapped_dek/ciphertext/AAD bytes for D21 envelope encryption; `TenantConnectionConfig` plaintext value object with module-level docstring stating it must not persist beyond function-call scope; `Tenant` aggregate composing the value objects + Jurisdiction reused from shared_kernel/ + display_name + TenantStatus stdlib StrEnum + datetime created_at; 14 domain tests). `e6d589b feat(p3/s9): define TenantRegistryPort in contexts/tenancy/ports` (Protocol with `register_tenant(plaintext)`, `get_tenant`, `list_tenants(jurisdiction=None)`, `update_tenant_status`; - Decisions: D32 (per-tenant Postgres instance topology, two at P3 open), D33 (control-plane separation from tenant data planes).
- Tests: `uv run pytest` → 76 passed + 4 skipped (the 59 baseline at S7 close + 14 tenancy domain + 3 tenancy port tests = 76 runnable; the 4 skipped are S10 contract scaffolds for the registry adapter). `make lint` → 15 contracts kept, 0 broken (was 14; added `layers-tenancy` per the hexagonal contract pattern; `domain-purity`, `litellm-confined`, `langfuse-confined`, and the three cross-context `independence` contracts grew their source lists). `make scan` → exits 0 across 14 image references (the three new Postgres services share the existing pgvector/pgvector:pg17 digest, so the dynamic image-list discovery in `make scan` picks them up automatically and the supply-chain surface is unchanged from P2 close; pip-audit reports no Python-side findings). `make up` → 14/14 services healthy (the 11 from P2 close plus postgres-control-plane, postgres-tenant-a, postgres-tenant-b); compose project name auto-derives to `zephyr` not `vadkkan` after the orphan teardown. Memory footprint of the three new Postgres instances at idle: 35-42 MiB each (postgres-tenant-a 35.07 MiB, postgres-tenant-b 35.01 MiB, postgres-control-plane 41.7 MiB), comparable to the existing P1 postgres at 36.88 MiB; total stack memory cost of P3/S9 is ~115 MiB additional at idle. `grep -ri vadkkan` returns 2 matches outside `log/sessions.md`: one in `charter/current-package.md` (the S9 description's "Vadkkan-to-Zephyr directory housekeeping folded in" narrative line) and one in `docs/archive/packages/p2.md` (the P2 archive's drift-list entry naming the typo). Both are accurate historical-narrative references in working/archive documents; the AC's literal "outside log/sessions.md historical entries" filter parallels the S8 grep-residue interpretation, where the principled reading is "no matches in code, configs, tooling, or routing", which holds. AST enforcement: `tests/_enforcement/test_no_getenv.py` passes after the new settings classes land (the new `os.getenv` calls are inside `zephyr/config/`, exempt by design). Vendor-doc reconciliation findings: pgvector/pgvector:pg17 multi-arch index digest is still `sha256:494dff7e...` (no bump warranted, the digest matched the existing pin byte-for-byte at S9 verification time); Compose healthcheck syntax (`pg_isready -U $${POSTGRES_USER} -d $${POSTGRES_DB}` with interval/timeout/retries/start_period) and `depends_on: condition: service_healthy` semantics unchanged from the patterns S4-S7 already use; SQLAlchemy 2.0 async forward-look (S10 will use `registry.map_imperatively(Tenant, table)` rather than declarative decorators on the domain class so domain-purity holds, with `create_async_engine("postgresql+asyncpg://...")` plus `async_sessionmaker(expire_on_commit=False)` — the S9 domain shape (frozen dataclass aggregate + bytes-shaped EncryptedCredentials + StrEnum status + stdlib datetime created_at) maps cleanly to a SQLAlchemy Table without round-trip transformations).
- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (housekeeping items lingering past their session of origin) — the directory rename was carried as housekeeping for two sessions (S8 close to S9 open) and folded into S9's first commit. The discipline rule that should fire is asymmetric by housekeeping shape. For housekeeping that affects the runtime substrate (directory names, port bindings, project names, image digests, schema), the rule is: at the session where it lands, scope the cleanup as a separate small commit before substantive work, so a future bisect over substantive commits is clean. For housekeeping that is documentation-shaped (a README line, an env-example comment), the rule is the opposite: fold into the next substantive commit so the diff stays cohesive. S9 followed the first rule — the rename was acknowledged in the charter commit's body and the orphan teardown happened pre-commit-1 — but the lesson is the trigger that should have caught it earlier: housekeeping items in the carryover list of the between-packages current-package.md are themselves a signal that something is owed across the package boundary, and a session that opens with carryover items should land them in commit 1 of the new package rather than allow the carryover bullet to roll forward. Reflection prompt 2 (memory and startup-cost data on three new Postgres instances) — three pgvector/pgvector:pg17 instances at idle cost 35-42 MiB each, totalling ~115 MiB additional resident memory above the P2-close 11-service footprint. By contrast, S6 captured Qwen 2.5 7B at q4 quantization sat at ~5GB resident with KV cache and runtime overhead pushing toward the 12g `mem_limit`. The data point is that adding three Postgres instances is structurally cheap (~38 MiB per instance amortised, dominated by shared-buffer initialisation) and that local dev cost of the database-per-tenant topology choice (D32) is bounded by Postgres's per-instance overhead, which is an order of magnitude smaller than the inference overhead. The cost calibration argument for D32's separate-instances posture (versus single-instance logical separation) holds: at this overhead level, there is no local-cost reason to compromise the routing-layer fidelity. The watchpoint is whether per-instance cost stays linear as tenant count grows from 2 to 5 in dev experimentation; if shared-buffer overhead scales worse than expected, the calibration revisits. The throughput data from S6 (~19 tokens/sec eval) and the memory data from S9 (~38 MiB per Postgres instance) are both Phase 1 close audit inputs on whether Phase 2 should budget hosted infrastructure for either substrate. Reflection prompt 3 (the principle for "share or separate" questions) — D33's control-plane separation and D18's Redis sharing read in opposite directions but the principle that distinguishes them is operational lifecycle, not data category. The question to ask at any future "share or separate" point is whether the two consumers have the same operational lifecycle — same actor administering them, same maintenance window, same blast radius on failure, same audit comment shape. D18 shared Redis because Zephyr caching and Langfuse ingestion are operationally similar (both queueing, both operator-administered as part of the platform stack). D33 separates because control-plane data and tenant data planes are operationally distinct (control plane is jurisdiction-spanning and operator-owned; tenant data planes are jurisdiction-local and tenant-owned-operator-administered). The principle is one-line statable: "share when the operational lifecycle is the same; separate when it is not." That sharpens the question and keeps it from being relitigated each time. Worth promoting into `principles.md` at the next charter audit cycle, not unilaterally now — the principle has only the two data points so far and the audit cycle is the right place to confirm it generalises before it lands as a principle. Reflection prompt 4 (introducing leak-surface types ahead of leak-prevention controls) — the rule is that a plaintext type's *existence* is acceptable ahead of its *use*, but its *write path* is not. S9 ships TenantConnectionConfig with no controls because S9 has no write path (the registry port docstring states the contract; no adapter is yet wired). S10 lands the controls in the same commit as the registry adapter that first writes plaintext through the type, never as a follow-up. The trigger for the rule is structural: the moment a function signature appears that *takes* the plaintext type as input, the leak-prevention controls (logging filter, AST test forbidding plaintext-in-instance-state, tenant-isolation test attempting cross-tenant credential read) are non-optional in that same commit. The implementation discipline that follows is to never write a TODO or "controls land later" framing for leak-prevention; the controls are part of the integration commit, never a follow-up. Worth promoting to the security checklist as a per-session gate item for any session that wires an adapter for a context with a plaintext value object. Reflection prompt 5 (state-at-open mismatch between brief and working tree) — the S9 brief assumed the P2 retrospective work had committed and the working tree was clean from the "Between packages" baseline. The actual state was that retrospective work sat uncommitted (charter changes, p2.md untracked, ops/scheduled_checks.yaml untracked, sessions.md S5/S6 reorder against append-only, project.zip untracked) and Docker still hosted the orphan vadkkan-* project. The discipline rule is that the framing conversation should produce a "state at session open" verification step as part of the brief, not assume the previous session's deliverables landed cleanly. Concretely: the brief's "Context to read first" section should grow a sub-bullet ("Verify state at open: `git status` clean from previous session close, `docker compose ls -a` shows the project name auto-derives from the current directory, no orphan projects, no untracked files at repo root") and the framing conversation should produce a "Pre-S<N> housekeeping" section listing any commits the brief expects to have landed but has not. The housekeeping lands as separately-named retrospective commits (as S9 did with the three `docs(p2/retrospective): ...` commits) so the audit trail names what each commit accomplishes against which package's accounting. The framing conversation produces a clean baseline for the brief author; the brief itself depends on that baseline holding. Worth folding into the prompt-drafting practice for S10 onward.

Pre-write reconciliation findings recorded inline in D34 and in code: SQLAlchemy 2.0's `sqlalchemy.dialects.postgresql` is not lazily exposed via `sa.dialects.postgresql` — explicit `from sqlalchemy.dialects import postgresql as pg` required (caught at first contract test collection); Alembic's named-section pattern uses bare `[NAME]` not `[alembic:NAME]` (caught when `alembic --name control_plane current` reported "No 'script_location' key found"); `cryptography.exceptions.InvalidTag` is the canonical exception for AAD mismatch on AESGCM decrypt; asyncpg's prepared-statement cache returns bytea as `bytes` regardless of cache state, so caching is irrelevant to decryption correctness (the cache concern was a S10-prompt forecast that turned out a non-finding).
- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (reconciliation findings) — three real corrections beyond what D34 forecast. SQLAlchemy 2.0 forced `from sqlalchemy.dialects import postgresql as pg` because `sa.dialects.postgresql` is not a lazy attribute on the top-level module; the migration file and the adapter both use this form, fix landed during the first contract test collection run when the adapter import failed at the column declaration. Alembic's bracket form is `[NAME]` not `[alembic:NAME]`; D34 originally cited `[alembic:control_plane]` as a forecast, the actual cookbook pattern is the bare section name, and `current-package.md` was corrected in commit 1's diff (not in D34, which retains its forecast wording with the bracket-form correction noted in the body to preserve the append-only spirit while keeping operationally relevant docs accurate). The `EncryptedField` wire shape from `zephyr/security/crypto.py` has five fields (wrapped_dek, dek_wrap_nonce, ciphertext, nonce, key_version), not three — D34 forecast eight total columns; the actual table has eleven (5 crypto + 1 AAD + 5 identity/metadata). D34's column count was corrected inline; the architectural commitment ("persist wire shape, AAD bound, no plaintext column") is unchanged. The cryptography AAD semantics held: `InvalidTag` on mismatch verified by the AAD-mismatch isolation test (scenario 5 in `test_registry_isolation.py`). Reflection prompt 2 (the three-control structure) — the AST test in commit 6 caught the deliberate-violation probe cleanly on the first run; the walker did not need iteration. Annotation extraction handles bare names, dotted attributes, `Optional[T]` subscripts, and `T | None` BinOp unions; the probe was annotated as a bare `TenantConnectionConfig`, which the bare-name branch picked up at `_class_offenders` walking `__init__`'s `AnnAssign` nodes. Substantive paragraph on the trigger that should fire in future sessions: the moment a new plaintext value object enters `contexts/<X>/domain/` with the contract that it must not persist beyond function-call scope (the marker is a docstring like `TenantConnectionConfig`'s "THIS TYPE HOLDS PLAINTEXT AND MUST NOT PERSIST BEYOND FUNCTION-CALL SCOPE"), and an adapter under `contexts/<X>/adapters/` gains a write path that takes the plaintext as input, the AST test's `_FORBIDDEN_TYPE_NAMES` set must grow to include the new type name in the same commit. The trigger is structural and mechanically detectable: any `AnnAssign` in an adapter `__init__` or class body whose annotation references a domain value object whose docstring contains the "MUST NOT PERSIST" phrase is a candidate for the AST list. Worth promoting to a per-session security checklist item: "any new plaintext value object entering the codebase: add to AST `_FORBIDDEN_TYPE_NAMES` in the same commit as the first adapter write path, verified by deliberate-violation cycle." The pattern itself is reusable: the AST walker, `_discovery.py`-driven source roots, and the deliberate-violation cycle reproduction together form a template. When the next plaintext type lands (most likely candidates are PII values in P5+ or session credentials in identity work), the cost is one entry in the forbidden set plus one cycle. The S10 reflection's job is to make sure that pattern doesn't have to be re-derived. Reflection prompt 3 (audit destination convention) — D34 commits to `tenant_id=""` (empty-string sentinel, since `AuditEvent.tenant_id` is typed `str` not `str | None`) for control-plane scope. Wiring the audit emissions in commit 4 surfaced one ambiguity: `AuditEvent.tenant_id` is `str`, not `str | None` (the audit domain pre-dates D34's "tenant_id=None for control-plane" framing). The empty-string sentinel is the pragmatic compromise; D34 was updated inline to record this. No registry mutation surfaced ambiguity about whether it is control-plane or tenant-scoped: register, update_status, and (future) deprovision all sit on the operator-administered control plane per D33, and the only "tenant-scoped" thing the registry could do is something the registry doesn't do (the registry stores per-tenant connection config, but that's a control-plane row). Substantive paragraph on the minimum shape S12's audit adapter routing logic needs: S12's audit adapter receives `AuditEvent` instances and must route to one of two physical destinations — a control-plane audit table on the control-plane database, or a per-tenant audit table on the tenant's data-plane database. The minimum routing logic is one if-statement at adapter entry: `if event.tenant_id == "": write_to_control_plane_audit_table(event) else: write_to_tenant_audit_table_via_routing(event.tenant_id, event)`. The control-plane audit table has the same hash-chained schema as per-tenant tables (D22 schema unchanged); the chain is per-destination — control-plane events form one chain, each tenant forms its own chain. S12 does not need a separate `AuditOperatorPort`; one port with adapter-level routing is sufficient (D34 alternative explicitly rejected the doubled port). The `compute_event_hash` and `verify_chain` pure functions in `contexts/audit/domain/events.py` already accept `tenant_id` as a string parameter and produce the chain hash deterministically; the empty-string sentinel feeds into that pipeline cleanly without special-casing. The one S12 design decision that S10 cannot pre-commit is the chain initialisation for the control-plane: when the very first control-plane event lands, what is its `previous_event_hash`? The answer is the same as for any tenant: the genesis sentinel `"0" * 64`. S12 ships with that. The pre-commit saved here: S12 does not need to relitigate routing-port shape, table schema, or genesis convention; the only S12 decisions are the SQL schema for the two audit tables and the `previous_event_hash` lookup logic per destination. Reflection prompt 4 (scheduled-checks runner first-run output) — the first run produced a well-formed report at `docs/security/scheduled-check-reports/2026-04-30.md` listing six families across monthly/quarterly/annual cadences. The Compose-side digest pins were captured cleanly from `compose.yaml`. PyPI lookups all failed with `SSL: CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED` — Python 3.14 on this macOS doesn't trust the system store without a `certifi` bootstrap. The runner correctly recorded `lookup_failed: <error>` rather than crashing, so the report's architectural value (current pins captured, structure intact) is preserved. The data point feeding the Phase 1 close audit on cadence calibration: at S10 close, no automated PyPI comparison happened, so the cadence calibration question is deferred until the next run from a host with working PyPI access. The substantive point worth recording is that the local-first runner shape works: a partial-network-failure first run produces a usable report rather than a crash, and the `lookup_failed:` markers tell the operator exactly which families need re-querying. The certifi gap on this Mac is operator housekeeping (one-time `Install Certificates.command` or `pip install certifi` + bootstrap), not a runner-design gap; if the next scheduled run still surfaces SSL failures, the runner gains an explicit `--insecure-pypi` mode or a `certifi`-vendored fallback. Reflection prompt 5 (state-at-open verification protocol) — S10 opened cleanly against S9's close: `git status` was clean, `docker compose ls -a` showed the project name auto-derived to `zephyr` from the working directory with no orphan projects, `make up` had 14/14 services healthy, `uv run pytest` reported 76 passed plus 4 skipped (the registry port contract scaffolds), `make lint` reported 15 contracts kept. One S9-deferred housekeeping item surfaced during commit 1 work: `apps/api/Dockerfile` did not COPY `contexts/tenancy/pyproject.toml`, so `uv sync --frozen` against the rebuilt image failed without the fix; folded into commit 1 as a prerequisite for the alembic + psycopg vendoring rebuild rather than a separate housekeeping commit because it landed alongside another runtime-substrate change in the same commit. Substantive paragraph on gaps in the verification list at the top of this prompt that would have caught issues earlier: the verification list named `git status` clean, compose project name auto-derivation, `make up` 14/14, `uv run pytest` 76+4, and `make lint` 15. It did NOT name "rebuilding the api image with `--frozen` succeeds against the current workspace topology", which would have caught the missing tenancy `pyproject.toml` COPY before commit 1 work began. The verification list should grow a runtime-substrate sub-bullet: "image rebuild verification: `docker build -f apps/api/Dockerfile -t zephyr-api:dev .` succeeds, image digest pin in compose.yaml matches the rebuilt image, `make up` recreates the api container against the rebuilt digest cleanly". The pattern generalises: any session that is expected to touch image contents (new runtime deps, new files COPYed in, new env vars passed through) should run the rebuild verification at session open, not at first need. The other verification gap is environment connectivity: SSL cert failure on PyPI lookups would have surfaced earlier if the verification list had named "outbound HTTPS reachability check" — at S10 the runner is the first session feature requiring outbound HTTPS, so this isn't a regression, but is an inclusion candidate. The verification list grows as session-open failure modes surface; both items above are candidates for the S11 framing.

- Tests: `uv run pytest` → 117 passed (105 S10 baseline + 7 connection_resolution + 3 e2e + 2 host-port-bindings = +12, meeting the +12 target). `make lint` → 15 contracts kept, 0 broken (same as S10 close — no new import-linter contract; `domain-purity` already forbids SQLAlchemy in domain code, and `tenancy.application/` legitimately uses SQLAlchemy types as opaque port-shaped values per D36's choice). `make scan` not re-run because the supply chain surface is unchanged from S10 close (no new image pins; no new vendor SDKs entering the codebase). `make migrate` runs cleanly: phase 1 control-plane no-op (S10 head), phase 2 applies `0001_create_tenant_audit` to `postgres-tenant-a` and `postgres-tenant-b`; `\d tenant_audit` on each shows the 13-column schema and the two indexes. Re-running `make migrate` is a no-op on both phases (Alembic-native idempotency). Failure-mode reproducible: stop `postgres-tenant-b`, run `make migrate`, observe tenant A migrates (no-op since already at head), tenant B fails with `psycopg.OperationalError: failed to resolve host 'postgres-tenant-b'`, `make` exits 2; restart tenant B, re-run, the runner resumes and brings tenant B to head. End-to-end factory test passes against live stack (factory construction, cache hit reuse, status-transition invalidation, all verified). Parser test deliberate-violation cycle reproduced. `make up` → 14/14 services healthy. `make seed-tenants` → both runs verified (registers first time, no-ops second time). zephyr-api image rebuilt twice during the session: once for `COPY ops ./ops` (commit 4) and once for the alembic-env.py logging fix (commit 5); final digest pinned in `compose.yaml` is `sha256:b10650e871682d349fb3969f6bf9971ca8844dabad3372c78f9d31e0fac6c364`.
- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (reconciliation findings) — no surprises beyond S10's reconciliation. SQLAlchemy 2.0 `create_async_engine(url, pool_pre_ping=True)` with conservative per-tenant pool sizing (`pool_size=2, max_overflow=3`) keeps total dev connections at ≤2 tenants × 5 max = 10 peak, well within Postgres defaults; `async_sessionmaker(engine, expire_on_commit=False)` is the binding shape; `await engine.dispose()` is the cache-eviction lifecycle call (S10's adapter already used these). Alembic programmatic invocation is `Config(file, ini_section=name); cfg.set_main_option("sqlalchemy.url", per_tenant_url); command.upgrade(cfg, "head")`; the bracket-form `[NAME]` named-section pattern from S10 is compatible with both CLI `--name` and programmatic `ini_section=`. asyncpg pool behaviour under SQLAlchemy 2.0 is unsurprising: `pool_pre_ping=True` handles stale-connection handling under `make down`/`make up` cycles cleanly and is the right idiom for the cache. One operationally relevant correction landed during commit 5 build: `alembic.ini`'s `[logger_root] level = WARN` plus `fileConfig(config.config_file_name)` in each env.py was silencing the runner's own progress messages once any Alembic command ran; passing `disable_existing_loggers=False` to `fileConfig` plus pinning `log.setLevel(logging.INFO)` on the runner's named logger restored the per-tenant progress lines. The fix landed in the same commit as the orchestrator. No D-entry update needed; the reconciliation findings are recorded inline in D36's body. Reflection prompt 2 (cache invalidation scope) — D36 commits to invalidation on `update_tenant_status` only. In commit 1 wiring, no other registry mutation surfaced as a current invalidation trigger. Substantive paragraph on future mutation paths: when the registry grows additional state-changing operations, three of them must call `invalidate_tenant_session_factory(tenant_id)` from the application layer in the same use case that performs the registry update — credential rotation (when a tenant's per-tenant Postgres password is rotated, the cached engine's pool holds the old credentials and will fail on next use; the application use case `rotate_tenant_credentials` must invalidate after the registry update succeeds), jurisdiction change (does not strictly require invalidation because the connection details do not depend on jurisdiction, but should invalidate for symmetry — a future audit hook may bind jurisdiction into the engine logger context, and invalidation guarantees no stale jurisdiction is carried in cached engine state), and instance re-pointing (when an operator moves a tenant's data plane to a different Postgres instance, the connection config changes and the cached engine points at the abandoned instance — `repoint_tenant_instance` must invalidate). The pre-commit here: when any of those three use cases lands, the implementation must accept an optional `session_factory_cache` parameter (the same shape as `update_tenant_status` does today) and call `invalidate` after the registry write commits. The pattern is structural and one-line per use case; documenting it now means the inheriting session does not need to re-derive the invariant. The deeper invariant: any registry mutation that changes the bytes that flow through `reveal_connection_config` must invalidate the cache; mutations that only touch metadata (display_name, jurisdiction-when-not-yet-bound-to-engine) need not, but doing so for symmetry is cheap. Reflection prompt 3 (per-tenant migration failure-mode and operational shape) — D36 commits to per-tenant transactional with retry; cross-tenant atomicity is rejected. In commit 5, the failure-mode shape matched the framing exactly. Stopping `postgres-tenant-b` produced `psycopg.OperationalError: failed to resolve host 'postgres-tenant-b'` raised through SQLAlchemy's connection pool initialisation, which propagated up through `command.upgrade` and out of the runner with `make migrate` exiting code 2. Tenant A's already-applied migration was untouched (Alembic's `alembic_version` row on tenant A's database persisted the head revision); restarting tenant B and re-running `make migrate` resumed cleanly — the per-tenant phase iterated tenant A (no-op via `alembic_version`) then tenant B (applied fresh). Nothing about Alembic or asyncpg complicated the picture: SQLAlchemy raises a typed exception, the runner's `for tenant in tenants` loop has no try/except so the first failure stops the iteration, and Alembic's idempotency on already-applied revisions is the tenant-A retry contract. Substantive paragraph on the production-deployment shape: when production lands and `make migrate` is replaced by infrastructure-as-code, the equivalent two-phase orchestration looks like a CI job (or a Kubernetes Job manifest) that runs the same `python -m ops.migrate` against a production cluster, reading `ControlPlaneSettings` from the secret-manager backend rather than `.env`. The failure-mode handling changes in three concrete ways: (a) the runner gains a `--retry-failed-only` flag that reads each tenant's `alembic_version` table directly and skips tenants already at head, so a retry after a partial failure is bounded to the unmigrated tenants — at dev scale of 2 tenants the existing iterate-everyone idempotency is fine, but at 100+ tenants the no-op `alembic_version` queries become observable per-run cost. (b) the runner emits per-tenant migration outcomes as structured events rather than logs, so the production observability stack can alert on partial-failure patterns (e.g. "tenant X has been at version Y for ≥1h while tenants A-W are at Z" → page operator). (c) the per-tenant phase parallelises within a configurable concurrency limit because `100 tenants × 30s migration each` becomes 50 minutes of serial wall time; bounded parallelism matches the per-tenant-instance topology cleanly because each tenant's database is independent (D32) and the migrations don't share locks. The runner's failure semantics stay the same — a per-tenant transactional failure raises and stops the affected tenant, but other-tenant migrations in the same parallel batch may continue. The architectural commitment from D32/D36 (no cross-tenant atomicity) is what makes parallelism safe; if cross-tenant atomicity were required, parallelism would need to be replaced by a Saga, and the production runner would be substantially more complex. Reflection prompt 4 (parser test scope) — the host-port parser test caught no unexpected current bindings beyond the two allowlisted services; `compose.yaml` is clean. The deliberate-violation cycle worked on the first run: adding `ports: ["6379:6379"]` to redis flipped the test red with offender `{'redis': ('6379:6379',)}`; reverting returned to green. The YAML walker did not need iteration — `yaml.safe_load` plus a per-service `ports:` lookup with short-form/long-form normalisation handled both shapes; a unit test specifically verified the long-form path so a structural-evasion sneak (replacing `"6379:6379"` with `{host_ip: "127.0.0.1", published: 6379, target: 6379}`) is caught with the same comparator. The data point feeding the next "checklist to AST/parser test" promotion candidate: comment-enforced rules with a discrete enumerable surface (compose service definitions, env files, dependency manifests) are the strongest promotion candidates because the parser is small and the allowlist articulates the rule inline. Future candidates: `.env.example` allowlist (forbid keys that pattern-match `*_KEY`/`*_PASSWORD` outside a documented set), `pyproject.toml` workspace-member enumeration (forbid third-party deps in cross-cutting code), and `compose.yaml` `image:` allowlist (forbid floating tags without `@sha256:` digests — D25). Each is one parser test in `tests/_enforcement/`. Reflection prompt 5 (state-at-open verification protocol, growth) — S10's two additions (image rebuild verification, outbound HTTPS reachability check) had different signal strength at S11 open. Image rebuild succeeded against the current workspace topology, so the `apps/api/pyproject.toml` and tenancy member additions from S10 round-tripped cleanly; the rebuild check did NOT match the existing `compose.yaml` digest because BuildKit's manifest list digest is non-deterministic across rebuilds (timestamps in attestation manifests change). The check still served its purpose — a successful rebuild verifies all `COPY` directives and `uv sync --frozen` against the lockfile — but the digest-equality assertion in the prompt's verification step is wrong as stated; the meaningful assertion is "the rebuild succeeds and produces a runnable image", which the running container at S10's pinned digest already proves. The S11 prompt wording should drop the digest-match phrasing in future framing. The outbound HTTPS check failed at S11 open with `SSL: CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED` (the same Python 3.14 + macOS certifi gap S10 surfaced); per the S11 prompt's framing this is operator housekeeping outside session scope, and confirmed: scheduled-check still produces a usable report with `lookup_failed:` markers, and S11's substantive work touches no outbound HTTPS surface. Substantive paragraph on gaps in the verification list that S11 surfaced: the new gap S11 caught is image-rebuild-plus-recreate, not just rebuild. Commit 4's work needed `ops/seed_tenants.py` to exist inside the running container, which required (a) a Dockerfile change adding `COPY ops ./ops`, (b) rebuilding the image, (c) updating the digest pin in `compose.yaml`, and (d) recreating the running container against the new digest. The current verification list catches (a) and (b) (rebuild succeeds) but does not catch the gap between "image rebuilt" and "container running the new image". The pattern: any session that adds Dockerfile-level surface (new COPY directives, new env vars passed through compose.yaml, new runtime deps) needs to verify the *running container* matches the rebuilt image's contents, not just that the rebuild succeeds. Concretely, the verification step should grow: "if the session is expected to touch image contents, after the rebuild and digest update, run `docker compose up -d <service>` and `docker compose exec <service> python -c 'import <new_module>'` to confirm the new code is reachable inside the running container before proceeding to live verification." The other gap is the per-tenant env-passthrough: commit 4's first run failed because `POSTGRES_TENANT_A_*` were not passed through `compose.yaml`'s `environment:` block to the zephyr-api service even though they exist in `.env`. This is the third time this pattern has bitten across the package: S9 (`POSTGRES_CONTROL_PLANE_*` env passthrough), S10 (`contexts/tenancy/pyproject.toml` COPY), and now S11 (`POSTGRES_TENANT_A_*` env passthrough). The structural promotion candidate: a parser test (or AST test) that walks `compose.yaml`'s `environment:` blocks for the zephyr-api service and asserts that any env var read by `zephyr/config/` is present in the passthrough list. The detection: `zephyr/config/` Settings classes declare `env_prefix=` values and field names; the union of `<env_prefix><FIELD_NAME>` for every Settings class is the set of env vars zephyr-api could need; the passthrough must be a superset. Deferring to S12 or to a future "checklist promotion" session; the pattern is now named so the inheriting session can pick it up.

- Decisions: D38 (project reframe as learning sprint and rename zephyr → padhanam; the architectural commitments D1-D37 stand unchanged and are now correctly framed as the curriculum the operator is learning rather than commercial-platform requirements; package and project name converge again because D28's package-name-decoupled-from-product-name rationale (commercial-rebrand-protection) no longer applies to a learning sprint).
