`GET /api/public/projects` against Langfuse returned `{"id":"padhanam-dev","name":"padhanam-dev","organization":{"id":"padhanam","name":"padhanam"}}` (single org/project, clean bootstrap from the renamed seed env vars); `GET /api/public/traces?projectId=padhanam-dev&limit=1` returned a trace with `projectId: padhanam-dev`. `make scheduled-check` wrote a fresh `docs/security/scheduled-check-reports/2026-05-01.md` (the 2026-04-30 report from S10 stays as the precedent shape per append-only spirit). `grep -rn "zephyr" --include="*.py" .` returns zero matches across the codebase. `grep -rn "zephyr" --include="*.md" .` matches confined to `log/sessions.md`, `charter/decisions.md` (historical D-entries D17/D18/D22/D28/D29 plus D38's body recording the rename), `docs/archive/packages/p2.md`, and `docs/archive/packages/p3.md` — exactly the AC #8 allowlist; an early commit-1 draft of `current-package.md` mentioned the rename literally as `zephyr/ → padhanam/` and surfaced as the lone non-allowlist match during commit-7 verification, rephrased to "the package-namespace rename" without the literal old name and the AC held. Browser interactive verification: not performed this session — S13 added no UI surface, and Langfuse's existing trace tree is unchanged from S12's verified state. Operator post-session can open `https://langfuse.localhost/project/padhanam-dev/traces` to confirm the new project name renders cleanly in the UI; the API-side query already confirmed the structural commitment.
- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (reframe scope versus rename scope) — the reframe and the rename landed as cleanly-separable work in five of the six code commits, and entangled in exactly one place that confirms the "rename consequent to reframe" framing was correct. Commit 1 was reframe-only: rewriting the Bet, the README opening, and appending D38; no code touched. Commits 2 through 5 were rename-only: `git mv` plus mechanical find/replace plus image rebuild plus narrative-doc text swap; no charter narrative touched. Commit 6 was operator-instruction-only. The single entanglement was the `current-package.md` between-packages block, which had to mention both the reframe (a strategic event) and the rename (a mechanical event) because the operator reading the charter at any point between S13 close and P4 open needs to understand both. The first draft attempted to keep them entangled in one sentence ("the project is now padhanam, a personal learning sprint under enterprise-realistic constraints; see D38 for the reframe and the rename of the Python package namespace `zephyr/` → `padhanam/`") and that entanglement is what surfaced the lone AC #8 violation in commit 7 — the literal old-name reference in narrative-shaped (non-archival) text. The fix was to rephrase to "see D38 for the reframe and the package-namespace rename," which keeps both facts present without naming the old namespace literally. The lesson is that "rename consequent to reframe" is correct framing for the D-entry (one decision, one alternatives section, one record) but the *narrative downstream* of the D-entry has to be careful about where the old name is allowed to appear. Append-only D-entries can cite the old name freely (it's historical); current-facing narrative must not. Two separate D-entries would not have helped — it would have produced two D-entries with cross-references, doubled the audit surface for the same logical decision, and left the same narrative-downstream question unsolved. The single-entry framing is durable; the discipline that has to attach to it is "the old name lives in append-only locations only." Reflection prompt 2 (append-only discipline at second instance) — no tooling, automation, or instinct pushed toward in-place edits of historical content during S13. The bulk find/replace operations explicitly excluded `tests/_enforcement/` (handled in commit 3), and the Markdown-rename pass in commit 5 explicitly excluded `log/sessions.md`, `charter/decisions.md` historical sections, and `docs/archive/packages/`. The only check needed was the post-edit `grep -rln` filtered against the allowlist, which surfaced the lone `current-package.md` literal-old-name mention discussed in prompt 1 — an instance of newly-written narrative (not historical), so legitimately editable, and edited. The S8 reflection's promotion candidate ("a small AST-or-parser test that walks decisions.md and sessions.md and refuses commits where past-session content changed without an explicit 'restoration' commit-message marker") remains a candidate, not promoted in S13; the second instance executed cleanly without the structural test, suggesting the discipline holds at human-review scale through the second instance. Whether it holds at the third instance (extremely unlikely for a learning sprint, but the prompt's reflection-5 framing keeps the question open) is the threshold question — three instances is the structural-promotion convention from S11/S12, and a third rebrand would tip the cost calculus toward the parser test. The discipline that did surface as load-bearing was the asymmetric edit allowlist: the bulk `sed -i ''` operations in commits 2 and 4 used explicit `-not -path` exclusions for `tests/_enforcement/` (which had its own commit) and the Markdown pass in commit 5 used an explicit grep filter chain (`grep -v "log/sessions" | grep -v "charter/decisions.md" | grep -v "docs/archive"`) before applying any sed. The mechanical guarantee was that the find/replace tool never crossed into append-only files because the file list was hand-curated per commit, not driven by a `find . -type f` blanket. That discipline is what kept the reflection paragraph at "no instinct pushed toward in-place edits" rather than "tooling almost did it." Reflection prompt 3 (Compose and volume reset cost at second instance) — the S8 lesson generalised cleanly with one new wrinkle. The orphan teardown pattern from S8 (`docker compose -p <old-name> down -v` before the new `make up`) applied directly: `make down` followed by explicit `docker volume rm zephyr_*` cleared the ten zephyr-named volumes; the `zephyr_default` network removal needed a force-remove of a leftover `zephyr-zephyr-api-1` container first because the network wouldn't drop with active endpoints (S8 didn't surface this because the directory typo there meant the orphan project had a different name and Compose handled the teardown more cleanly). The qwen2.5:7b model re-pull cost (~5GB, ~3 minutes wall time) was the only material additional cost beyond the rename mechanics — same shape as S8's "model re-pulled into the new ollama_data volume" line. The reset-versus-rename-script calculus from S8 reflection 3 still favours reset at this stage: the volumes held only operator-replaceable data (registry rows seeded from `.env`, no customer data, traces operator-replaceable, model re-pullable). The calculus continues to invert when the first customer's data lands in a tenant data plane, at which point the per-tenant rename script (D1 keeps the rename surface bounded to per-tenant database renames) becomes mandatory; that switch belongs in a future session and is not S13 work. Reflection prompt 4 (the Bet rewrite as the load-bearing reframe) — looking at the rewritten Bet, the reframe reads as accurate to what the project actually is, with no overcorrection toward learning-only and one targeted concession to commercial residue. The accuracy: every architectural commitment (SOC 2 Type II, ISO 27001, database-per-tenant, hash-chained audit, supply-chain hardening, jurisdiction as first-class, OTel as portability boundary) is now framed as curriculum the operator wants fluency in, which matches the operator's actual stated goal and matches every D-entry's reasoning if read with "what enterprises require" rather than "what makes the platform sellable" framing. The "What success doesn't look like" section is the load-bearing paragraph that prevents the framing from drifting back: it explicitly names the absence of commercial intent, sales motion, customer pipeline, and exit, and it gives a one-line interpretation rule for the existing D-entries' reasoning ("what enterprise procurement requires from the systems they buy or build, which is what the operator wants fluency in"). The targeted concession is in that very paragraph: the rule reframes "what makes the platform sellable" rather than deleting it, which is the right call because deleting would force a rewrite of every D-entry's body for no architectural gain. The under-correction risk is in `charter/deferred-decisions.md` where line 33 still reads "Padhanam's commercial differentiator is the optimization layer that builds recommendations from trace history" — this phrasing now reads oddly given the reframe (a learning sprint doesn't have a commercial differentiator), and the prompt's explicit out-of-scope directive is what kept S13 from rewriting it. Worth flagging that the deferred-decisions file is a candidate for editorial reconciliation against the reframed Bet at a future strategic conversation, not now; the architecture stands, the framing on that single line drifted relative to the new Bet but doesn't change any architectural decision. Whether to revisit in P4 framing or at the Phase 1 close audit is the operator's call; logging it as data for that decision rather than acting unilaterally. The over-correction risk would have been to frame the project as "learning only with no architectural value beyond the operator's skill," which the rewrite avoids — the platform is the artifact that proves the fluency, and the artifact's architectural quality is what makes the proof convincing; the rewrite preserves that by naming the platform as the artifact and the operator's fluency as the deliverable, which are not the same thing and shouldn't be conflated. Reflection prompt 5 (convention question for future rebrands) — two rebrands (S8 Quorum→Zephyr, S13 Zephyr→Padhanam) both executed cleanly with the same five-shape playbook (charter commit, package-rename commit, enforcement-rename commit, operational-defaults commit, narrative-doc commit) plus the housekeeping commit and the verification commit. S13's seven-commit shape adjusts the S8 five-commit shape only by adding the AST-enforcement-discovery commit (which S8 didn't have because the S7 workspace-manifest discovery helper landed after S8) and the explicit working-directory housekeeping commit. The playbook is stable enough to execute without further reflection at the second instance; the framework for a hypothetical third would be the same seven commits with the same gates (pytest + lint after each, image rebuild + scan during operational-defaults, full smoke + scheduled-check + grep-allowlist at verification). The session-execution gap that surfaced at S13 and is worth promoting to a future-rebrand checklist: the AC #8 allowlist check (grep for old name across narrative docs, exclude the append-only allowlist) needs to run once during the verification commit, and any non-allowlist match is an indicator that newly-written narrative leaked the old name — the fix is rephrasing, never adding to the allowlist. That check is one grep + one filter chain and is the cheapest way to catch the entanglement-failure surfaced in reflection prompt 1. Adding it to the prompt's "verification list" for any future rename session would close the gap before the verification commit. Operational note carried from S8: directory rename is operator-only because Claude Code can't `mv` its own cwd; the post-session housekeeping documented in commit 6 follows the S8 precedent verbatim, and the venv interpreter path recreate (`rm -rf .venv && uv sync`) is the lesson that survives across the two instances.
- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (reconciliation findings) — two pre-write reconciliations produced real corrections beyond memory. OTel semantic-conventions group has NOT stabilised multi-tenant attribute naming as of 2026-04-30; `tenant.id` is de-facto practice in early-2026 guidance for both resource and span attributes but is not in the stable semconv spec; D37 records the choice to use `tenant.id` and `tenant.jurisdiction` as Zephyr-domain attribute names per D27's OTel-as-portability-boundary commitment, with the migration cost (a span-attribute rename and a one-line update at the emission site) made explicit if the OTel group converges on a different namespace. SQLAlchemy 2.0 row-locking idiom is `select(...).with_for_update().limit(1)` inside `async with session.begin()` at the default READ COMMITTED isolation; the framing's SELECT

## P3→P4 boundary strategic session
roles: analyst, PM, architect, technical writer
mode: strategic

- Produced: One charter commit landing D41 through D48 plus supporting artefacts. A methodology-comparison synthesis document (Kano-categorised absorptions, reverse-Kano list, bet-preservation framing as the absorption-decision criterion). An early descriptive methodology draft surfaced late in the integration conversation, which forced a real revision rather than the lighter editorial pass the first two sources alone would have allowed; the gap was that this third source had operationally specific content (DORA proxy definitions, session log tagging YAML, "metrics measure methodology not operator" framing, Patterns/Failure-modes observation surfaces, reading-as-tool, architect-direction-without-identity) that neither of the first two sources fully captured.

  The integration shape committed to: prescriptive structural disciplines (committed via D-entries, mechanically enforced) sit alongside descriptive observation surfaces (Patterns observed and Failure modes observed, opened as placeholders that grow as Phase 1 progresses). The framing tension between declarative-doctrine and provisional-observation was resolved by acknowledging both at the right scope: structural commitments are doctrine because they are committed; emergent patterns are provisional because the observations are still accumulating. Honest reporting against measurement evidence is committed as discipline, including revising the bet document and methodology document if at any phase audit the trend stops supporting the proposition.

  The D43 reading on epic-note-versus-archive reconciliation was caught during drafting. The first p4-epic.md draft put the as-built section as a placeholder inside the epic note itself (parallel to the `phase-1-prd.md` shape from D43's other clause). The convention now: epic notes carry no as-built section; archives reconcile against the epic note as part of their retrospective shape per D31.

Neither blocks P4. The methodology document references both as living artefacts refreshed at every phase audit, which is the formal commitment; the v1 voice work is the unfinished portion.

Cost-ceiling configuration columns land at S14 alongside the cost-attribution column as forward-affordance, Kano must-have on the same logic D41 used for the cost-attribution column itself (avoidable retrofit is a learning failure given the case study's audit posture). S15 includes the tenant context value-object refactor, Kano performance: the option preserved is the smaller refactor cost when later sessions need jurisdiction-aware routing, classification gating, or cost ceiling enforcement. The S14 prompt was drafted in the strategic conversation and is ready to fire as the next build-mode session, with state-at-open verification including a check that this strategic-mode commit has landed.

Unknown-model lookups raise `UnknownModelError` rather than returning zero. Pricing-status carries `table_hit` for known models and `unknown_model` for drift (model routed through LiteLLM but missing from `PRICING_TABLE`); on unknown-model, all three USD attributes emit as `0.0` rather than breaking inference, with the monthly review reconciling drift. The cost computation does not use `litellm.completion_cost`: the vendor helper reads from LiteLLM's bundled `model_prices_and_context_window` dictionary, which couples Padhanam's cost numbers to LiteLLM's release cadence and bundled pricing data, and the dev/Ollama zero-cost case is not clean against the bundled pricing. Three new adapter tests use a `monkeypatch` fixture that swaps the module-level `_tracer` for an SDK tracer backed by `InMemorySpanExporter`, then assert attributes on the captured span: zero attributes for the dev model with `pricing_status=table_hit`, full USD math for the commercial model (1M+500K tokens → 0.150+0.300=0.450 USD), and zeros + `pricing_status=unknown_model` for the drift path. `c1cdb96 feat(p4/s14): control-plane migration adding cost-attribution and cost-ceiling columns` added Alembic revision `0003_add_cost_columns` on the control-plane track. `cost_attribution_id` lands as text NOT NULL with the migration body backfilling existing rows from `tenant_id::text`; `cost_ceiling_usd_monthly` (numeric, nullable) and `cost_ceiling_action` (text, nullable, CHECK on the action enum `block`, `throttle`, `notify`, `audit_only`) land as forward-affordance not read by any code path at S14. `charter/schema.md` updated in the same commit per the schema-discipline rule. This entry (`docs(p4/s14): session log entry`) closes S14 with the structured tagging block per the methodology and updates `charter/current-package.md` to mark S14 closed with the trace ID and the artefacts that landed.

- Decisions: D49 (cost-capture wiring shape: pricing table at `padhanam/config/inference.py` as format-(b) Pydantic model + module-level dict, Padhanam-domain `gen_ai.cost.*` OTel attribute namespace per D27 with forward-compat to OTel's eventual stabilisation, own `cost_for()` cost computation rather than `litellm.completion_cost`, unknown-model handling via zeros + `pricing_status=unknown_model` flag rather than user-request failure, cost-ceiling columns as forward-affordance with CHECK pinning the action enum at the schema layer; Kano: must-have).

- Tests: `uv run pytest` → 143 passed (129 pre-S14 baseline + 8 pricing-table tests + 3 LiteLLMAdapter cost-attribute tests + 3 tenant-isolation tests for the new columns = +14, comfortably covering the AC #2 requirement of pricing lookup, missing-model error, adapter cost computation, adapter cost-attribute emission, and per-tenant column isolation), 4 skipped (unchanged baseline; the four `tests/integration/test_p3_full_slice.py` tests still skip when registry isolation tests have cleared the seeded set). `make lint` → 15 contracts kept, 0 broken (unchanged from S12 close). `make scan` clean against documented exceptions after the four new findings landed under category A. `make migrate` runs cleanly: control-plane phase applies `0003_add_cost_columns`; per-tenant phase unchanged at `0002_audit_sentinel_check`. `\d tenant_registry` against `postgres-control-plane` shows `cost_attribution_id` (text not null), `cost_ceiling_usd_monthly` (numeric), `cost_ceiling_action` (text) plus the new CHECK constraint `tenant_registry_cost_ceiling_action_check`. `\dt` against `postgres-tenant-a` and `postgres-tenant-b` shows only `alembic_version` and `tenant_audit` — no `tenant_registry`, instance independence per D32 holds. End-to-end inference call: authenticated `POST https://localhost/api/inference/completions` with a tenant-A JWT returned HTTP 200 with `text="Hello, nice to meet you."`, model `qwen2.5:7b`, 35 input + 8 output tokens, trace_id `9677d5252e095b8d8a2158e11d17a4c6`. Langfuse API query against `/api/public/traces/9677d5252e095b8d8a2158e11d17a4c6` returned the trace with the `chat qwen2.5:7b` LiteLLMAdapter span carrying all four cost attributes (`gen_ai.cost.input_usd=0`, `gen_ai.cost.output_usd=0`, `gen_ai.cost.total_usd=0`, `gen_ai.cost.pricing_status=table_hit`) alongside the existing `gen_ai.usage.*` attributes; the LiteLLM gateway's own `Received Proxy Server Request` GENERATION span carries the matching `gen_ai.usage.*` token counts but not the cost attributes (gateway-side cost emission is deferred — the operator-side authoring of cost happens at the adapter span which is what P11 will read). Browser interactive verification per the S4 convention: operator opened `https://langfuse.localhost/project/padhanam-dev/traces/9677d5252e095b8d8a2158e11d17a4c6` and confirmed the four cost attributes render correctly on the LiteLLMAdapter span in the trace tree. The image was rebuilt twice; final digest pinned at `sha256:5a4e144082212c5efea1ea1548f07d03b86db7b181bb582b868d9c4f450f8f0b` in `compose.yaml`.

- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (pre-write reconciliation findings) — Both reconciliations produced material judgement that drove the D49 alternatives section. LiteLLM 1.83.x exposes `litellm.completion_cost` and `litellm.cost_calculator.completion_cost`, both of which read from LiteLLM's bundled `model_prices_and_context_window.json` dictionary; neither accepts a custom pricing dict cleanly (the `custom_cost_per_token` per-call parameter exists but is awkward for multi-model routing), and `qwen2.5:7b` is not present in the bundled pricing data because Ollama-hosted local models are not vendor-priced. The choice to implement own cost computation against `PRICING_TABLE` rather than the vendor helper was driven by two concerns surfaced in reconciliation: cost-data drift between the platform's monthly review surface and LiteLLM's release cadence, and the dev/Ollama zero-cost case which the bundled pricing dictionary could not serve cleanly. The OTel GenAI semantic-conventions reconciliation surfaced the same not-stabilised condition that D37 caught for `tenant.id` and `tenant.jurisdiction`: the working-draft namespaces include `gen_ai.usage.cost.*` candidates but no stable convention as of 2026-05-05. Adopting a working draft now would force a rename when convergence happens; D27's OTel-as-portability-boundary commitment makes the rename cheap (a span-attribute set call site plus consumers), so deferring the namespace decision and emitting Padhanam-domain `gen_ai.cost.*` attributes in the meantime is the same forward-compat pattern D37 set. Both reconciliations were not mechanical — they shaped the D-entry's alternatives directly, and the eventual decision (own implementation, Padhanam-domain namespace) is defensible against external scrutiny because the alternatives section names what was rejected and why.

  Reflection prompt 2 (pricing-table format choice) — Format (b) was chosen: a separate `ModelPricing` Pydantic model and a module-level `PRICING_TABLE` dict alongside `InferenceSettings`. Format (a) — pricing as a typed field on `InferenceSettings` — was rejected because pricing is pure code data, not environment-driven, and putting it on Settings would force callers to construct a Settings instance (with the required `litellm_master_key` secret) just to read pricing; tests would have to provide a master key for every cost-related fixture. Format (c) — YAML/TOML under `ops/` — was deferred until pricing complexity (multi-region rates, time-zoned rates, large rate cards) justifies the extra load surface; at the current single-region, two-row scale, file-IO is wasted complexity. The decision surfaced one concern worth flagging for Phase 2 framing: production pricing is rarely a single dict per model. Vendors publish per-region rates, time-zoned rates (off-peak discounts), tier rates (volume discounts), and reserved-capacity rates that differ from on-demand. The `PRICING_TABLE` shape committed at S14 is honest for the current scale but will need a structural change at the moment Phase 2 introduces multi-region or any rate-card complexity beyond a single price-per-model. Capturing as a forward note for Phase 2 framing rather than over-engineering at S14 — the YAML/TOML escape hatch (format c) is the cleanest landing once that complexity arrives, and the structural-promotion threshold convention is the right gate for when to pull it in.

  Reflection prompt 3 (cost-ceiling forward-affordance columns) — The migration's forward-affordance comments held against AST tests and tenant-isolation tests, but with one concern worth surfacing. The columns are guarded at the schema layer by NULL defaults (no value lands without an explicit write) and CHECK constraints (the action enum cannot accept unknown values), but no AST or import-linter rule prevents future code from accidentally reading or writing them. The S14 registry adapter's `Table` definition includes the cost-ceiling columns purely so SQLAlchemy reads them on `SELECT *`, but no use case touches them; a future session could add a consumer without realising that Phase 2 enforcement architecture has not landed. The structural-promotion threshold (a comment-level rule that bites three times across a package gets promoted to a parser- or AST-level test, per S11/S12) would apply if accidental consumption surfaces twice more — at that point the candidate is an AST test that walks `contexts/` and `apps/` for any reference to `cost_ceiling_usd_monthly` or `cost_ceiling_action` outside the registry adapter's `Table` definition and the migration file, and fails CI when one is found. Naming the candidate now so the structural promotion has an obvious shape if it bites; not promoting at S14 because the rule has not bitten yet, and structural enforcement that anticipates rather than responds to drift produces the wrong incentive structure (rules added before they bite are paper rules).

  Reflection prompt 4 (D-entry alternatives quality) — The six alternatives in D49 represent real tradeoffs the operator weighed during reconciliation, not post-hoc rationalisation. Vendor cost helper rejection: the LiteLLM helper is the obvious default and would have shipped with less code; the rejection turns on operator judgement about coupling cost data to vendor release cadence, which is a Padhanam-specific concern about audit posture rather than a generic engineering tradeoff. Pricing on `InferenceSettings` rejection: option (a) is the immediately-natural place to put pricing alongside the other inference config; the rejection turns on the secret-coupling concern that surfaced during test design (a fixture for reading pricing should not need to provide a master key). YAML/TOML under `ops/` deferral: option (c) is the most production-shaped option; the deferral turns on a forward judgement about when the YAML's load surface earns its place, which the structural-promotion threshold convention makes honest. OTel working-draft adoption rejection: the convergence concern is real (rename cost is small per D27) and the deferral is honest. Hard-fail on unknown-model rejection: this is the most operator-judgement-shaped alternative; the rejection turns on the audit posture's preference for honest reporting over hard failure (the pricing-status flag carries the drift signal without breaking the user request, which is the same shape as security-event emission for soft-fail conditions across the codebase). Postgres `CREATE TYPE` enum rejection: this is the most engineering-shaped alternative; the rejection turns on migration ergonomics (`ALTER TYPE ADD VALUE` has subtle transaction restrictions in Postgres versions before 12, and even in 17 the migration shape is more brittle than `text` + `CHECK`). Honest read: alternatives 1 and 5 are the two that exercised the most operator judgement (the others felt closer to mechanical reasoning once the constraints were named). The alternatives section is doing the audit work the methodology commits it to; the metrics-measure-methodology framing applies — these are alternatives a senior product leader would have surfaced under enterprise-procurement scrutiny, not synthetic options added to make the D-entry look thorough.

- methodology: The migration commit's scope expanded mid-build to include the registry adapter update needed to satisfy the new NOT NULL column. The prompt's commit-shape granularity treated the migration as a self-contained `feat(p4/s14)` commit, but the migration is a broken state on its own — `make seed-tenants` and any future tenant registration would fail without the consequent adapter change. Folding the adapter update into the same commit kept the repo in a working state across every commit boundary; surfacing for the pattern: schema-tightening migrations (NOT NULL adds, CHECK additions, FK enforcements) should explicitly list adapter consumers in the prompt's substantive-work shape, or the commit-shape granularity should account for the schema-and-consumer pair as one commit.

The adapter span swapped the legacy `padhanam.tenant_id` attribute (S7 wording) for three Padhanam-domain attributes — `tenant.id`, `tenant.jurisdiction`, `tenant.cost_attribution_id` — extending the namespace D37 established. The `gen_ai.cost.*` attributes from S14 keep their position alongside; the trace tree now carries seven Padhanam-domain attributes on the LiteLLMAdapter span (three tenant.* + four gen_ai.cost.*) plus the OTel-standard `gen_ai.usage.*` attributes the adapter has carried since S7. One new test (`test_tenant_attributes_emitted_on_span`) asserts the three new attributes land and the legacy one is gone. `36a1026 test(p4/s15): end-to-end integration across tenant A and tenant B` landed `tests/integration/test_s15_tenant_context_e2e.py` with three tests, environment-gated. The two parametrised tests drive a real Ollama completion through `POST /api/inference/completions` for each tenant, query Langfuse via its public API for the trace, and assert the LiteLLMAdapter span carries the three tenant.* attributes (with tenant-specific values) for that tenant alongside the four gen_ai.cost.* attributes from S14, with the legacy padhanam.tenant_id absent. The third test asserts the inference path produces no audit rows on either data plane (D35 invariant against the enriched payload), guarding against an accidental cross-tenant write being introduced when audit emission is later added to the inference path. Skip cleanly when stack is not reachable or seeded tenants are absent, matching test_p3_full_slice from S12. `0591dc8 fix(p4/s15): poll Langfuse for chat-span presence rather than trace shell` corrected a polling-loop bug in the e2e test — the trace shell becomes queryable before all child observations ingest through the Redis → worker → ClickHouse path, so the loop now waits for the chat span specifically rather than just the trace endpoint returning 200. `81d5efb docs(p4/s15): record TenantContext shape and propagation as D50` appended D50 to `charter/decisions.md` recording shape (frozen dataclass with `__post_init__` validation per D16 framework-free policy), location (`shared_kernel/` per shared-kernel referential-equality criterion), propagation pattern (FastAPI Depends at router boundary then explicit parameter through use case → port → adapter), and OTel attribute namespace inheritance from D27/D37/D49. Six rejected alternatives weighed: Pydantic shape (forbidden by `shared-kernel-policed` contract), context-specific location (would force cross-context imports through api facade), contextvars and pure-Depends propagation (invert explicit-passing pattern from S7), classification field added now (no schema column, no consumer), keeping cost_attribution_id internal (loses trace-level audit surface), new resolve_tenant_context method (duplicates registry-read surface). Reasoning grounded in D9, D16, D27, D32-D35, D37, D41, D49. Kano: performance. `1b78079 docs(p4/s15): P4 close — archive at docs/archive/packages/p4.md` landed the P4 retrospective following the P3 archive shape per D31 with seven sections (goal, sessions, architectural commitments shipped, stack at close, data surfaced, drift surfaced, durable lessons, what the package leaves to P5+, convention changes) and a delta-against-epic-note section per D43 reconciling against `charter/packages/p4-epic.md` v1 with four named divergences: classification deferred per option C from framing with the schema-verification surfacing as cause; four `gen_ai.cost.*` attributes versus the three named in v1 (the `pricing_status` drift flag landed during build); two D-entries shipped matching the forecast; contract count held at 15 with no new structural enforcement. `93fc7c5 docs(p4/s15): replace log/packages.md placeholder with P4 entry, first measured-outcomes instance per D40` replaced the file's placeholder format with the methodology-aligned shape: header (closed date, sessions, archive link), seven industry-overlay metrics paragraph (deployment frequency proxy, lead time, change failure rate, MTTR, reliability, developer experience, contribution effectiveness), three bet-native metric layers (discipline-adherence, architectural-durability, bet-direction integrity), and a "what the numbers do not say" coda framing the small-sample noise honestly. `6b1779c docs(p4/s15): transition current-package to between-packages state` updated `charter/current-package.md` to point at the P4 archive and the first log/packages.md entry, with carryovers active across P4→P5 (classification deferred to P7/P8, cost-ceiling forward-affordance columns awaiting Phase 2, pricing-table monthly review cadence, pricing-table format evolution forward-note, PRFAQ operator-voice rewrite, Phase 1 PRD operator-review) and items that closed inside P4 dropped off. This entry (`docs(p4/s15): session log entry`) closes S15 with the structured tagging block per the methodology.

- Decisions: D50 (TenantContext value object — three immutable fields tenant_id/jurisdiction/cost_attribution_id; frozen dataclass in `shared_kernel/` per D16's framework-free policy and `Tenant` aggregate's `frozen=True` precedent; FastAPI Depends at the router boundary for the registry-read step, explicit parameter through use case → port → adapter chain thereafter; `tenant.cost_attribution_id` joins D37's `tenant.*` Padhanam-domain namespace alongside `tenant.id` and `tenant.jurisdiction`, with the same forward-compat property D27/D37/D49 established for emerging-standards spaces; classification field deferred to whichever package consumes it per S15 framing decision option C; Kano: performance).

- Tests: `uv run pytest` → 155 passed, 7 skipped (143 pre-S15 baseline + 6 TenantContext + 1 tenant-attributes-on-span + 5 resolver = 12 new passing tests; 3 new env-gated e2e tests added in `test_s15_tenant_context_e2e.py` skip when registry isolation tests have wiped seeded tenants in the same suite run, joining the 4 pre-existing `test_p3_full_slice` skips with the same reason; standalone runs pass cleanly). `make lint` → 15 contracts kept, 0 broken (unchanged from S14 close). `make scan` clean against documented exceptions. `make migrate` runs cleanly: control-plane unchanged at `0003_add_cost_columns` from S14; per-tenant phase unchanged. End-to-end tenant A: authenticated `POST https://localhost/api/inference/completions` with a tenant-A JWT (UUID `00000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000a001`) returned HTTP 200, model `qwen2.5:7b`, 35 input + 4 output tokens, trace_id `045f5e2040c11b026451ddccc3635dab`. Langfuse public-API query against `/api/public/traces/045f5e2040c11b026451ddccc3635dab` returned the trace with the `chat qwen2.5:7b` LiteLLMAdapter span carrying `tenant.id=00000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000a001`, `tenant.jurisdiction=eu-west`, `tenant.cost_attribution_id=00000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000a001`, and the four cost attributes (`gen_ai.cost.input_usd=0`, `output_usd=0`, `total_usd=0`, `pricing_status=table_hit`). End-to-end tenant B: authenticated `POST` with a tenant-B JWT (UUID `00000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000b002`) returned HTTP 200 with trace_id `e701d88a655321f61281641bcb180572`. Langfuse trace shows `tenant.id=00000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000b002`, `tenant.jurisdiction=us-east`, `tenant.cost_attribution_id=00000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000b002`, with the four cost attributes co-existing correctly and the legacy `padhanam.tenant_id` absent. Browser interactive verification per the S4 convention: operator opened both trace URLs in Langfuse (`https://langfuse.localhost/project/padhanam-dev/traces/045f5e2040c11b026451ddccc3635dab` and the tenant-B equivalent) and confirmed the three tenant attributes plus the four cost attributes render correctly on the LiteLLMAdapter span in the trace tree for both tenants. Per-tenant audit isolation verified via SQL: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tenant_audit` against `postgres-tenant-a` and `postgres-tenant-b` returns 0 in both (the inference path emits no audit events; D35's per-destination chain commitment holds vacuously against the enriched payload, and the integration test asserts no cross-tenant write occurs as a guard against future audit emission). - Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (TenantContext shape and propagation) — Shape choice was structurally pre-empted by the `shared-kernel-policed` import-linter contract that forbids Pydantic in `shared_kernel/` per D16; the dataclass-with-`__post_init__` shape was the only option compliant with the architecture, and the `Tenant` aggregate's `frozen=True` precedent made it consistent with the rest of the domain. The pre-write reconciliation on shape was not mechanical even given the constraint — the operator decision worth flagging is that I considered locating the value object in `contexts/tenancy/domain/` instead and rejected it on the referential-equality criterion (TenantContext is consumed by the inference adapter at minimum and will be consumed by audit, observability, and orchestration paths in later packages; locating in tenancy/ would force cross-context imports through the api facade for what is essentially a typing primitive). Propagation pattern (hybrid Depends at router boundary + explicit parameter through internal layers) followed the existing precedent of `get_principal` and `get_inference_port` Depends in the inference router; ContextVar would have inverted the explicit-passing pattern that the inference path follows from S7 with predictable test-fragility around context isolation in async code, and pure Depends through internal layers would have coupled the use case and adapter to FastAPI contradicting the hexagonal commitment. The friction surfaced at commit 2/3 split (described under prompt 3) was operator-discipline-shaped, not pattern-mismatch-shaped.

  Reflection prompt 2 (attribute namespace coherence) — The three `tenant.*` attributes from S15 plus the four `gen_ai.cost.*` attributes from S14 sit cleanly on the same LiteLLMAdapter span without duplication or implicit coupling. The two namespaces are conceptually distinct (tenant identity versus call cost), and within each namespace the attributes form a complete unit (tenant.id + tenant.jurisdiction + tenant.cost_attribution_id is the request-scoped tenant identity payload; gen_ai.cost.input_usd + gen_ai.cost.output_usd + gen_ai.cost.total_usd + gen_ai.cost.pricing_status is the per-call cost payload with the drift flag). One forward note worth surfacing: `tenant.cost_attribution_id` is the bridge between the two namespaces — it is the per-tenant attribution shape that downstream cost rollup queries (P9+) and optimization recommendations (P11) join on to produce per-tenant cost-aware recommendations. The bridge attribute is structurally honest at the trace level but creates a dependency that the audit posture should track: if `tenant.cost_attribution_id` ever decouples from `tenant.id` at the registry level (e.g., subsidiaries of a parent organisation sharing billing), the cost rollup queries will need to read against `cost_attribution_id` rather than `tenant_id`. Recording as a forward note for whichever package introduces multi-tenant billing aggregation, rather than committing to the one-to-one assumption now.

  Reflection prompt 3 (the classification deferral in implementation) — The deferral felt right in implementation. The TenantContext at three fields is structurally coherent (tenant identity, jurisdiction, cost-attribution); adding a classification field would have been paper architecture without a schema column, a registry-row value, or a consumer that reads it. No place in the code surfaced as wanting classification — the inference adapter span emits what it has; the `request_completion` use case has no policy logic that would gate on classification yet; the registry adapter would have had to invent a default value to populate the field, which is exactly the failure mode D34 prevents for credentials. The schema-verification-at-framing pattern is a strong promotion candidate for the methodology document's Patterns observed section: framing decisions that name fields, columns, or attributes should be cross-referenced against `charter/schema.md` (and equivalent code-truth documents) before close, because schema state is the truth and v1 epic notes that contradict it produce paper architecture that surfaces only at build time. The pattern is structural enough to live in the methodology document; recording explicitly here so the next strategic conversation can promote it.

  Reflection prompt 4 (P4 measured outcomes against the bet) — The seven industry-overlay metrics produced honest numbers but at a small enough sample (two sessions, both same-day, both clean-close) that face value is provisional. Change failure rate of 0% reads as a discipline win at the package scale but cannot be read as a methodology trend until phase-level data accumulates. Lead time of ~4-6h same-day reflects P4's small RICE-effort score (0.8) rather than throughput discipline. Reliability of 100% is the meaningful number at this scale because the discipline either holds or doesn't hold — it held under the small window. The bet-native metrics surface more usefully at the package scale: discipline-adherence concrete (charter touch-points updated in-commit at every required boundary, AST tests passed at every commit, decision-to-code translation within-session for both D49 and D50), architectural-durability concrete (contract count stable at 15, +29 tests, five named drift findings recorded explicitly), bet-direction integrity concrete (zero new roadmap versions during P4, four of five role-functions exercised across the package with PM exercised at the strategic boundary rather than within build sessions). The metrics-measure-methodology framing applies: the perfect numbers in this entry are signal about whether the discipline stayed tight on a small package, not signal about whether a senior product leader at scale can sustain this performance. The Phase 1 close audit numbers will be the first credible signal at the methodology-trend scale.

  Reflection prompt 5 (what P4 demonstrated and what it did not) — P4 closed the optimization-recommendation triangle that D9 commits and D41 architects: token, latency, and quality data have been on the trace level since P2; cost is now there too via D49's four `gen_ai.cost.*` attributes; per-tenant attribution is there via D50's `tenant.cost_attribution_id`. The case-study evidence for the bet's enterprise-defensibility claim now exists at the trace level — a procurement reader can open a Langfuse trace and see the cost dimension structurally captured rather than computed downstream, which is the architectural-correctness claim P4 was framed to demonstrate. What remains unproven that P5 onward must address: the eval harness (P5) needs to consume the cost data to produce cost-per-successful-task per D8; the optimization layer (P11) needs to actually generate the third recommendation shape ("this costs N% more for M% quality at the same task type") that the trace-level cost data unlocks; the production-shaped tenant onboarding (D13 deferred) and the cost-ceiling enforcement architecture (deferred to Phase 2) are the two adjacent commitments that the case study has not yet executed. Nothing in P4's outcome changes the framing of subsequent packages; the dependency chain (P4 → P5 → ... → P11) holds, and P5 inherits a stack that has cost data structurally available rather than something it must add itself. The classification deferral is the one open commitment the case study now carries forward to P7 or P8; it is small enough (one field, one column) that it does not change phase-level scope, but it does mean the audit at Phase 1 close will surface it as deferred rather than landed.

- methodology: The S15 commit-shape granularity question recurred and was resolved by operator discipline rather than tooling. The prompt named commit 2 as the resolver and commit 3 as the propagation + attribute swap; the naïve order (port signature widening in commit 2) produces an intermediate broken state where the resolver returns a TenantContext that has nowhere to flow. Resolution: commit 2 keeps the port signature accepting `tenant_id`, the router's resolver extracts `tenant_context.tenant_id` for the call, then commit 3 widens the port and switches the OTel namespace together. The split preserves the prompt's commit shape without producing a broken intermediate state. Same shape as the S14 lesson (schema-tightening migrations should explicitly list adapter consumers) but at the application-layer port level instead of the schema-and-consumer level — surfacing as a generalisation worth landing in methodology: any change that widens a function or method signature should explicitly enumerate the consumer call sites that must update in the same commit, or the commit-shape granularity should treat the signature-and-consumers pair as one commit. The pattern recurred at S14 and S15; if it recurs again at the next package boundary, the structural-promotion threshold suggests promoting the discipline to a session-prompt convention.

## Carryover-cleanup strategic block
roles: analyst, PM, architect, technical writer
mode: strategic

- Produced: Five charter commits, no code touched. Commit 5: this entry.

- Decisions: D51 (PRFAQ voice and audience supersede D45's case-study framing — vendor product-launch voice in press release, vendor voice in FAQ, AI Labs teams and procurement as the target audience; D45's living-artefact commitment preserved intact, only voice and audience superseded; Kano: must-have). D52 (identity foundation deferred to Phase 2 in supersession of D3 — Keycloak realm, OIDC, SAML SP, SCIM 2.0, federated session management activate at Phase 2 when production deployment context arrives; Phase 1 retains auth-middleware-on-every-endpoint per D23 with the dev signed-token backend and the production-shaped Keycloak backend stubbed; D10's Keycloak 26 stack-version pin stands for Phase 2 activation; Kano: must-have).

- Tests: `make lint` 15 contracts kept, 0 broken. `uv run pytest` 155 passed, 7 skipped — baseline unchanged across the session. No code touched; this is a documentation-only strategic session. The state-at-open verification surfaced one open question — the operator's `docs/status-2026-05-06.md` was untracked at session open and resolved into commit 1 by relocating to `docs/notes/`.

- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (pattern recurrence and audit posture) — The Patterns observed entry's full lineage S8 → S13 → S14 → S15 reads honest precisely because the pattern was visible across multiple sessions before it was named as recurrence. What changed at this session was not the pattern's third or fourth instance; sub-shape A's two instances at S8 and S13 had already produced the comparative S13-vs-S8 reflection-prompt-5 paragraph, and sub-shape B's articulation lived as the S15 methodology line. The threshold that flipped was the existence of the Patterns observed section itself as a place for the observation to land — `charter/methodology.md` v1 opened at the P4-open strategic block with both Patterns observed and Failure modes as placeholder sections, and the carryover-cleanup session was the first strategic conversation since then. The pattern had been visible; what was missing was the surface to record it on. This reads against the methodology's own "metrics measure methodology, not operator" framing — the observation was carried in operator memory across S14 and S15 and waited for the right surface. The honest reading is that the Patterns observed section's existence is itself a discipline addition that activated the recording; without it, the pattern would have remained as scattered reflection-prompt content across multiple session-log entries, never compacted into a named pattern. Sub-shape B's structural-promotion-threshold note carries forward verbatim because the recurrence is fresh and the threshold is the right gate; sub-shape A's two instances are honest precedent but the rebrand pattern is unlikely to recur in a learning sprint, so the lineage cite is honest precisely because both shapes are commit-granularity-emergent rather than because both predict future drift.

  Reflection prompt 2 (drift-uncaught failure) — The Failure modes entry records that identity drift was uncaught from S4 forward and surfaced 2026-05-06 by a status-snapshot pass that the operator initiated outside the established audit cadence. This is honest data about what the audit posture caught and what it did not. The discipline as it stood at S4 through P4 close included session-log reflections, charter touch-points, decisions log, package-archive retrospectives, and AST-and-import-linter mechanical enforcement — all operating correctly within their scope, none performing a charter-vs-archive consistency check on package descriptions. No D-entry committed that check; no AST or import-linter rule covered package-description content. The catch was operator-initiated analysis, not mechanical enforcement. The case study is honest if it reports this honestly: the existing audit posture did not catch silent-deferral-class drift, the drift was caught by an unrelated operator action, and the discipline addition (charter-vs-archive consistency check at every package close) is committed in the Failure modes entry as a promotion candidate rather than as a now-active mechanical rule. The structural-promotion threshold convention applies: this is the first instance the drift was caught; if it recurs at a later package, the cost calculus tips toward landing the mechanical check. Until then, the operator's discipline is to run the comparison manually as part of each package close walkthrough. The honest framing is that the methodology improved by virtue of catching the drift; it did not catch it because the methodology was already mature. Whether the case study can claim the discipline before the mechanical-enforcement upgrade lands is a phase-audit question, not a session-level question; the conservative framing is to claim the discipline catches drift when it has caught drift mechanically, not when it has caught drift via operator initiative.

  Reflection prompt 3 (PRFAQ form mismatch) — D45 was committed three sessions ago at the P3-post strategic block with the case-study voice and audience commitment. The structural-form mismatch surfaced at v1 implementation: the press release and FAQ accumulated `[OPERATOR REWRITE — ...]` markers throughout because the case-study voice did not fit the Amazon-PRFAQ vendor-launch shape. D51 corrects voice and audience in this session; v1's markers are now read as canary signals rather than as operator-rewrite-required placeholders. This says something specific about strategic-mode commitments that have not yet been implemented at the moment of commitment. D45 committed an artefact shape (PRFAQ as living external-voice document) and an artefact-content commitment (case-study voice for senior product leaders, CPOs, consultancies) in the same D-entry. The shape commitment held; the content commitment did not survive implementation. The existing commitment-then-correction shape (D45 then D51) handled this honestly — D45 is preserved as written, D51 supersedes the parts that did not survive, the version log of `charter/prfaq.md` preserves v1 verbatim with v2 alongside, the operator-authored v1-framing note relocates v1's scaffolding-paragraph context as historical content. This is the right discipline: D-entries that commit artefact shape and artefact content should be allowed to be partially superseded by later D-entries that touch only the parts that did not survive implementation, rather than forcing wholesale supersession. A "form-confirmed-on-implementation" sub-state on D-entries would add ceremony for a question the existing commitment-then-correction shape resolves cleanly. The reading worth recording: D-entries are commitments at a moment in time; supersession by later D-entries is the methodology's structural answer to commitments that did not survive, and the discipline does not require pre-emptive sub-state on D-entries that anticipate the survival question.

  Reflection prompt 4 (Phase 2 cluster) — The Phase 2 deferred-features cluster is now eight named items: cost ceilings (D41); multi-tier model routing (D41); progressive throttling (D41); full DORA / CORE4 instrumentation (deferred per the methodology metrics entry in `charter/deferred-decisions.md`); pricing-table format evolution to YAML/TOML under `ops/` (S14 reflection forward-note); production-shaped tenant onboarding, full D13 implementation; step-mode-shaped automation for narrow task types (deferred to Phase 1 close audit per `charter/phase-1-prd.md` out-of-scope); federated identity foundation (D52 this session, in supersession of D3). These cluster around a coherent shape: production deployment plus scale-out. This is materially earlier than the "Phase 2 framing happens at Phase 1 close audit" commitment, and the cluster's coherence is itself signal worth recording. Two readings on whether to act on it now versus at the Phase 1 close audit. First reading: the cluster is signal that Phase 2 framing should start surfacing now, because waiting until the Phase 1 close audit means the framing conversation receives a fully-formed cluster as a fait accompli rather than as a starting point. Second reading: the deferred-features list is exactly the input the Phase 1 close audit framing conversation should consume, and acting on it earlier would compress the audit's strategic surface. The honest answer is that the cluster's emergence is captured here as observation, but the Phase 1 close audit retains the formal framing role — the cluster informs the framing conversation's starting point rather than substituting for it. The reflection prompt's framing tension is preserved rather than collapsed; the audit posture is more rigorous when the framing conversation is reserved for its proper moment. The roadmap.md v3 entry's note that "Phase 2 absorbs the deferred identity foundation in addition to the deferred-decisions cluster" is the formal record that the cluster is tracked; the framing remains delegated.

  Reflection prompt 5 (in-block fabrication-class drift, surfaced by operator) — A drift event surfaced in the framing conversation that produced this strategic-session prompt: an earlier draft of PRFAQ v2 content fabricated a name attribution ("Casey Whitfield") for a customer or executive quote, conflating fabrication with vendor-PR voice. The operator caught the miss during framing review and flagged fabrication as rejected for the v2 commit; decision 1 at this session's voice-sensitive surfacing for commit 4 reflects the corrected discipline (representative-role attributions only, no fabricated names). The miss is named here as an in-block observation worth surfacing because it sits adjacent to the Failure modes section's first entry without being literally the same drift class — silent-deferral drift versus fabrication-class drift are different shapes — but the no-fabrication policy that flows from the operator's caution applies to model-drafted content broadly. The methodology question worth recording: model-drafted artefacts in PRFAQ-shaped voice (or any vendor-style voice) carry a default-toward-plausibility risk that fabrication is the path of least resistance; the discipline addition implied is that representative-role attributions are the safe shape for model-drafted vendor-voice content, with real names landing only when the operator has named them explicitly. Captured as a candidate for a future Failure modes entry once a recurrence happens or once a separate strategic conversation can land it; treating it as a single-instance observation in this session-log entry is the conservative discipline given the prompt's commit shape was locked at five commits and the operator did not direct its addition as a sixth Failure modes entry in this session.

- methodology: The carryover-cleanup session was itself an instance of the discipline of running periodic charter-vs-as-built consistency checks outside the established audit cadence. The pattern that produced it (an operator status-snapshot question that turned into a charter audit) is descriptive enough to record as a methodology observation: the act of asking "where am I against the roadmap?" with the model can produce charter-audit deliverables as a side-effect, particularly at between-packages quiet moments when the active surface is not contested. This is not a discipline addition (the answer is not "always do a status snapshot at every between-packages boundary") but it is a captured pattern: between-packages quiet moments are low-cost windows for consistency-check passes, and the resulting findings can flow into a strategic-mode session like this one. Whether to promote the pattern to a methodology Patterns observed entry depends on whether it recurs; recording here so the next strategic conversation can decide.

## Fabrication-class promotion strategic session
roles: analyst, architect, technical writer
mode: strategic

- Produced: Two charter commits, no code touched. Commit 1 (`docs(charter): land second Failure modes entry on fabrication-class drift`) appends a second Failure modes entry to `charter/methodology.md` following the existing entry's four-part shape (what drifted, when caught, how corrected, discipline addition), promoting the in-block fabrication-class miss observed in the prior carryover-cleanup session's reflection prompt 5; the same commit adds a promotion-candidate item (proper-noun-attribution check on model-drafted vendor-voice artefacts) to `charter/deferred-decisions.md`'s Methodology mechanical-enforcement upgrades section. Commit 2: this entry.

- Decisions: None. The discipline addition is recorded in the methodology Failure modes entry; the mechanical-enforcement candidate lives in deferred-decisions. Neither edit selects between alternatives nor makes a fresh architectural commitment, so no D-entry is required.

- Tests: `make lint` 15 contracts kept, 0 broken. `uv run pytest` 155 passed, 7 skipped — baseline unchanged. Documentation-only strategic session.

- Reflection: Promotion reasoning is the structural finding worth naming. Failure modes entries land on single-instance whereas Patterns observed entries require recurrence. The asymmetry is the methodology's discipline shape — descriptive observations need recurrence to earn naming, but corrective discipline-additions land on a single observed drift event because waiting for recurrence pays additional drift instances. The prior carryover-cleanup session's reflection prompt 5 framed fabrication-class drift as "candidate for a future Failure modes entry once a recurrence happens or once a separate strategic conversation can land it"; the framing was procedurally cautious but conflated the Patterns observed recurrence rule with the Failure modes single-instance rule, and this session's promotion corrects that. The asymmetry was nearly missed at the prior strategic block; recording the corrective in the Reflection here so the next strategic conversation does not need to re-derive it. The recovery sequence — drift surfaced in framing → operator catch via "who is Casey Whitfield?" → in-block correction at carryover-cleanup commit 4 (PRFAQ v2) → observation captured in carryover-cleanup reflection prompt 5 → promotion to methodology Failure modes entry in this session — closed cleanly across five steps with every transition having a surface to land on; no signal was lost between steps. The no-fabrication policy named in the new entry's discipline addition flows directly from the operator's correction at commit 4 and extends beyond PRFAQ to any model-drafted artefact in external/vendor voice, because the structural risk (vendor-shape expects named attributions, model defaults to plausibility) generalises across that artefact class.

- methodology: The act of promoting an in-block observation from a session-log reflection prompt to a methodology Failure modes entry is itself a methodology pattern. The reflection prompt is the staging surface; the methodology entry is the load-bearing surface; the promotion is the discipline that converts in-flight observation into permanent methodology content. The prior session's conservative framing (defer to recurrence or separate strategic conversation) was honest at the time it was written — the prompt's commit shape was locked at five commits and the operator did not direct a sixth Failure modes entry — so the staging-surface-then-promotion pattern actually worked: the observation landed where it could, waited for the right strategic moment, and was promoted in the next strategic session whose deliverable it could land as. Recording here because the staging-then-promotion shape is reusable for future in-block observations whose proper home is the methodology document but whose moment of capture is a different session's commit shape.

## P5-open strategic block
roles: analyst, PM, architect, technical writer
mode: strategic

- Produced: One charter commit landing the P5 epic note at `charter/packages/p5-epic.md` (v1 draft preserved per D43, reconciliation deferred to package archive at P5 close), D53 in `charter/decisions.md` capturing the framing-level commitments (scoring sheet primitive, per-tenant storage, appliers-as-data, Reading-C human-oversight posture), and a `charter/current-package.md` transition from between-packages state to active-P5 state with three forecast sessions S16-S18.

- Decisions: D53 (P5 evaluation harness framing). Existing D-entries reified in the framing: D1 (database-per-tenant), D8 (cost-per-successful-task as Phase 1 metric), D9 (recommendation-shaped output), D11 (incremental scaffold; narrow-scope discipline), D13 (production-shape tenant onboarding deferral; activation condition for platform-baseline library), D15 (default model Qwen 2.5 7B; LLM-as-judge default until calibration evidence surfaces), D16 (bounded context on hexagonal), D23 (auth middleware with signed-token Phase 1 backend), D27 (OTel as observability portability boundary; harness-side cost computation reads cleanly), D32 (no shared tables for tenant data; per-tenant storage falls inside this discipline), D37 (history-shape storage precedent), D41 (cost capture commitment; P5 consumes the cost dimension), D42 (Kano + RICE), D43 (epic note plus archive reconciliation; first P5 instance lands), D52 (identity-foundation deferral; Phase 1 auth-middleware-with-signed-token).

- Tests: None. Documentation-only changes; no code touched. `git status` clean prior to commit; tree dirty only with the artefacts staged for this commit.

- Reflection: Four load-bearing moments in this framing. First, naming as architecture. The framing surfaced "scoring sheet" as the user-facing name for what eval literature calls a rubric. The change is naming, not architecture, but the rationale is case-study-shaped: layman terminology helps the audit-shaped reader without losing precision because the underlying primitive (criteria with levels and definitions, versioned and applied via three applier modes) is unchanged. Naming convention committed: scoring sheet at the user-facing layer; rubric retained in code where literature precedent matters (the rubric-application record name stays). Second, Reading-A/B/C as a framing tool. The three-reading frame let the architectural commitment (data model absorbs human review) separate cleanly from the UI scope (deferred). The pattern likely recurs at P11 (recommendation surface) and P12 (active testing) framing, where a similar reading-decomposition between data-model architecture and UI scope will need to land. Worth elevating to a methodology-level pattern at the next phase audit if the recurrence holds. Third, per-tenant-only as discipline-holding. The framing resisted a hybrid storage default and named the activation condition for control-plane platform-baseline sheets explicitly. This mirrors D11 (incremental scaffold), D52 (identity deferred to Phase 2), and the cost-ceiling enforcement deferral. The recurring pattern: in the absence of a forcing function (proximate writes that would later need migration, or a real consumer in Phase 1), narrow-scope is the right discipline; the migration path stays mechanical when activation arrives because schema is stable. Fourth, confidence-2 forecast at framing. P4 closed at confidence-3 because the substrate carried it; P5 enters at confidence-2 because the design space is wider. Framing committed five sub-decisions (storage, primitive name, versioning, applier-as-data, Reading-C), leaving three open for build-session framing (replay seam at S17, cost-computation locus at S17, scoring-extensibility shape at S16 or S17). The hypothesis is that S16 close reads as confidence-3; if S17 still reads as confidence-2, the framing under-narrowed and the Phase 1 close audit should surface that as drift.

- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (applier port shape, D54) — Decided during S16 as Option A (single polymorphic async port with adapter-side dispatch). The build experience supports the choice cleanly: at one concrete deterministic adapter, the dispatch logic in `ExactMatchApplier.apply` is a three-way `if/elif` over `ApplierType` values, with deterministic resolving the function name against a small registry and the other two branches raising `NotImplementedError`. No domain-shaped knowledge leaked into the dispatch — the choice of executable behaviour is data, exactly as D53 framed it. The dispatch-mechanical contingency named at framing (revisit if S17's prompt-applier addition starts encoding domain knowledge) does not bite at S16 and remains the right gate for S17 framing. The substantive build-time deviation worth flagging is the return type: framing recommended `-> RubricApplication`, the build returned `str | None`. The reason was structural-honesty — the applier owns scoring, the use case owns record assembly, and threading persistence-layer fields (id, created_at) plus context-layer fields (interaction_id) through the port couples the applier to concerns it has no opinion about. The use case is the structural authority on RubricApplication construction; the port stays free of those concerns. This is the kind of build-time refinement the framing's "decided during build" framing anticipates; D54's text records both the choice and the deviation explicitly. Worth surfacing as a methodology observation: framing prompts that name return types should be read as recommendations subject to the structural-honesty test at build time, not as fixed contracts. The reflection-prompt-then-D-entry pipeline carried the deviation cleanly into the charter without requiring the framing to anticipate every build-level structural question.

  Reflection prompt 2 (schema shape watch-items) — (a) STI vs CTI for appliers. The cross-column NULL invariant on `ApplierConfig` is enforced at the domain layer per framing (no schema CHECKs on the cross-column shape; only on `applier_type` itself). At one concrete applier type (deterministic), the `__post_init__` validation is a simple three-branch `if/elif` and reads cleanly; the unit tests in commit 1 cover all 12 (three acceptance plus nine rejection) combinations. The shape did not strain at S16. Watch-item carried forward to S17: when the prompt applier lands, the validation gains a fourth nullable-column commitment (prompt_template + judge_model both required for prompt; both forbidden for deterministic and human); if the validation logic begins growing branches per added applier type or the cross-column conditional becomes hard to read, CTI promotion (separate `deterministic_appliers` and `prompt_appliers` tables) is the candidate at S17 framing rather than S17 build. (b) Score-as-text foreclosure (D55). The e2e test exercised text scoring with `pass` and `fail` labels and the persistence path round-tripped them cleanly; the use case never needed to aggregate or compare scores numerically, so the foreclosure did not bite at S16. The structural-honesty argument held: the criterion's level definitions are the right authority on what its scores mean, and S17's cost-per-successful-task implementation reads them rather than relying on a generic numeric default. Watch-item: if S17 surfaces a path where criterion-level interpretation cannot cleanly serve (for example a regression report that wants to compute an average across heterogeneous criteria), the refinement lands at S17 alongside the cost-per-successful-task wiring — but the locked-in choice prevents a quality-foreclosing default leaking ahead of that judgement.

  Reflection prompt 3 (first-deterministic-applier choice) — `exact_match` was the right S16 choice. It is the simplest possible deterministic primitive, exercises the registry shape without dispatch complexity (the function takes interaction + output + criterion and returns "pass"/"fail"), and reads naturally against the `interaction.expected_output` jsonb shape (`expected_output["value"]` is the comparable bytes). The choice signals design intent for the deterministic library at `contexts/evaluation/adapters/outbound/deterministic_library.py`: each entry is a callable that takes the interaction (so it can read expected_output for the comparison shape it implements), the output, and the criterion (so it can consult criterion-specific configuration like a threshold or pattern when needed), and returns a string label that aligns with the criterion's `levels`. Future deterministic adapters at S17 (regex match, JSON validate, embedding cosine) extend the same shape: each is a function in the library, registered by name, taking the same three keyword arguments and returning a label string. The criteria for adding a deterministic primitive: (1) the comparison rule is small and stable (no LLM call, no DB round-trip); (2) the input shape is derivable from interaction.expected_output plus output without bespoke per-criterion configuration; (3) the output label space is small and aligns naturally with criterion levels (binary outcomes for `exact_match`/`regex_match`/`json_validate`; thresholded continuous for `embedding_cosine`).

  Reflection prompt 4 (forward-affordance discipline at S16) — The trace_id exclusion held cleanly. The integration test exercised the full apply_scoring_sheet flow, the use case constructs RubricApplication records, and at no point did the build surface a pull toward needing trace_id at the S16 column shape. The S14 contrast (cost-ceiling forward-affordance landed alongside cost-attribution because a single migration was the natural unit) does not apply: trace_id at S16 has no co-arriving column to ride alongside, no S17 backfill against existing rows (the e2e test data is short-lived and self-cleaning per the script's truncate step), and no existing consumer asking for the column. The discipline-holding shape was the right call. The methodology observation: forward-affordance is not a default — it is justified by a forcing function (a co-arriving migration, a backfill cost, an active consumer). When neither pressure exists, deferring is the discipline; when one or more does, landing alongside the proximate work avoids the retrofit D41 named explicitly as a learning failure. The S14-S16 contrast is the cleanest articulation of the principle yet — a follow-on methodology Patterns observed entry candidate at the next strategic conversation, if the S17 trace_id landing supports the same shape.

  Reflection prompt 5 (confidence-2 → confidence-3 trajectory) — P5 entered framing at confidence-2 because the design space was wider than P4's. S16 close supports a confidence-3 read for the foundations slice: the bounded context exists with the structural shape D16 commits, the seven-table data model lives on the per-tenant track per D32, the polymorphic ApplierPort with adapter-side dispatch realises the appliers-as-data framing from D53, and the apply_scoring_sheet use case ends-to-end produces the rubric_application records that S17's cost-per-successful-task and S18's regression report consume. The build did not surface unforeseen structural questions that reshape the S17 framing. Two refinements lived at the build-time shape level rather than the architectural level: (a) D54's return type deviated from the framing recommendation in the structurally honest direction, and the deviation is documented in D54's text. (b) D55 promoted from a sub-decision-in-reflection-paragraph at the framing prompt to a load-bearing must-have D-entry at S16 close per the operator's promotion. Both refinements are within-foundations-scope adjustments rather than re-foundations work. The S17 framing question is whether the polymorphic-adapter switch logic stays mechanical when the prompt-applier branch lands or whether the dispatch starts encoding domain knowledge; the framing prompt for S17 should name this as the explicit-during-build decision (mirroring D54's contingency clause). Confidence-3 read holds for foundations; the residual confidence question is at the S17 boundary, which is the right altitude for it.

- methodology: Build-time refinements to framing-prompt specifics surfaced as a recurrence shape worth recording. S15's commit-shape adjustment (port signature widening pair landed as one commit rather than two consecutive commits) and S16's return-type adjustment (ApplierPort returns `str | None` rather than `RubricApplication`) share a structural family: framing prompts name specifics (commit boundaries, function signatures, module names) that the build experience refines toward structural honesty. The discipline that worked across both: the framing prompt's specifics are recommendations subject to the structural-honesty test, not fixed contracts; refinements land in the relevant D-entry text (S15 in D50's reasoning, S16 in D54's reasoning) rather than as in-prompt corrections. Methodology candidate worth surfacing at the next strategic conversation: the framing-vs-build relationship is itself a methodology pattern — framing names the option space and the strongest recommendation; build commits to the choice with whatever refinements the actual code surfaces; D-entries record both. This is the appliers-as-data framing applied to the framing-prompt-vs-build relationship: framing is the data substrate the build invokes against, not the executable contract.
