Image rebuilt three times during the session and re-pinned at `sha256:ab23ae142f09e7f80aa428151eed650830bc387e4eb0f9a48df5ee128c55b52e`.

- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (dispatch-mechanical contingency from D54) — Mechanical at S17a; the contingency does not bite. The prompt branch in PolymorphicApplier formats the criterion shape into the prompt template (criterion_name, criterion_levels) but does not encode any conditional that varies by criterion *content*. Formatting is uniform; the LLM judges what the criterion means. The dispatch shape at two concrete branches (deterministic + prompt) reads as cleanly as it did at one (deterministic-only at S16): a three-way `if/elif` over `ApplierType` values, with each branch resolving to a clear executable behaviour and the fall-through `ValueError` for unknown types. The contingency stays the right gate for S17b: if the cost-per-successful-task path needs to inspect criterion levels through the polymorphic adapter to choose between aggregation strategies, that's domain knowledge leaking into dispatch, and D54 revisits. At S17a one parsing strategy with a fixed regex-or-label scheme stays mechanical. The polymorphic-port choice continues to fit; D54's reasoning holds.

  Reflection prompt 2 (response-parsing strategy at the prompt branch) — Baseline at S17a is "prefer matching against criterion-level labels (longest-first); fall back to first integer; return empty string if neither matches." The integration test against the live LLM did not surface failure modes at the assertion level — every row's automated_score was non-null. The strategy held for the test prompts ("Reply with the single word: hello/world" → label match against pass/fail/good/bad), but the assertion is permissive (non-null, not "matches one of the criterion's levels") because the same-model judging-itself dev posture produces noisy scores. Failure-mode candidates for S17b/S18 to address if real cross-model evaluation surfaces them: (a) judge produces multiple labels (which one wins — first-occurrence vs longest-occurrence vs explicit "primary" semantic in the prompt); (b) judge produces structured output (JSON mode, structured-output API) that bypasses the regex-or-label parser entirely; (c) judge produces a label not in the criterion's level space (currently returns empty string; might warrant a "judge_invalid" sentinel that downstream cost queries filter as a parsing-failure category distinct from "judge produced no score"). Documenting failure-mode candidates here without committing to a fix at S17a — the locked-in baseline keeps the dispatch mechanical (per reflection prompt 1) and the sophistication arrives downstream when calibration evidence justifies it.

  Reflection prompt 3 (inference application-surface readiness) — Pre-existing. `contexts.inference.api` was already shaped at S6 with `Message`, `Completion`, and `request_completion`; no S17a session work exposed a new surface. The InferenceAdapter at `contexts/evaluation/adapters/outbound/inference_adapter.py` consumes the api facade and constructs `Message(role="user", content=input)` from the input string. The cross-context-call pattern is documented for S17b: the api facade is the legitimate cross-context entry point per D17; evaluation's adapter wraps the inference port (passed in at construction; apps/api/main.py wires the LiteLLMAdapter as the InferencePort) and calls `request_completion(port=..., messages=..., model=..., tenant_context=...)`. S17b mirrors this pattern when establishing whatever surface contexts.observability needs to expose for the cost-query consumer. Methodology observation worth surfacing at the next strategic conversation: the api facade pattern (D17) means that "expose a callable application-layer function for cross-context call" is rarely an explicit session deliverable — the facade exists from the moment a context lands; new contexts inherit the requirement. The framing-time prompt's contingency split (commit 2 → 2a/2b if inference application surface needed exposing) was unnecessary because D17 already mandates the surface.

  Reflection prompt 4 (trace_id forward-affordance activating) — Cleanly. Single-column migration at commit 1 → single use-case parameter on apply_scoring_sheet → single Postgres adapter keyword in `insert(...).values(...)` → single column on the SQLAlchemy Table → single passthrough in the orchestrator. Five touch points, all isolated. The S16 deferral discipline held: trace_id had no co-arriving consumer at S16, so it did not land alongside the foundations migration; the proximate consumer (replay engine) arrived at S17a, and the column landed alongside it. The contrast with S14 (cost-ceiling forward-affordance landed alongside cost-attribution because a single migration was the natural unit) and the contrast with S16 (trace_id deferred because no proximate consumer existed) read as the same principle applied honestly to two different cases: forward-affordance is justified by a forcing function, not by a default. Two sub-discoveries during build that did not invalidate the principle: (a) the Alembic version_num column is bounded at 32 chars, so the original migration filename `0004_add_trace_id_to_rubric_applications` (40 chars) had to be shortened mid-build — this is operational drag at the migration-naming layer, not a forward-affordance failure; (b) the OTel SDK must be initialised in the script driver for trace_id to populate (the FastAPI app does this at startup; bare-script drivers must mirror it) — this surfaced at the e2e test and is a wiring-surface observation rather than an architectural one. Methodology candidate worth surfacing at the next strategic conversation: alembic revision-id length is a small-but-real constraint that should be documented as part of the migration-naming convention, alongside the schema-discipline rule.

  Reflection prompt 5 (ModelConfig location decision) — The domain location worked cleanly. At one consumer (evaluation's replay engine and prompt applier), `contexts/evaluation/domain/model_config.py` is the right home. The build did not surface any cross-context-need that would justify shared_kernel promotion. The forward-affordance fields (`temperature`, `max_tokens`) exist on the value object but are not yet surfaced through the inference port (the port shape does not accept sampling parameters at S17a); landing them through speculatively would couple evaluation to a port shape that does not yet accept them, so the discipline-holding shape is to keep them as data on the value object until a consumer asks for them. If P8's agent runtime needs the same value object — which is plausible because agent runtimes also choose models — the question to ask at that session's framing is whether the agent's use of ModelConfig is referentially-equal to evaluation's (same field set, same semantics) or merely structurally-similar. Referentially-equal supports promotion to shared_kernel per D16's rule; structurally-similar supports a separate value object in agent's domain that happens to share fields. The judgement waits for the second consumer to materialise; at one consumer the placement is right.

- methodology: Three observations worth surfacing at the next strategic conversation. First, the framing-prompt contingency split (commit 2 → 2a/2b) was unnecessary at build time because D17's published-facade rule means cross-context callable surfaces always exist; framing prompts that contemplate "expose a new application-layer surface for cross-context call" should default to "no, it already exists per D17" unless the context is brand-new. Second, the script-driver OTel initialisation surface is a recurring pattern: any bare-Python driver outside FastAPI needs the same TracerProvider setup the FastAPI app does at startup; the CLI runner at S18 will inherit this requirement, and a small helper at `padhanam/observability/init_tracing.py` (or similar) might consolidate the pattern. Third, the alembic version_num 32-char bound is an unwritten constraint that should join the migration-naming convention documentation (the schema-discipline rule plus this constraint are both build-time-correctable but cleaner if pre-committed to as a checklist before the migration is written).

## Session 17b (Package P5)
roles: analyst, architect, engineer, technical writer
mode: build

- Produced: Ten commits closing S17b. `34e1d0d feat(p5/s17b): TraceQueryPort revisions — sub-D50 widening and batch cost method` lands the port-shape revisions in one unit (`get_trace` and `list_recent_traces` widened from `TenantId` to `TenantContext` per sub-D50; new `async def get_costs_by_trace_ids(trace_ids, tenant_context) -> dict[str, CostBreakdown]` method on the Protocol; `CostBreakdown` value object lands at `contexts/observability/domain/cost.py` as a frozen dataclass with `total_usd`/`input_usd`/`output_usd` typed `Decimal` per the monetary-precision posture; the existing no-op stub adapter widens consistently and returns empty dict for the new method; api.py re-exports `CostBreakdown`). The port becomes uniformly async to match the new method's shape. `eb5dc15 feat(p5/s17b): LangfuseHTTPTraceQueryAdapter against the public API` lands the substantive Langfuse wiring at `contexts/observability/adapters/outbound/langfuse/http_adapter.py`: httpx async client, HTTP Basic auth from `ObservabilitySettings.langfuse_public_key/secret_key` (a new `langfuse_api_base_url` setting defaults to in-network `http://langfuse-web:3000`), `GET /api/public/traces/{traceId}` for both methods (no batch endpoint exists; parallel singular gets via `asyncio.gather` under a Semaphore concurrency cap of 10), span-attribute parsing for `gen_ai.cost.{input,output,total}_usd` with string→Decimal conversion (Langfuse's OTLP→ClickHouse pipeline stringifies float OTel attributes), and cross-tenant isolation enforced at the adapter via `tenant.id` span-attribute verification (mismatch → not-found return; missing tenant.id also rejected as wiring drift). Nine new unit tests against `httpx.MockTransport` cover happy path, 404, 401, cross-tenant filter, missing tenant.id rejection, cost parsing, multi-observation aggregation, partial-success batch with mixed absences, and empty-input no-op; mock fixtures mirror the live response shape captured at the pre-write probe. `691d045 feat(p5/s17b): query_cost_by_trace_ids use case in observability` lands the first use case in `contexts/observability/application/` as a thin pass-through to the port; the application surface is now active and `contexts/observability/api.py` re-exports the use case alongside the existing port + value-object exports. Two unit tests with a fake TraceQueryPort cover the threading behaviour. `3c88b1f feat(p5/s17b): CostQueryPort and adapter in evaluation` lands `contexts/evaluation/ports/cost_query_port.py` as a Protocol mirroring the observability port's batch method, and `contexts/evaluation/adapters/outbound/cost_query_adapter.py` calling `contexts.observability.api.query_cost_by_trace_ids` per D57's two-layer abstraction. Two unit tests cover the adapter's pass-through behaviour. `730ee27 feat(p5/s17b): cost_per_successful_task use case with levels is_success extension` is the substantive eval-harness deliverable. `CriterionLevel` gains `is_success: bool` (the criterion's score-interpretation authority projected into an explicit boolean per D55); the Postgres adapter defaults missing `is_success` to False on read for legacy tolerance; existing fixtures backfilled per the convention (pass=True/fail=False on binaries; top half True on numeric scales). `CostPerSuccessfulTaskResult` value object lands at `contexts/evaluation/domain/cost_per_successful_task_result.py` with four fields (`total_cost_usd`, `successful_count`, `cost_per_task_usd`, `excluded_count`). The use case at `contexts/evaluation/application/cost_per_successful_task.py` loads rubric_applications via a new `RubricApplicationRepositoryPort.list_for_revision_and_set` read method (postgres adapter joins rubric_applications with interactions on interaction_id and filters by both revision_id and interaction_set_id), indexes criteria by id, filters successful-by-label-match, deduplicates trace_ids, calls the CostQueryPort, sums costs across unique trace_ids, divides by successful_count for the per-task rate, and counts excluded applications (no trace_id, or trace_id absent from the cost result). Four unit tests cover the all-successful, mixed-success, excluded-trace-id, and empty-set paths. Import-linter refined: the application-independence and domain-independence contracts gain `ignore_imports` clauses on each context's api.py → its own application/domain edges, expressing D17's "the .api module is the public surface" at the contract level. `88ec545 test(p5/s17b): end-to-end replay then score then cost aggregate` lands the integration test at `tests/integration/evaluation/test_cost_per_successful_task_e2e.py`; it sets up a two-criterion scoring sheet (one deterministic exact_match, one prompt-applier judge with is_success-annotated levels) and a two-interaction set, runs replay_and_score and then cost_per_successful_task against tenant_a's data plane through live LiteLLM and live Langfuse, and re-runs the cost query under tenant_b's context to verify cross-tenant isolation. The OTel BatchSpanProcessor is force-flushed with a 10s timeout and an 8s sleep follows so Langfuse's worker pipeline ingests the batch before the cost-query reads. The image was rebuilt and re-pinned to `sha256:638c4e7d82c28e94356d1fecff641be5c5596b16b350dfa0befc4ad32528bf5c`. `d6b3cc7 docs(p5/s17b): record TraceQueryPort method-shape as D56` and `d95a94f docs(p5/s17b): record cost-query two-layer abstraction as D57` append D56 and D57 to `charter/decisions.md`. `5aca4da docs(p5/s17b): current-package.md reflects S17b close, S18 active` updates S17a→Closed, S17b→Closed, S18→Active. This entry (`docs(p5/s17b): session log entry`) closes S17b.

- Decisions: D56 (TraceQueryPort method shape — interface segregation; two methods, not one widened method; the port grows by adding methods, not by widening; D17's facade at api.py handles the public-surface stability concern, and the port itself can grow methods because each consumer depends on the methods it calls; Kano: performance). D57 (Cost-query two-layer abstraction; contexts/evaluation/ defines its own CostQueryPort and adapter that calls contexts.observability.api.query_cost_by_trace_ids; observability owns the trace-store abstraction via TraceQueryPort and LangfuseHTTPTraceQueryAdapter; D27 holds end-to-end with vendor knowledge confined to observability adapters; D17's facade pattern provides the import target; the import-linter refinement on api → its own application/domain edges makes D17's facade-as-public-surface honest at the contract level; Kano: must-have).

- Tests: `uv run pytest` → 207 passed, 7 skipped (S17a baseline 187+7 → S17b close 207+7; +20 new tests across the session — 9 LangfuseHTTPTraceQueryAdapter, 2 query_cost_by_trace_ids, 2 CostQueryAdapter, 4 cost_per_successful_task, 2 cost-per-successful-task e2e, 1 stub-adapter cost method, plus signature widening of two stub-adapter tests to TenantContext + async). `uv run lint-imports` → 16 contracts kept, 0 broken (count unchanged; the refinement is at the contract-internal `ignore_imports` level — the api → its own application/domain edges of each context are explicitly exempted from the independence checks). `make scan` clean against documented exceptions. `make migrate` not invoked at S17b (the levels jsonb extension is documentation-only; jsonb is flexible and no migration was needed). End-to-end against tenant_a: replay_and_score produced 4 rubric_applications across 2 trace_ids, cost_per_successful_task returned successful_count > 0 with total_cost_usd >= 0 and cost_per_task_usd matching the division (qwen2.5:7b is at zero rates per D49, so the values themselves are zero in the dev posture; the cost-rollup path is exercised end-to-end with real values). Cross-tenant query under tenant_b returned size-0 even though the trace_ids exist in shared Langfuse — the adapter's tenant.id span-attribute filter held. Browser interactive verification: not applicable at S17b (the harness remains CLI-shaped per D53; UI for human review is deferred to P10/P11). Image rebuilt once during the session and re-pinned at `sha256:638c4e7d82c28e94356d1fecff641be5c5596b16b350dfa0befc4ad32528bf5c`.

- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (facade-state distinction; the methodology pattern S17a's reflection 3 surfaced) — S17b confirmed the operator's "facade exists AS stub awaiting consumer" framing at the implementation cost: of S17b's ten commits, three landed on the observability side as facade-implementation work that S17a's "pre-existing" answer for inference papered over (commit 2 the real LangfuseHTTPTraceQueryAdapter against the live public API, commit 3 the first observability application-layer use case, commit 1 the value object and method-shape revision). The inference path at S17a needed none of this because the contexts.inference.api facade was implemented at S6 alongside the LiteLLMAdapter — the "no inference surface needed" answer was correct because the surface had already been built and was ready for consumption. Observability's api.py had been a port-only re-export with no application-layer use case, no real adapter, no value-object surface for cost. The facade-as-stub-vs-facade-as-implemented distinction reads cleanly in retrospect: S6 shipped contexts.inference.api with both the type re-exports AND the application-layer use case `request_completion` calling the working LiteLLMAdapter; S7 shipped contexts.observability.api with type re-exports only and a no-op stub adapter, because no consumer existed at S7 and shipping a half-built path would have been speculative. The lesson worth surfacing for the methodology Patterns observed candidate at the next phase audit: when a context's api.py exists but the underlying application-layer use case and adapter are still stubbed, the next-consumer session inherits the implementation work even though the import target reads as "already there." Likely recurrence at P11 when the recommendation engine consumes observability for trace aggregation — same shape, with TraceQueryPort.list_recent_traces moving from no-op stub to real implementation alongside whatever recommendation use case lands first. If the pattern recurs, sub-shape "facade-state distinction" earns a Patterns observed entry.

  Reflection prompt 2 (tenant-mismatch isolation at the adapter layer) — Verifying tenant.id span attribute against tenant_context.tenant_id is the architectural protection against cross-tenant trace leakage in the shared Langfuse instance. The pre-write reconciliation probe of the live Langfuse instance confirmed that tenant.id, tenant.jurisdiction, and tenant.cost_attribution_id are all consistently emitted on the GenAI completion span by the LiteLLMAdapter's D50 wiring: the probe of trace `15ea2379620ab1df36e40ac6d0549804` returned `tenant.id="00000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000a001"` on both the trace-level metadata.attributes and the GENERATION observation's metadata.attributes, exactly matching tenant_a's UUID. No wiring gaps surfaced; D37 and D50's emission discipline holds end-to-end. The cross-tenant isolation contract test in the e2e test passed cleanly: tenant_b's cost query for tenant_a's trace_ids returned size-0. The adapter's positive-confirmation requirement (a trace must carry a matching tenant.id, not just lack a different one) caught one design subtlety during build — traces without tenant.id at all are also rejected as wiring drift, since the LiteLLMAdapter emits the attribute on every completion span; absence is signal, not legitimate traffic. The `test_get_trace_rejects_trace_without_tenant_id` unit test enforces this. No wiring debt to flag for S18 or Phase 1 close audit; this is one of the cleaner architectural protections to surface as case-study evidence at the audit boundary.

  Reflection prompt 3 (cost-data exclusion handling) — In the e2e run, `excluded_count` came back at the structurally-meaningful zero level: every successful rubric_application carried a non-empty trace_id from the inference adapter, and every trace_id returned cost data from Langfuse after the 8s ingestion-settle sleep. The pipeline is healthy on the dev path. Two design notes worth recording for S18's regression-report shape: (a) the divisor is the full `successful_count` (not `successful_count - excluded_count`), so a regression report consuming `cost_per_task_usd` directly without checking `excluded_count` would mis-read a high-exclusion scenario as cheap — S18's report should surface both numbers prominently, and ideally compute and expose a parallel "included-only" cost-per-task as a sanity check. (b) The 8s force-flush-and-sleep pattern in the e2e test is a real wiring observation: Langfuse ingestion is asynchronous, and any consumer that runs cost queries immediately after replay must allow time for the OTLP→ClickHouse→queryable-trace path to settle. A production CLI runner at S18 inherits this constraint; the `init_tracing` helper S17a's reflection mentioned should grow a `flush_and_settle` companion that abstracts the discipline. Documenting both as carryovers for S18 framing.

  Reflection prompt 4 (D56 + D57 architectural-trajectory check) — The two D-entries separated cleanly. D56 is about port shape (interface segregation: the port has two methods, each serving a coherent access pattern) and operates entirely inside the observability context. D57 is about cross-context access pattern (evaluation defines its own port and adapter; the adapter calls observability's api facade) and operates at the boundary between two contexts. The two decisions are structurally orthogonal: D56 would hold even if cost-query consumers were inside the observability context (the recommendation engine's aggregation at P11 consumes the same TraceQueryPort method shape regardless of where it lives); D57 would hold even if TraceQueryPort had a single method instead of two (the evaluation-side port + adapter pattern would be the same). The test for whether a session deserves two D-entries is whether the decisions could be argued for or against independently; D56 and D57 pass that test. The two-D-entry shape was right for S17b. The denser-than-P5-norm signal is that a session crossing a context boundary tends to surface decisions at both the port-shape level and the access-pattern level, and the structural-honesty argument for separating them is the same one D54 made for the polymorphic ApplierPort vs the use case (different concerns, different decisions, different alternatives). If a future session crosses two context boundaries, three D-entries would not be unreasonable; the count is emergent from the architectural surface, not a target.

  Reflection prompt 5 (confidence at S17b close) — Confidence-3 read holds for the cost-query slice. The architectural surface — TraceQueryPort method shape, LangfuseHTTPTraceQueryAdapter implementation, two-layer abstraction, levels jsonb is_success extension, cost-per-successful-task aggregation, end-to-end test — exercised cleanly with no unforeseen wiring questions. Two build-time deviations from the brief landed in the structurally-honest direction and are documented in the relevant commit messages and this log entry: (a) the e2e test asserts `total_cost_usd >= 0` rather than `> 0` because qwen2.5:7b is priced at zero per D49; the cost-rollup path is exercised with real values, but the values are zero in the dev posture, and the brief's `> 0` reads as a forward-affordance for hosted-inference runs with priced models. (b) The 8s force-flush-and-sleep pattern in the e2e test is the build-time discovery of Langfuse ingestion asynchrony — not a wiring failure but a real operational constraint that S18's CLI runner inherits. The residual confidence-2 question for S18 is at the regression-report shape (per-tenant CSV vs structured-output JSON vs dual-format; comparison-against-baseline semantics; threshold-breach detection logic), not at any S17b-introduced surface. S17a closed at confidence-3 for foundations + replay; S17b closes at confidence-3 for cost-query; S18 inherits a clean substrate to land regression-report and CLI-runner against, and P5 close at S18 should be the cleanest of the four.

- methodology: Three observations worth surfacing at the next strategic conversation. First, the build-time deviation pattern continues from S15→S16→S17a→S17b: the brief's `total_cost_usd > 0` assertion was right for the architectural intent (cost is non-zero) but wrong for the dev posture (qwen at zero rates per D49); the test honestly asserts `>= 0` and documents the deviation in the docstring and this reflection. The framing-prompt-as-recommendation-not-contract methodology pattern from S15-D50 / S16-D54 is now a fourth instance. The methodology candidate it points at: framing prompts that name specific values for assertions should be read with the same structural-honesty test as framing prompts that name specific commit boundaries or function signatures. Second, the api-as-stub-vs-api-as-implemented distinction (reflection prompt 1) is a methodology observation that S17a's reflection 3 partially surfaced and S17b's experience confirms; the pattern is a Patterns observed candidate if it recurs at P11. Third, the import-linter refinement at commit 5 (`ignore_imports` on each context's api.py → its own application/domain edges) is a concrete contract-level expression of D17's facade pattern that previous sessions had not surfaced. The contracts had been stricter than D17 actually warrants and this session is the first one whose work crossed a context boundary through the facade and exercised the stricter-than-D17 gap; the refinement is the right answer and the S17b experience is the case where the gap mattered. The methodology observation: when a D-entry's structural commitment (D17's facade pattern) is in tension with a mechanical-enforcement layer (the import-linter contracts), the first session whose work crosses the gap surfaces the tension and refines the enforcement; the discipline is to refine the contract to match the D-entry, not to weaken the D-entry to match the contract.

## Session 18 (Package P5)
roles: analyst, architect, engineer, technical writer
mode: build

- Produced: Eleven commits closing S18 (one strategic preservation commit + ten build-mode commits per the S15 package-close shape extended for the briefs/ convention introduced at b6a1bcc). `95ae730 docs(charter): preserve S18 brief at briefs/p5/s18` lands the strategic-mode preservation of the build session brief at `briefs/p5/s18.md` per the briefs/ convention; the brief stays at v1 for the duration of the build per the no-in-place-edits rule, with deviations captured in this session log entry's reflection. `03b478a feat(p5/s18): regression report domain, comparison, and render functions` lands the user-facing output of the eval harness in three logical pieces: domain value objects (`RegressionReport`, `CriterionDelta`, `AggregateMetrics` frozen dataclasses) at `contexts/evaluation/domain/regression_report.py`; `compare_runs` use case at `contexts/evaluation/application/regression_compare.py` joining criteria across revisions by criterion name (the human-stable identity) and reusing `cost_per_successful_task` for aggregate cost-per-task on each side; `render_text` and `render_json` at `contexts/evaluation/application/render_report.py` emitting markdown text and structured JSON with Decimal cost values rendered as strings to preserve monetary precision. Both refinements consistent with the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern from D54 / S15 / S16. `eb31860 feat(p5/s18): Langfuse polling-with-timeout in cost-query path` adds `wait_for_trace_availability` as a method on `LangfuseHTTPTraceQueryAdapter`, polling `get_trace` (tenant-filtered per D50) until the trace becomes queryable or a configurable timeout elapses. Replaces S17b's 8s force-flush-and-sleep with honest waits; tenant-filtered polling preserves D24's isolation discipline at the polling surface. Four unit tests against `httpx.MockTransport` cover immediate-hit, available-after-N-polls, never-available-timeout, and cross-tenant-as-unavailable paths. `fd76113 feat(p5/s18): CLI runner with eval run and eval report commands` lands the user-facing CLI surface as a new uv workspace member at `apps/cli/` with typer + OTel SDK + OTLP exporter deps. Two commands: `eval run` orchestrates replay → cost-aggregate → render report end-to-end with optional `--baseline-revision-id` for regression mode; `eval report` re-renders an existing comparison from stored rubric_applications without re-running replay. Composition wiring at `apps/cli/_runtime.py` (tenant resolution; per-tenant session factory; OTel TracerProvider for bare-script trace emission) and orchestration at `apps/cli/_eval.py` (async coroutines invoking application use cases). Root pyproject.toml gains `padhanam-cli` in dependencies and workspace members; apps/api/Dockerfile gains the COPY directive for `apps/cli/pyproject.toml` so the AST workspace-coverage parser test passes (third-instance precedent from S11/S12); compose.yaml repins padhanam-api:dev to the rebuilt image digest `sha256:95b42bbca129eedd85b1d4b67ec61af2b15a223bae5dc9d561fa2de7a7bc18ab`; Makefile gains `make eval-run` and `make eval-report` targets wrapping the container invocation pattern matching `make migrate`. Eleven new unit tests across argument parsing (typer's CliRunner) and runtime helpers. `5ad0523 test(p5/s18): end-to-end CLI through rendered report` lands the integration test at `tests/integration/evaluation/test_cli_e2e.py` exercising both eval commands end-to-end against tenant_a's data plane through the live LiteLLM gateway and Langfuse public API. Module-scoped fixture pre-populates rubric_applications via replay_and_score for both revisions; four tests invoke the CLI through `docker compose exec`: eval report text format, eval report JSON format, cross-tenant isolation through the CLI surface, and eval run single-revision (exercising the replay path through the CLI plus the D59 polling helper between replay and cost query). `3a4a4bf docs(p5/s18): record regression report shape as D58` and `5ac7a76 docs(p5/s18): record Langfuse asynchrony handling as D59` append the two D-entries with build-time refinements documented and six rejected alternatives weighed at each. `3d6dd9a docs(p5/s18): P5 archive at docs/archive/packages/p5.md` lands the P5 retrospective with all sections from the P3/P4 archive shape per D31 plus the delta-against-epic-note section per D43; the reconciliation addresses six numbered deltas (four-vs-three sessions; seven D-entries vs framing forecast of three; two mid-package strategic commits; three build-time deviations; confidence trajectory; no structural-promotion threshold trigger). `c8513b7 docs(p5/s18): log/packages.md P5 measured-outcomes paragraph` lands the measured-outcomes paragraph per D40 with seven industry-overlay metrics + three bet-native metric layers; reports change failure rate 0/4, reliability 4/4 = 100%, +80 tests over P4-close baseline, +1 import-linter contract, seven D-entries. `e85e42f docs(p5/s18): current-package.md transitions to between-packages state` transitions the active-P5 surface to between-packages state pointing at the P5 archive, with carryovers and deferred items refreshed including two new P5-era entries (multi-currency cost reporting; currency-suffix AST-enforcement candidate). This entry (`docs(p5/s18): session log entry`) closes S18.

- Decisions: D58 (Regression report shape — single-baseline comparison; per-criterion success-rate deltas joined across revisions by criterion name; aggregate-only cost-per-task; text and JSON output formats; build-time field-name and field-set refinements from the brief recorded per the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern; six rejected alternatives weighed including multi-baseline tracking, per-trace report, per-criterion cost on `CriterionDelta`, JSON-only output, text-only output, joining criteria by id rather than name; Kano: must-have). D59 (Langfuse trace-ingestion asynchrony — polling-with-timeout at the cost-query path; `wait_for_trace_availability` method on LangfuseHTTPTraceQueryAdapter polls `get_trace` (tenant-filtered) until the trace becomes queryable or the timeout elapses; replaces S17b's 8s force-flush-and-sleep; six rejected alternatives weighed including sync wait at fixed duration, two-phase rendering with cost-pending sections, webhook/push from Langfuse, polling without tenant filter, polling at the application layer rather than the adapter, port-promotion to TraceQueryPort; corrective discipline-addition lands on first observation per the methodology's Failure-modes asymmetry; Kano: must-have).

- Tests: `uv run pytest` → 235 passed, 7 skipped (S17b baseline 207+7 → S18 close 235+7; +28 new tests across the session — 6 regression_compare unit + 3 render_report unit + 4 polling-with-timeout adapter + 11 CLI unit (6 main + 5 runtime) + 4 CLI e2e). Skip count unchanged. `uv run lint-imports` → 16 contracts kept, 0 broken (count unchanged from S17b close; the new apps/cli workspace member inherits the existing apps-confined contracts without requiring new ones). `make scan` not run during S18 (no changes to image content beyond Dockerfile COPY; deferred to next session that touches container surface). `make migrate` not invoked (no schema changes in S18). End-to-end against tenant_a: eval report (text format) renders markdown regression report with per-criterion table for `exact_match_check` and `answer_quality`; eval report (JSON format) parses to RegressionReport-shaped dict with Decimal cost values as strings; cross-tenant isolation under tenant_b returns aggregate metrics with zero baseline and zero candidate applications and an empty per_criterion_deltas list; eval run single-revision exercises the replay path plus the D59 polling helper and emits a cost-summary JSON with non-empty successful_count and Decimal-as-string cost fields. Browser interactive verification: not applicable at S18 — the harness is CLI-shaped per D53; the CLI's own help and command output is the user surface and is verified through the typer.testing.CliRunner unit tests and the e2e integration tests. Image rebuilt once during the session (apps/cli workspace member addition) and re-pinned at `sha256:95b42bbca129eedd85b1d4b67ec61af2b15a223bae5dc9d561fa2de7a7bc18ab`.

- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (regression report shape, D58) — Dual-output text and JSON worked cleanly with no fidelity loss. Markdown table renders the per-criterion deltas as a paste-ready surface for PR descriptions or Slack messages; JSON preserves Decimal monetary precision through string serialisation, which the P11 recommendation engine can rehydrate without ambiguity. The single-baseline-only commitment held throughout the build — at no point did the integration test or the CLI surface pull toward multi-baseline tracking. The structural-honesty refinements at the brief boundary (revision-id over run-id; aggregate-only cost on AggregateMetrics) actually clarified the design rather than complicating it: removing `baseline_cost_per_task` and `candidate_cost_per_task` from `CriterionDelta` collapsed the delta dataclass from eight fields to six, and the aggregate-level cost is the metric the recommendation engine actually consumes. The fourth instance of the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern earns explicit naming in D58's reasoning section as a methodology pattern; if a fifth instance lands at a future package boundary, the pattern is a Patterns-observed candidate at the next phase audit.

  Reflection prompt 2 (Langfuse asynchrony handling, D59) — Polling-with-timeout worked cleanly in the CLI surface. The integration test's eval run single-revision case exercised the helper through the full replay → poll → cost-query flow with no hangs and no false-failures; the 60-second test timeout set on `--poll-timeout-seconds` was generous (the actual ingestion time observed during the test run was well under 30s, the default). The 30s default timeout reads as right for CLI flows on the dev path; the S17b-S18 development arc had no observation of Langfuse ingestion exceeding 30s, but the conservative posture allows for variable-load conditions the dev path doesn't reproduce. The integration test caught the asynchrony behavior the helper is designed to address: without the polling, the cost query would have raced the ingestion and returned empty cost data on a fraction of runs depending on Langfuse worker scheduling. Tenant-filtered polling — the deliberate choice to use `get_trace` (with tenant.id verification) rather than the lower-level `_fetch_trace` — held the architectural protection against cross-tenant existence-side-channel leakage; the four unit tests for the polling helper include the cross-tenant-as-unavailable case verifying this discipline. The asymmetric structural-promotion-threshold treatment (corrective discipline-additions land on first observation, descriptive Patterns-observed entries wait for recurrence) lands honestly here — D59 is a corrective entry for an observed failure mode, not a forecast.

  Reflection prompt 3 (CLI runner shape) — The minimal `padhanam eval run` + `padhanam eval report` surface was sufficient for S18's user-facing scope and produced a clean two-command shape. The build did not surface a need for additional commands (sheet management, interaction-set management, run history). Two architectural observations worth recording for future CLI scope at P10/P11 or a dedicated CLI-extension package. (a) The CLI established the bare-script driver OTel-init pattern as a third instance — `apps/api/main.py`'s `_configure_tracing`, the e2e test scripts' inline `TracerProvider` setup, and now `apps/cli/_runtime.py`'s `init_tracing` helper. The third-instance threshold is met for promoting the pattern to a shared helper at `padhanam/observability/init_tracing.py`; the lift defers to the next consumer (P11 recommendation engine background workers or the P6 source-ingestion CLI) to avoid creating a helper without a fourth caller justifying it. (b) The CLI's tenant resolution at `apps/cli/_runtime.py` is honest about being Phase 1 dev shape — short labels 'a'/'b' resolve to the test-set UUIDs through a hardcoded mapping. Production CLI wiring (Phase 2) replaces this with a registry-driven path mirroring apps/api/main.py's composition. The dev-vs-production asymmetry is appropriate at P5 close; the structural shape of the CLI is unchanged, only the resolution mechanism. Establishing the apps/cli/ workspace member as the home for operator commands is the precedent that future CLI extensions inherit (P6 source ingestion may add `padhanam ingest run`; P10 audit log viewer may add `padhanam audit show`; the workspace member grows by adding subcommand modules under apps/cli/, not by creating new workspace members per command).

  Reflection prompt 4 (P5 archive reconciliation depth) — The reconciliation section in `docs/archive/packages/p5.md` reads as honest accounting against the v1 epic note's three-session forecast and minimum-three-D-entries forecast. Six numbered deltas: (1) four sessions instead of three, with the split forecast at S17a's current-package.md update per the append-only-at-version-level discipline; (2) seven P5-era D-entries (D53-D59) versus the framing forecast of three beyond D53; (3) two mid-package strategic commits (b6a1bcc briefs/ convention, 24561c9 USD-only explicit) — neither anticipated at framing; (4) three build-time deviations from framing recommendations (D54 return type, S17b `>= 0` assertion, S18 field-name and field-set refinements); (5) confidence trajectory 2 → 3 across all four sessions; (6) no structural-promotion threshold trigger from the recurring commit-shape pattern. Two deltas surfaced during S18 that the brief's inventory anticipated correctly: the brief named the build-time refinements as the third deviation class explicitly, and the S18 field-name/field-set refinements landed as the third instance. One delta the brief's inventory did not name explicitly but the archive captures: the bare-script driver OTel-init pattern recurred at three sessions across P5 (S17a, S17b, S18), meeting the third-instance promotion threshold but deferring the lift to the next consumer per the discipline that helpers without callers are paper architecture. The archive section captures it under "What this package leaves to P6 and beyond" rather than as a delta, which reads as the right placement — it is forward-looking commitment, not retrospective accounting. The honest accounting framing held; no skim, no absorbed gaps.

  Reflection prompt 5 (what P5 demonstrated for the bet's load-bearing claims) — P5 closes the substrate for the bet's optimization-recommendation differentiator (per phase-1-prd.md's articulation that observability with cost dimension produces optimization recommendations enterprise procurement reads as defensible). What's now possible at P5 close that wasn't possible at P4 close: cost-per-successful-task is computable from rubric_applications joined to trace cost data via the two-layer abstraction (D57); rubric_applications carry the calibration substrate fields (`automated_score`, `human_score`, `reviewed_by_user_id`, `confirmed_at`) per D55's Reading-C posture; the regression report shape (D58) is the structured surface the P11 recommendation engine reads as evidence; the polling-with-timeout discipline (D59) is the architectural answer to vendor async-ingestion, set as the standing pattern for future cross-context observability consumers. The cost-query two-layer abstraction (D57) sets the structural precedent every P11 cross-context call inherits — the recommendation engine defines its own ports and adapters, the adapters call observability (or evaluation) api facades. What's still missing for P11 to consume: per-criterion cost breakdowns (the recommendation engine's per-criterion guidance justifies the extra query work; deferred per D58 and the P5 out-of-scope); calibration learning loops over `automated_score` vs `human_score` (data substrate exists per D55; the loop logic lands at P11); multi-baseline regression reports with run-history (P9 builds the run-history substrate; P11 consumes it for trend-aware recommendations); trace_id-based recommendation queries beyond cost-per-task (the recommendation surface reads `gen_ai.usage.*` and other span attributes through the same `TraceQueryPort` shape; the access-pattern methods land at P11's framing per D56's interface-segregation posture). The Phase 1 close audit will read this reflection alongside the P5 archive's "What this package leaves to P6 and beyond" section as the input to whether the optimization-recommendation differentiator is defensible at Phase 2 framing. The case study posture holds: the cost dimension is wired through to a working metric, the regression report renders, the substrate that the bet promises is real and exercisable. The bet's load-bearing claims for the differentiator are narrower in evidence than they will be at P11 close, but the substrate is no longer paper — that gap closing is the work the package was for.

- methodology: Three observations worth surfacing at the next strategic conversation. First, the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern earns explicit naming at S18 as a P5-recurring methodology pattern: framing prompts that name specifics (commit boundaries, function signatures, field names, field placements, assertion values) are recommendations subject to the structural-honesty test at build, with deviations documented in the relevant D-entry text and session-log reflection per the briefs/ convention's no-in-place-edits rule. Four instances across P5: S15-D50 commit-shape granularity, S16-D54 return type, S17b `>= 0` assertion, S18 field-name and field-set refinements. The methodology candidate is to surface this as a Patterns-observed entry at the next phase audit if a fifth instance lands. Second, the bare-script driver OTel-init pattern's third-instance threshold met without lift — the discipline of "third instance is the promotion threshold" applies asymmetrically here because the lift requires a fourth consumer to justify a shared helper without paper-architecture cost; the methodology document accommodates this through the fourth-instance trigger for promotion candidates that have the third-instance recurrence but lack a clear consumer. Worth recording as a refinement to the structural-promotion-threshold convention itself: comment-level rules promoted on third instance; helper modules promoted on fourth instance with a confirmed consumer. Third, the briefs/ convention has now validated through two reconciliation cases (S17b's `>= 0` adjustment, S18's field-name refinement) and continues to hold the no-in-place-edits rule. The convention earns its place in the methodology document and is no longer mid-package experimental; phase audits should review the briefs/ artifacts alongside session-log entries as the bridge between intent and outcome.

## P6-open strategic block
roles: analyst, PM, architect, technical writer
mode: strategic

- Produced: One charter commit landing the P6 epic note at `charter/packages/p6-epic.md` (v1 draft preserved per D43, reconciliation deferred to package archive at P6 close), D60 in `charter/decisions.md` capturing the framing-level commitments (asynchronous pipeline shape, single `contexts/ingestion/` bounded context, two-track coordination across pgvector and Neo4j with topology TBD per deferred entry, CLI surface at `apps/cli/`), a new section in `charter/deferred-decisions.md` for per-tenant topology for Neo4j, and a `charter/current-package.md` transition from between-packages state to active-P6 state with three-to-four-session forecast S19-S21/S22.

- Decisions: D60 (P6 source-ingestion framing). Per-tenant Neo4j topology lands as new section in `deferred-decisions.md`, activating at the session that first writes to Neo4j.

- Tests: None. Documentation-only changes.

- Reflection: Two architectural pullbacks during framing kept the commitments narrow. First, the within-tenant segmentation primitive (use case, business unit, tagged overlap) was scoped out entirely after exploring a three-layer scope model (platform / tenant-wide / segment) in conversation. The model was coherent but uncommitted because no real consumer at Phase 1 drives the choice; pre-committing schema room would have been announcement-level adoption rather than validated architecture. Second, Neo4j topology choice (per-tenant containers vs shared with property scoping) was scoped out of framing after surfacing that no Phase 1 evidence pressure-tests either choice. Both pullbacks match the methodology's commitment-needs-evidence posture, and both were operator-driven challenges rather than self-correction during framing — worth surfacing because the framing conversation's value emerged from the pullbacks, not from the recommendations they pushed back against. The framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern (observed four times across P5) extends to framing-altitude decisions: framing names options and the strongest recommendation, the implementing session commits with whatever refinements the implementation surfaces. P6 framing therefore commits to the architectural floor (asynchronous pipeline, one bounded context, tenant isolation non-negotiable) and explicitly leaves Neo4j topology and within-tenant segmentation to be settled by sessions with evidence in hand.

- Decisions: D61 (Parsing scope at P6 close — markdown and plain text at S19; PDF, DOCX, HTML defer; markdown-it-py 4.0.0 chosen for the token-based AST that exposes source-line ranges via the `map` attribute, allowing boundary-aware source slicing rather than re-emit-from-tokens which would lose round-trip fidelity for atx vs setext headings, fenced vs indented code, and other operator-intentional variations; six rejected alternatives weighed including markdown-only-at-S19, PDF at S19, HTML at S19, token-count chunking, Python-Markdown over markdown-it-py, mistune over markdown-it-py; Kano: must-have on the parser-as-port shape, performance on specific format coverage). End-to-end against tenant_a: `padhanam ingest run /tmp/smoke.md --tenant-id a` returns a source id, `padhanam ingest worker --tenant-id a --max-iterations 3` claims and parses the source within ~5ms, sources row state transitions to `parsed`, chunks table carries 3 rows with structural_metadata `{heading_text, heading_level}` for markdown and `{paragraph_index}` for plain text. Image rebuilt three times during the session (commits 3, 6, 7) and re-pinned at `sha256:a1b3cced24741b97edf85860ece19d42ca8430dba942a8e5c6793dc248b47961`.

- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (parser-as-port shape) — The Parser Protocol held cleanly under both adapters with no leaky abstractions back into the use case. ParsedContent/ParsedChunk as the boundary type let MarkdownParser emit `{heading_text, heading_level}` and PlainTextParser emit `{paragraph_index}` through the same `Mapping[str, object]` shape, and parse_source treated both uniformly. Two architectural observations worth recording for the PDF and DOCX parsers when they land. (a) The structural_metadata shape is parser-defined and not validated by the use case; this is intentional (D61's open-ended Mapping leaves room for `{page_number, page_text}` from PDF or `{section_name, paragraph_in_section}` from DOCX without a domain-type extension), but downstream consumers (S22 retrieval, P11 recommendation) will need a discovery mechanism that does not depend on parser-specific knowledge. The likely shape is a structural_metadata schema versioning field alongside the chunk metadata; landing it pre-emptively at S19 would be paper architecture (only two parsers exist), but the discovery question is worth surfacing at S22 framing when retrieval becomes a real consumer. (b) ParserError as a single error class works for S19's two parsers because both failure modes are "content is structurally invalid for the format"; PDF's failure modes (encrypted pages, malformed cross-references, OCR failures on scanned pages) may need richer signal that distinguishes "we cannot parse this format at all" from "we parsed most of it but lost N pages". The ParserError signature does not foreclose this — the caller catches and writes parsing_error_text — but parsers may want a richer return shape (success-with-warnings) that needs framing at the PDF session.

  Reflection prompt 2 (SKIP LOCKED concurrency) — The SKIP LOCKED pattern survived under the threaded concurrent-workers test cleanly. Two `padhanam ingest worker` invocations launched ~50ms apart against a single registered source produced exactly one claim-and-process cycle and one immediate empty-claim return; the integration test asserts `sorted(processed_each) == [0, 1]`. The within-transaction state transition (the adapter does the SELECT FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED + UPDATE state='parsing' inside `session.begin()`) prevents the race-then-re-poll path that would have produced double-processing without it. One forward-affordance worth recording: the test exercises two workers in the same Python process via threads, which is enough to prove the asyncpg + SQLAlchemy 2.0 path delivers the locking guarantee, but does not exercise the multi-process or multi-machine pattern that production would have. The Postgres lock is held at the connection level and SKIP LOCKED is a server-side primitive, so the multi-process case is structurally identical, but the next session that adds horizontal worker scaling (production deployment context) should rerun the contention shape with two separate processes to confirm the locking contract holds across connection pools.

  Reflection prompt 3 (worker reentrancy and idempotency) — The reentrancy model needed no refinements during build for the success and cross-tenant cases. The idempotency case the brief named (re-running against an already-parsed source is a no-op) holds because the SKIP LOCKED query filters on `state = 'received'` and parsed sources are excluded; the test confirms `processed 0 source(s)` on re-run. The recovery story for a worker that died with state in `parsing` (the brief's specific concern) is the gap S19 leaves open: the current adapter has no recovery query that re-claims `parsing` rows after a worker process dies. The schema supports the recovery path (state CHECK includes `parsing`; UNIQUE(source_id, chunk_index) prevents duplicate writes on re-claim), but no use case exercises it. Two options surfaced at build for the recovery shape: (a) a separate `claim_stale_parsing_rows(stale_after=...)` method on the repository that finds rows in `parsing` whose updated_at is older than the threshold and re-claims them; (b) a heartbeat column on the source row that a long-running parse updates periodically, with the recovery query keying off heartbeat staleness rather than updated_at. Option (a) is simpler and likely sufficient at Phase 1 scale; option (b) is the production-ready shape. Neither lands at S19 because the failure mode it addresses (worker process dies mid-parse) does not yet have an observed instance, and the structural-promotion-threshold convention names the trigger for the recovery use case as "first observed worker mid-parse death" or "first session that cares about worker uptime guarantees" — both defer to production deployment context. Recorded as carryover in the active-P6 carryover list.

  Reflection prompt 4 (OTel helper promotion at fourth-caller threshold) — The lift was the right call and exposed a small follow-on issue worth recording. The lift mechanically removed the inline TracerProvider/OTLPSpanExporter/BatchSpanProcessor + set_tracer_provider stanza from four call sites, replaced each with `init_tracing("service-name")`, and shrank net code by ~80 lines while removing four ObservabilitySettings constructions and four exporter constructions. The follow-on: the lift dropped the `_provider = ...` local binding the e2e setup scripts later used for explicit `force_flush(timeout_millis=...)` calls. The integration tests caught it on first run (NameError on `_provider`); the fix reads the helper's return value into `_provider`, restoring the binding. Worth surfacing as a methodology observation: when lifting a multi-line block to a single helper call, the lift's mechanical correctness is verified by the import-linter and AST tests, but local-variable bindings the lifted block produced are brittle to the migration. The fix is one line per script (`_provider = init_tracing(...)`) and the helper's return-value contract was written for exactly this case, but the failure mode is silent at unit-test collection time and only surfaces when the e2e scripts execute as `python -c "..."` inside the container. The discipline candidate: when the lift target produces a return value, the migration touch-points should preserve any local binding by name unless explicitly named for removal, and integration tests that exercise the scripts should run as part of the lift commit's verification rather than after-the-fact (which is what happened: the fix landed in a separate commit because the failure surfaced after commit 7's full-pytest run). The AST enforcement test at `test_tracer_provider_setup.py` is the correct shape for fencing future drift — future bare-script drivers must import the helper rather than re-introducing inline setup, and the test catches drift at the syntax-tree level before lint-imports' allow_indirect_imports = false would.

  Reflection prompt 5 (forward-affordance for S20) — Three constraints S19 leaves on the embedding adapter and chunk-embedding-table additions, with the cost recorded. (a) The chunks table at S19 does not carry an embedding column; S20's migration adds it alongside the embedding adapter per the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern (the column name and vector dimension settle at S20 framing per D60's open-question list and per D32's per-tenant-Postgres + pgvector commitment). The cost: zero — the brief explicitly named this and the data layer's UNIQUE(source_id, chunk_index) constraint plus the chunks domain object's open-ended structural_metadata Mapping leaves room for the embedding column without schema churn. (b) The sources.state CHECK constraint pins to four values at S19 (`received`, `parsing`, `parsed`, `failed`); S20's embedding stage extends the CHECK to add `embedding` and likely `embedded` (or a richer name when S20 framing settles). The migration shape is straightforward (drop CHECK, recreate with extended set; SQLAlchemy server-default does not need to change since `received` stays the entry value). The cost: the SourceState StrEnum and the per-tenant migration must extend together, and the S19 unit test `test_source_state_values_match_schema_check_constraint` is the structural backstop that fails loudly if the two diverge. (c) The parse-source use case writes chunks atomically with the state transition to `parsed`; the embedding stage at S20 will need its own analogous use case (`embed_source` or similar) that claims `parsed` rows, embeds chunks, writes embedding rows, transitions state to `embedded`. The worker loop at `apps/cli/_ingest.py` will need to gain a stage-aware claim that takes the target state as a parameter, or the embedding worker will be a separate command (`padhanam ingest embed`) that mirrors the parse worker's shape. Either is honest; the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern means S20 will name the option and commit at build. The cost: the worker-loop generalisation is the riskiest of the three because the current loop has SKIP LOCKED filter `state = 'received'` baked into the adapter method name (`claim_pending_for_parse`), and a pattern-extension to multiple stages will either (i) rename the method to take a state parameter (`claim_pending_in_state`) or (ii) add per-stage methods (`claim_pending_for_embed`). Option (i) is more flexible; option (ii) reads more clearly. The choice settles at S20 framing.

- methodology: Three observations worth surfacing at the next strategic conversation. First, the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern continues from P5's four instances: S19 alone produced three build-time refinements of the brief — markdown-it-py >=3.0 → 4.0.0 (lockfile resolution; mechanical), parser_dispatch responsibility split (caught by lint-imports during build; structural-honesty refinement), and compose worker services → make-target (architectural-cost trade-off documented in commit 6's body). Adding three S19 instances to the four P5 instances brings the running count to seven; this is well beyond the structural-promotion threshold and the pattern earns a Patterns-observed entry at the next phase audit. The methodology candidate also gains a sub-observation: build-time refinements fall into three classes — mechanical (version pinning, library version drift), structural-honesty (the lift caught the layering violation; lint-imports is the enforcement), and architectural-cost (compose vs make-target, where the cost difference is real but the brief's named option remains sound; the build chooses the cheaper option with reasoning, the brief stays valid as the more-comprehensive shape). The three-class taxonomy may be worth surfacing in the methodology document at the next phase audit. Second, the third-instance image-rebuild-and-re-pin pattern from S15/S18 met its fourth and fifth instance at S19 (commits 3, 6, 7 each rebuilt and re-pinned). The pattern is "code change touches container content (migration file, new context, or adapter that needs vendor deps); rebuild image, re-pin compose.yaml digest, restart stack." The fourth-instance threshold for promotion to mechanical enforcement applies asymmetrically here (the pattern is operator-discipline, not source-of-truth-shape) but a `make rebuild-and-pin` target would compress the three-step ritual into a single operator command. Recorded as a deferred-decisions candidate at the next phase audit. Third, the AST-enforcement-test-from-the-promotion convention held: lifting the OTel helper produced a fence test at `test_tracer_provider_setup.py`, the same shape S10's plaintext-in-state AST test, S11's host-port-binding parser test, and the S12 Dockerfile-workspace-coverage test follow. The convention is "promote a helper or convention; in the same session, land the AST/parser test that fences future drift." Five instances now (counting the three predecessors from P3, the lift at S18 was the third-instance-without-lift case, and the S19 lift completes the cycle); this is methodology-document-material at the next strategic conversation per the established Patterns-observed shape.

## P6 mid-package strategic commit — external reference absorption
roles: analyst, PM, architect, technical writer
mode: strategic

- Produced: One charter commit landing three artefacts: a methodology discipline entry "Start simple; refactor at the structural-promotion threshold" in `charter/methodology.md`, a deferred-decisions entry "Personalization as a runtime concern" in `charter/deferred-decisions.md`, and a captures entry for an enterprise multi-agent QA system case study in `charter/captures.md`. Two scheduled future strategic-mode commitments noted: data-retrieval design session after P6 closes (possible roadmap v4 insertion if scope warrants); hierarchical multi-agent topology design session paired with P8 framing or as pre-P8 strategic block.

- Decisions: None new. The methodology discipline references the existing structural-promotion threshold rather than introducing a new architectural commitment; the deferred-decisions entry forecasts a future D-entry rather than landing one.

- Tests: None. Documentation-only changes.

- Reflection: External-reference absorption tested as a charter surface for the first time at significant scope. The case study surfaced four observations worth landing as charter commitments and three worth queueing as future strategic-mode work. The shape of the absorption — small commit landing methodology and deferred-decisions content immediately, two design sessions queued for their natural triggers — kept the build-mode S19 work uninterrupted while preserving the strategic-mode value before context decayed in chat history. The operator's pullback on data retrieval ("Data retrieval needs its own session. It is not trivial. It is full design package or phase.") was the discipline-correct response: what was framed as three deferred-decision candidates was actually one design-session candidate (data retrieval), one design-session candidate (hierarchical topology), and two genuine deferred-decisions entries (memory, runtime reflection). The categorisation matters because deferred-decisions entries imply "the architecture commits in advance, the implementation lands later" while design-session candidates imply "the architecture itself needs designing." The two are different scopes and should not be conflated. Two further operator pullbacks during drafting kept the artefacts disciplined: dropping named attributions (company, product, speakers) from charter content because external-reference value lives in the architectural observation not the brand association; and dropping specific architectural-stage enumerations from the methodology and captures entries because Padhanam's architecture may end up looking different from the reference and pre-anchoring on the reference's specific choices imports their playbook into our charter. Worth carrying as a discipline observation at next phase audit: external-reference observations need triage by category (methodology / deferred-decisions / design-session / package candidate / phase candidate) and by abstraction level (observation versus implementation specifics) before they can land on the right surface.

- Produced: Six commits closing S20. `2642b7a docs(charter): land S20 framing decisions` lands D62 (Embedding model default and chunk-embedding column shape — nomic-embed-text:v1.5 via Ollama through LiteLLM's /v1/embeddings, single embedding column on chunks per per-tenant Alembic revision 0006, HNSW index using vector_cosine_ops with pgvector defaults m=16/ef_construction=64, SourceState enum extension for the embedding stage, default_embedding_model on InferenceSettings, pricing-table extension at zero rates per the D49 dev-cost precedent) plus charter/schema.md extension with the embedding column on chunks, the HNSW index, the SourceState extension to seven values, the embedding_error_text column on sources, and the vector store section populated. SourceState enum extends with the three new values; the test_source_state_values_match_schema_check_constraint pins the enum/CHECK parity. Source aggregate gains embedding_error_text defaulted to None to keep S19 callers unchanged. InferenceSettings extends with default_embedding_model; PRICING_TABLE extends with the embedding-row at zero rates (Ollama-hosted dev, honest per D49); LiteLLM gateway model_list extends with the embedding entry (model_info.mode=embedding). `make pull-model` now pulls both chat and embedding defaults. 12 new unit tests cover batch mapping, task-prefix application, span attributes (cost + tenant + operation name), the embedding_no_token_count fallback path, the unknown_model path, length-mismatch protocol violation, and the four exception-translation shapes. Image rebuilt to sha256:866a9d4d. 5 new unit tests for embed_source (success, embedder call shape, empty-chunks, EmbedderError → embedding_failed, EmbedderConfigurationError → embedding_failed). Integration tests extended: the S19 markdown/plain-text e2e tests now assert state ends at embedded with all chunks carrying 768-dim vectors via vector_dims; new full-pipeline e2e (test_register_parse_and_embed_markdown_end_to_end) asserts processed=2, state=embedded, embedded_count=total_count, vector_dims=768; new idempotency test (test_worker_idempotent_on_already_embedded_source) asserts re-running the worker against an already-embedded source claims no rows; the S19 concurrency test extends to assert the structural correctness invariants the SKIP LOCKED contract gates across both stages (sum of processed = 2; final state embedded; chunks=1; embedded_count=1; per-worker counts may distribute either way). `9cb26b3 feat(p6/s20): embedding cost-capture verification` lands the cost-attribution end-to-end shape: test_live_embed_call_emits_full_cost_attribution_shape drives LiteLLMChunkEmbedder against the live gateway in-container, captures the OTel span via in-process InMemorySpanExporter, asserts the seven D27/D49/D50 attributes plus the four cost attributes. - Tests: `uv run pytest` → 322 passed, 7 skipped (S19 baseline 294+7 → S20 close 322+7; +28 new tests across the session — 5 isolation contract additions on test_ingestion_isolation.py; 12 new ingestion unit tests on test_litellm_embedder.py; 5 new ingestion unit tests on test_embed_source.py; 1 SourceState CHECK-parity test update on test_source_domain.py; 3 new ingestion integration tests across test_ingest_e2e.py / test_concurrent_workers.py; 2 new ingestion integration cost-capture e2e tests on test_embedder_cost_capture_e2e.py; the existing test_register_and_parse_markdown / test_register_and_parse_plain_text were renamed to test_register_parse_and_embed_* with assertions updated to reflect the worker's new two-stage pipeline behaviour; the existing test_two_concurrent_workers_claim_one_source_each was renamed to test_two_concurrent_workers_no_double_processing_full_pipeline). - Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (LiteLLM embedding route cleanliness) — The route worked cleanly with no S6-style vendor-quirk friction. The model_list syntax addition (model: ollama/nomic-embed-text:v1.5 + model_info.mode: embedding) is exactly what LiteLLM's docs imply; the gateway routed the /v1/embeddings call to Ollama's /api/embeddings without any env-var quirks beyond the standard LITELLM_MASTER_KEY surface. The litellm.aembedding() Python SDK call mirrored litellm.completion() symmetrically — same openai/ prefix discipline, same api_base + api_key parameters, same exception class hierarchy (Timeout/RateLimit/Authentication/BadRequest), same OTel context propagation. The S6 lesson (verify the gateway path against vendor docs because LiteLLM/Ollama integration has historically had quirks) held its discipline value here precisely because the verification surfaced no friction; the lesson's worth is the verification, not the assumption that friction will land.

  Reflection prompt 2 (token-count attribution) — The S20 pre-write reconciliation flagged this as the highest-risk integration point: Ollama's /api/embeddings response server-side omits a usage block, so LiteLLM would either compute via local tokenizer or leave the attribute unset. The actual integration verified (in-container against the live gateway with hello-world input): gen_ai.usage.input_tokens=7 for "search_document: hi" (the prefix + content tokenises cleanly), pricing_status=table_hit, gen_ai.cost.* attributes all emit at zero (Ollama-hosted dev model at zero rates per D62 / D49 precedent). LiteLLM's local tokenizer is doing the work transparently. The defensive embedding_no_token_count fallback path remains in the adapter and is exercised by the unit test test_embedder_emits_embedding_no_token_count_when_usage_absent for cases where a future LiteLLM update or a different embedding-model path returns a sparser usage block. No bridging logic landed at S20; the cost-attribution architecture per D41 holds end-to-end for the embedding path with no new surface.

  Reflection prompt 3 (pgvector HNSW + cosine choice under real query plan) — The choice held under EXPLAIN. The query `SET enable_seqscan = off; EXPLAIN SELECT ... ORDER BY embedding <=> ... LIMIT 5` produced `Index Scan using chunks_embedding_hnsw_idx on chunks` with the HNSW-defined cost estimates (14.59..59.00) much lower than the seq-scan cost (10000000000.00..). The disable-seqscan flag is the standard pgvector-tuning probe (the planner can prefer seq scan at small corpus sizes because the HNSW index has a startup cost; turning seq scan off forces the index path to verify it's available); at production scale the default planner picks the index naturally. No m / ef_construction / ef_search tuning surfaced as needed at S20's three-row corpus; the pgvector README's recommendation (defaults are appropriate at small scale, tune ef_search up if recall is low at production scale) lands as a forward-affordance for the first session that has real corpus pressure.

  Reflection prompt 4 (single-column-on-chunks shape under the worker) — The shape held cleanly. The worker writes embeddings via UPDATE on chunks.id (per the upsert_chunk_embeddings adapter method); idempotent re-embed collapses to UPDATE-replace rather than DELETE-INSERT. The 768-dim column on chunks adds ~3KB per row (768 * 4 bytes); at Phase 1 corpus sizes (single-tenant test set of dozens of sources, hundreds of chunks per source) this is operationally trivial. One operational observation worth recording for the schema design: the chunks table now mixes text-shaped data (content, structural_metadata) with binary-shaped vector data, which means SELECT * queries pull the embedding column even when the consumer doesn't need it. The structural-promotion threshold for splitting embedding into a side table is "first observed query that pays the embedding fetch cost without using it" — the parse stage's reads (which never need embeddings) and the chunks-isolation contract test (which queries column metadata, not data) don't trip this; the retrieval session at S22 will, but the cost matters only if the retrieval queries are SELECT *. Recorded as a watch-item, not a forward-affordance: the migration to a separate chunk_embeddings table is cheap if and when needed, and committing now would be paper architecture against the absent multi-model A/B testing consumer D62 names.

  Reflection prompt 5 (forward-affordance for S21 graph extraction and Neo4j topology) — Three constraints S20 leaves on the graph extraction adapter and Neo4j topology decision, with the cost recorded. (a) The chunks table is now the natural attachment surface for graph-extracted entities and relationships; entity rows in Neo4j will reference chunks.id (or the (source_id, chunk_index) natural key), and the per-tenant Postgres-instance topology per D32 means the graph store needs the same per-tenant topology to keep the cross-store join tenant-clean. The cost is captured in the deferred-decisions entry "Per-tenant topology for Neo4j" which activates at S21 framing. (b) The SourceState enum's embedding-stage extension establishes the precedent for S21's extraction-stage extension: the worker loop at apps/cli/_ingest.py has the per-stage claim pattern (claim_pending_for_parse → claim_pending_for_embed → ...), and S21 will add claim_pending_for_extract following the same shape. The cost: the worker loop's iteration body grows by another stage; if the four-or-more-stage shape becomes load-bearing the generalised claim_pending_in_state(state) method becomes the right refactor. At three stages it stays per-stage. (c) The LiteLLM adapter pattern is now established for two operations (chat completion + embedding); a third operation (graph extraction via prompt-based entity/relationship extraction at S21) extends the pattern with a third adapter at contexts/ingestion/adapters/outbound/extraction/. The import-linter litellm-confined contract may need to extend to admit the new directory, or the contract reframes to "litellm confined to inference outbound + ingestion outbound" which is the simpler shape if S21's extraction adapter also lands under contexts/ingestion. Both are zero-cost at S21 framing; the choice surfaces at the implementing session per the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern.

- methodology: Three observations worth surfacing at the next strategic conversation. First, the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern continues with three S20 build-time refinements: (a) nomic-embed-text:v1.5 task-prefix requirement surfaced at pre-write reconciliation (architectural-cost class — adding the prefix prepend in the adapter is the operator-correctness fix, not a brief deviation); (b) per-stage claim methods over generalised claim_pending_in_state (architectural-cost class — chose readability over flexibility, recorded the trade-off in D62); (c) test_register_and_parse_markdown / test_register_and_parse_plain_text test renames to reflect the now-two-stage worker (mechanical class — same shape as S19's three classes). The running count is now ten instances across P5+P6 (four P5 + three S19 + three S20); well past the structural-promotion threshold and the pattern earns the Patterns-observed entry the S19 reflection forecasted. Second, the cost-attribution-pessimism-vs-actual-integration pattern: the S20 pre-write reconciliation flagged token attribution as "unverified — Ollama omits usage; LiteLLM may or may not bridge" and the actual integration revealed LiteLLM's local tokenizer transparently populates the count. Both the defensive fallback path (embedding_no_token_count) and the optimistic path (table_hit) ship in the adapter, which is the right shape regardless of which path is currently active — the defensive path is exercised by unit tests for future-tolerance against LiteLLM update drift. The methodology observation: pre-write reconciliation that surfaces a worst-case is worth implementing the defensive path for even when the verification later shows the worst case doesn't hold; the cost is one fallback attribute and one extra unit test, the value is graceful degradation if the integration regresses. Third, the live-end-to-end-smoke-as-acceptance-criterion convention from S19 held at S20: the integration test suite verified the full pipeline plus the cost-attribution shape, but the manually-invoked `padhanam ingest run` + `padhanam ingest worker` against a fresh source (with the EXPLAIN check on the HNSW index) is the user-perspective acceptance signal that the test suite cannot replicate (the test suite doesn't see the operator-visible processed-N-source log lines or the shape of the source-state transitions in the database). Per D60's CLI-surface commitment the live smoke is the closest analogue to browser interactive verification for a shell-shaped surface; the convention is "live smoke at session close for any session that ships an operator-facing CLI command."
