Makefile gains `make neo4j-up`, `neo4j-down`, `neo4j-reset`, `neo4j-shell`; help-text service count updates from 14 to 15. Image rebuilds to sha256:155f19df9dd6 to vendor the neo4j driver and migrations/. `b11924c feat(p6/s21): GraphRepository port and Neo4j adapter` lands the graph-store-as-port shape: `Entity` aggregate, `Relationship` value object plus `EntityRef` endpoint identifier, `GraphRepositoryPort` Protocol with `merge_entities` / `merge_relationships` / `get_entities_by_chunk_ids` / `get_relationships_by_chunk_ids` (build-time refinement of the brief's `get_*_for_source(source_id)` shape — Neo4j's queryable join is by chunk_id, the application orchestrates the Postgres source_id → chunk_ids lookup), two error categories (`GraphRepositoryError` retryable / `GraphRepositoryConfigurationError` non-retryable) parallel to the embedder port. `TenantScopedNeo4jSession` at `contexts/ingestion/adapters/outbound/neo4j/session.py` is the single Cypher-execution surface: constructed with a driver and a TenantContext, every Cypher template auto-binds `$tenant_id`, used as an async context manager, cross-tenant write attempts raise ValueError, relationship-type strings validated against `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$`, source-chunk provenance preserved across re-extraction additively. `Neo4jGraphRepository` adapter implements the port and translates driver exceptions into the port's two error categories. Two new import-linter contracts (`neo4j-confined` + `neo4j-confined-apps`) bring the kept count from 17 to 19. Together with the AST enforcement test at `tests/_enforcement/test_no_raw_neo4j_session.py` they make property-based tenant scoping defensible at audit per the user's risk-3 guidance: missing-predicate Cypher cannot exist in callable code because every Cypher path goes through the wrapper that auto-binds the tenant_id predicate. Three tenant-isolation contract tests at `tests/contract/tenant_isolation/test_neo4j_isolation.py` extend the harness from D24: write isolation, read isolation, schema invariant. Nine wrapper unit tests cover the bound-tenant-id binding, cross-tenant ValueError, relationship-type validation, no-op-on-empty-input, read return-shape conversion, and the explicit-context-manager-required RuntimeError. Image rebuilds to sha256:4fc764b0d083. `d447c3e feat(p6/s21): EntityExtractor port and LiteLLM adapter` lands the extractor-as-port shape: `ExtractionResult` value object, `EntityExtractorPort` Protocol with retryable / non-retryable error categories, `LiteLLMEntityExtractor` adapter using `response_format={"type": "json_object"}` against `qwen2.5:7b` via Ollama through LiteLLM keyed by per-chunk extraction (one model call per input chunk so each Entity carries the exact chunk it surfaced from). Build-time refinement of D64 per the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern: pre-write reconciliation revealed Qwen 2.5 7B via Ollama through LiteLLM tool-calling has poor fidelity (the model called the function but with malformed `{"text": "..."}` arguments — the Ollama tool-call JSON-schema binding does not robustly route through the LiteLLM bridge); JSON mode produced reliable schema-conformant output. The adapter uses JSON mode plus a versioned prompt template at `prompts/extraction_v1.txt`. Defensive parsing filters malformed entities and relationships whose endpoints are not in the extracted entity set, so adapter output is always consistent with the wrapper's MATCH-on-endpoints discipline. Trace propagation per D27 / D41 / D49 / D50 mirrors the chat (S15) and embedding (S20) shapes; cost lands as `table_hit` at zero rates per the D49 dev-cost precedent for `qwen2.5:7b`; the defensive `extraction_no_token_count` flag remains for future model-swap tolerance. `litellm-confined` contract extends to forbid litellm in `contexts.ingestion.adapters.outbound.neo4j` (closing a gap from commit 3). Eight unit tests at `test_litellm_extractor.py` cover the happy path, intra-chunk dedup, orphan-endpoint filtering, missing-name-or-type skip, no-op empty input, unparseable-JSON to ConfigurationError translation, Timeout to retryable Error translation, and per-chunk call-count invariant. `d303206 feat(p6/s21): extract-source worker stage and CLI integration` lands the worker-side surface: SourceState extends with EXTRACTING / INDEXED / EXTRACTION_FAILED; per-tenant Alembic revision `0007_extend_state_for_extraction` extends the CHECK constraint and adds `extraction_error_text` column. SourceRepositoryPort grows `claim_pending_for_extract` (per-stage method per the established D62 reasoning) and `update_source_state` grows the third error-text parameter. The Postgres adapter implements both. SourceState/CHECK parity test extends to ten values. `extract_source` application use case orchestrates load chunks → extract → merge entities → merge relationships → state transition to INDEXED on success or EXTRACTION_FAILED on any of the four port-level error types; entities are merged before relationships so the relationship MATCH-on-endpoints discipline holds; a partial entity write before relationship failure is fine because re-running the stage MERGE-deduplicates. Worker loop extends to drain three stages in pipeline order under a new `stages` filter (frozenset of {parse, embed, extract}); the CLI exposes the filter via `--stages parse,embed,extract` (default: all three). The flag was a build-time refinement — a structural-honesty addition that preserves existing parse-and-embed-shaped tests' scope without invoking the LLM-heavy extraction path. The existing concurrent-workers test scopes to `parse,embed` for runtime; the SKIP LOCKED extract-stage concurrency lands implicitly through the full-pipeline e2e test exercising the same claim-and-update transactional path. Six unit tests cover the success path plus the four port-level failure paths. This entry (`docs(p6/s21): session log entry`) closes S21.

- Decisions: D63 (Neo4j topology — shared instance with tenant-property scoping plus structural enforcement at three layers — property predicates, the TenantScopedNeo4jSession wrapper, and the tenant-isolation contract tests; explicit deviation from D32 with reasoning; ~1GB-RAM-per-tenant cost recorded as the production-deployment-revisit trigger). D64 (Graph extraction shape — Qwen 2.5 7B via LiteLLM structured output, single :Entity node label, free-form entity_type and relationship_type, composite-key uniqueness for both, MERGE for idempotent re-extraction; eight rejected alternatives weighed including specialised extraction models, distinct LLM for extraction, free-form prompting without function calling, fixed entity-type enum, opaque entity_id keys, multi-label nodes at S21, direct Postgres-to-Neo4j projection, and the structural posture of the prompt template). The D64 build-time refinement lands JSON mode over function-calling per the pre-write reconciliation; Qwen 2.5 7B via Ollama through LiteLLM tool-calling produced malformed function arguments while JSON mode produced reliable schema-conformant output. The deferred-decisions entry "Per-tenant topology for Neo4j" stays as the activation marker for production-deployment context, with framing language updated to reflect S21's Phase 1 choice and three concrete revisit triggers (residency, blast radius, security-review).

- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (LiteLLM structured-output fidelity) — Pre-write reconciliation surfaced a real fidelity gap and the build-time refinement honored it. The probe sent a strict JSON-shape extraction request to `qwen2.5:7b` through the gateway via two paths: tool-calling (`tools=[{...}]` + `tool_choice="extract_graph"`) and JSON mode (`response_format={"type": "json_object"}`). Tool-calling returned a tool_call where `arguments` was `{"text": "<original prompt>"}` — the model invoked the function but did not bind its arguments to the JSON schema. JSON mode returned a clean entities + relationships JSON object that parsed cleanly into the D64 schema. The pattern matches the S6 LiteLLM lesson (verify the gateway path against vendor docs because LiteLLM/Ollama integration has historically had quirks); D64's Choice text and the litellm_extractor module docstring both record the deviation. Decision-shape continuity: the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern lands its eleventh instance across P5+P6 (S6 + four P5 + S19 three + S20 three + S21 one — JSON mode over tool-calling is the structural-honesty class). Worth recording at the next phase audit as a decision-discipline note: pre-write reconciliation that probes LLM-vendor-quirks-class integrations is consistently load-bearing across S6 / S15 / S20 / S21.

  Reflection prompt 2 (property-based tenant isolation) — The pattern held cleanly under the contract tests and under the live full-pipeline test. Three structural reinforcements per the user's risk-3 guidance landed at architecturally appropriate layers: (a) property-based scoping at the data layer (every node and edge carries `tenant_id`), (b) the `TenantScopedNeo4jSession` wrapper at the routing layer (constructed with TenantContext, every Cypher template auto-binds `$tenant_id`, raw `neo4j` driver imports forbidden outside the wrapper), (c) the import-linter `neo4j-confined` contract plus AST enforcement test as the mechanical fence against drift. The contract tests verified all three layers under cross-tenant write (rejected at the wrapper boundary) and cross-tenant read (filtered by the tenant_id predicate). One edge case worth recording: the wrapper's relationship-type validation rejects non-Cypher-identifier strings before the format-substitution into the MERGE template — a defensive belt against future prompt-output drift even though backtick-quoting would tolerate arbitrary identifiers. The user's pre-build risk-3 guidance ("the cleanest architectural shape is a tenant-aware Neo4j session wrapper, mirroring how Postgres-side tenant scoping works") proved structurally correct — the wrapper plus the import-linter / AST fence is the load-bearing pattern, not the per-query operator-discipline of remembering the predicate. The structural enforcement is the thing that makes property-based scoping defensible at audit.

  Reflection prompt 3 (single-:Entity-node-type schema under the worker) — The schema held cleanly. The extraction prompt's free-form entity_type values ("Person", "Organisation", "Location", "Company") and free-form relationship_type values ("employs", "headquartered_in") are operationally sensible at the small corpus tested. No pressure to add additional node labels surfaced during build — the single-node-label-with-free-form-type-property shape carried the extracted graph cleanly without label proliferation. Forward-affordance for the data-retrieval design session: when the use case needs label-based subgraph projection (e.g. "give me all :Regulation nodes for this jurisdiction"), the architecture extends additively at the schema layer (a future migration can add labels to existing :Entity nodes via `MATCH (e:Entity {entity_type: 'Regulation'}) SET e:Regulation`) without restructuring the wrapper's auto-binding pattern.

  Reflection prompt 4 (forward-affordance for the data-retrieval design session) — The direct-to-Neo4j write pattern at S21 felt right against build evidence. Three observations: (a) provenance via `source_chunk_ids` on entities and `source_chunk_id` on relationships gives retrieval a clean join surface back to Postgres chunks (and therefore to source row state, jurisdiction, classification when those land); (b) the per-chunk extraction shape preserves provenance at the granularity the eval harness needs (a retrieval result citing "ACME Corp" can resolve back to the exact chunk the entity surfaced from); (c) MERGE-on-composite-key collapses repeated entities across chunks naturally — the source_chunk_ids array accumulates. The Postgres-as-source-of-truth projection pattern (where Postgres holds the canonical extraction result and Neo4j is materialised from it) was forecast in the brief's out-of-scope list and the build evidence does not push back against that scope choice. Cost recorded: if the data-retrieval design session decides Neo4j should be projection-only, the migration is a per-tenant Postgres `entities` + `relationships` schema addition plus a worker stage that re-projects to Neo4j on source-row writes; the entity uniqueness key `(tenant_id, name, entity_type)` would carry across cleanly to the Postgres side.

  Reflection prompt 5 (entity-type and relationship-type taxonomy operationally sensible) — The free-form values the extraction prompt produced were operationally sensible and consistent within the small sample. Taxonomy-drift risk: the prompt does not constrain the entity_type or relationship_type space, so different chunks could in principle produce "Organization" vs "Organisation" vs "Company" for the same conceptual class. At Phase 1 corpus sizes the drift is low and the MERGE-on-composite-key still produces correct uniqueness — an entity named "ACME Corp" with entity_type "Organisation" is distinct from "ACME Corp" with entity_type "Company", which is honest about the prompt's instability rather than papering over it. Watch-item recorded: if eval surfaces retrieval-quality problems traceable to taxonomy drift, the prompt extension that ships first is a small whitelist of preferred entity_type values with "use the closest match" framing, not a fixed enum. The conservative posture (free-form, no taxonomy commitment per D64) leaves room for that prompt-level refinement without schema churn.

  Reflection prompt 6 (forward-affordance for S22 / P6 close-shape) — Two constraints S21 leaves on the retrieval interface, with cost recorded. (a) The GraphRepositoryPort exposes only chunk-id-keyed reads per the build-time refinement of the brief's `_for_source(source_id)` shape; the retrieval interface at S22 / P6-close needs richer queries (subgraph traversal from a starting entity, path-finding between two entities, neighbourhood expansion). The current wrapper is structurally honest but the wrapper would need new methods like `traverse_neighbourhood(entity_ref, max_depth, tenant_context)` or `find_paths_between(source_ref, target_ref, max_depth, tenant_context)`. The cost is per-method: each new wrapper method gets a Cypher template with the auto-bound tenant predicate; zero structural churn. (b) Provenance back to chunks gives the retrieval interface a clean shape for cited-passage retrieval: a graph query returns entities, the entities carry source_chunk_ids, the application looks up those chunks from per-tenant Postgres for citation rendering. The two-hop lookup is unavoidable given D32's per-tenant Postgres + D63's shared-Neo4j topology; the cost is one Postgres query per cited chunk-id batch.

- methodology: Three observations worth surfacing at the next strategic conversation. First, the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern continues — the eleventh instance across P5+P6 lands at S21 commit 4 (JSON mode over tool-calling, structural-honesty class). Adding to this the build-time `get_*_for_source(source_id)` → `get_*_by_chunk_ids(chunk_ids)` port-shape refinement (architectural-cost class) and the worker `--stages` flag addition (architectural-cost class). Three S21 instances bring the running count to thirteen; well past the structural-promotion threshold, the pattern earns its Patterns-observed entry at the next phase audit. Second, the user-driven build-time course-correction pattern fired again at S21: the user's risk-3 guidance ("the cleanest architectural shape is a tenant-aware Neo4j session wrapper, mirroring how Postgres-side tenant scoping works") rerouted the brief's per-query predicate-discipline framing to the wrapper-plus-import-linter shape that lands as load-bearing in D63. This is the second observed instance after S20's per-stage claim methods. The pattern is "operator at framing time names options and the strongest recommendation; build session commits with refinements; on the most architecturally load-bearing choices, the operator's pre-build guidance is itself a course-correction not just a recommendation". Worth a methodology entry at the next phase audit as a related-but-distinct pattern from framing-prompt-as-recommendation. Third, the LiteLLM-vendor-quirks-class pre-write reconciliation pattern lands its fourth instance at S21 (S6 + S15 + S20 + S21). All four were load-bearing — the reconciliation surfaced concrete integration-shape information that materially changed the implementation. The discipline value compounds: for the next session that touches the LiteLLM gateway in any new operation mode, the pre-write reconciliation is non-negotiable. The methodology observation is that LiteLLM/Ollama integration has a higher-than-baseline quirk surface, sufficient that a session-template prompt for any LiteLLM-touching session should explicitly require pre-write reconciliation against the specific operation mode being added.

- Produced: Seven commits closing S22. `ea4750c docs(charter): land S22 framing decisions` lands D65 (RetrievalClient port surface — two methods, `search_vector` and `traverse_graph`, hybrid composition deferred to the agent layer per D5; cross-track readiness as a `state='indexed'` predicate at retrieval time on both adapters; ChunkEmbedder task-hint refinement with DOCUMENT and QUERY task values; build-time refinements absorbed in the D-entry's Choice text per the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern). charter/schema.md intro for source ingestion notes that S22 added retrieval methods over the existing schema without altering it. S22 brief preserved at briefs/p6/s22.md per the convention from S17b. `8c78d26 feat(p6/s22): RetrievalClient port and result types` lands the read-side surface: `ChunkResult` and `EntityResult` value objects (frozen dataclasses with __post_init__ tenant_id and jurisdiction non-empty validation per D50); `RetrievalClient` Protocol at `contexts/ingestion/ports/retrieval_client.py` with the two method signatures. Seven unit tests fence the value-object invariants. `00fe4ef feat(p6/s22): ChunkEmbedder task-hint refinement` lands the port-surface refinement: `EmbeddingTask` StrEnum with values `DOCUMENT` and `QUERY`; `ChunkEmbedderPort` grows two methods (build-time refinement of the brief's single-method shape per the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern) — `embed` for the corpus-side batch path the worker uses, `embed_query` for the query-side single-string path the retrieval adapter uses. The two-method shape avoided the synthetic-Chunk-as-query antipattern (Chunk carries provenance fields a query lacks); `LiteLLMChunkEmbedder` extends with task-prefix dispatch table (`search_document:` for DOCUMENT, `search_query:` for QUERY) and the new `embed_query` method. The S20 worker call site updates to pass `EmbeddingTask.DOCUMENT` explicitly per no-backwards-compat-shims discipline. Five new unit tests + existing tests updated. `c17c4b0 feat(p6/s22): pgvector vector retrieval adapter` lands `PgVectorSearch` at `contexts/ingestion/adapters/outbound/retrieval/pgvector_search.py` implementing `RetrievalClient.search_vector` against the per-tenant chunks table. Embeds the query with `EmbeddingTask.QUERY` so the `search_query:` prefix applies symmetrically to the corpus side; performs cosine-distance search via `<=>` against the HNSW index from S20 with `1.0 - distance` cosine-similarity coercion at the SQL boundary. Cross-track readiness via `s.state = 'indexed'` JOIN-and-WHERE; tenant scoping via WHERE on both `chunks.tenant_id` and `sources.tenant_id` (defence-in-depth alongside D32's per-tenant Postgres topology). Five unit tests for SQL parameter shape and embedder dispatch. `b892391 feat(p6/s22): neo4j graph retrieval adapter` lands `Neo4jTraverse` at `contexts/ingestion/adapters/outbound/retrieval/neo4j_traverse.py` implementing `traverse_graph` via the `TenantScopedNeo4jSession` wrapper. The wrapper extends with `traverse_from_seed` taking `(seed_name, depth, indexed_chunk_ids)` — depth-bound validation (non-negative, max 8 for Phase 1) and the variable-length path template (depth interpolated as a literal because Cypher does not parameterise path-length integers; the validation prevents arbitrary input from reaching format-substitution). Cross-track readiness via two-store query: the adapter pre-queries per-tenant Postgres for the set of indexed chunk_ids (`sources.state = 'indexed'`), passes the set as a Cypher parameter for the `ANY(cid IN reachable.source_chunk_ids WHERE cid IN $indexed_chunk_ids)` predicate. The retrieval adapter avoids importing the `neo4j` package directly per D63's "single Cypher-execution surface" commitment — driver typed as Any, all execution through the wrapper. AST enforcement test passes; import-linter contracts kept count stays at 19. Ten new unit tests (5 wrapper + 5 adapter). `bc5eb89 feat(p6/s22): retrieval CLI commands and end-to-end test` lands `padhanam ingest search` and `padhanam ingest traverse` subcommands at `apps/cli/_ingest.py` + `apps/cli/main.py`. New `make_async_driver(settings)` factory in the neo4j wrapper module so the retrieval adapter and CLI get a driver without importing `neo4j` directly. End-to-end test at `tests/integration/contexts/ingestion/test_retrieval_e2e.py` exercises six cases (search returns chunks for indexed source, traverse returns entities for indexed source, cross-tenant search returns zero results, cross-tenant traverse returns zero results, search excludes non-indexed sources, query-embedding cost-attribution span shape per D41/D49/D50 carries all eleven attributes including the new `padhanam.embedding.task` value). Tenant-isolation contract tests extend at `tests/contract/tenant_isolation/test_ingestion_isolation.py` (parametrized over both tenants, both retrieval methods). The S20 embedder cost-capture e2e updates to pass `EmbeddingTask.DOCUMENT` explicitly. Image rebuilt to `padhanam-api:dev@sha256:c8d3fadbb47d` and re-pinned. This entry (`docs(p6/s22): session log entry`) closes S22.

- Decisions: D65 (RetrievalClient port surface, cross-track readiness, and ChunkEmbedder task-hint refinement; eight rejected alternatives weighed including single `retrieve(query, scope, strategy)` method with strategy enum, three methods including separate `hybrid`, denormalised ready flags on chunks and entities, adapter-level task dispatch via separate methods on the embedder port, pgvector L2 / inner-product distance, source state denormalised onto Neo4j Entity nodes, worker-side projection of indexed_chunk_ids into a Neo4j label, and adding the task parameter with a DOCUMENT default; Kano: must-have on the port surface and readiness semantics, performance on the task-hint refinement). Three build-time refinements absorbed in D65 per the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern: (a) cross-store readiness query in `neo4j_traverse` is the structurally honest shape under D32 + D63 (Neo4j has no chunk-source linkage natively; the readiness predicate consumes a chunk-id set computed from per-tenant Postgres); (b) the CLI surface lands at S22 because the e2e test needs an operator-visible boundary to verify retrieval; (c) `ChunkEmbedderPort` grows two methods (`embed` and `embed_query`) rather than wrapping a query in a synthetic Chunk — Chunk's provenance fields are dishonest to fabricate for a query string.

- Tests: `uv run pytest` excluding the four slow LLM-driven e2e files → 376 passed, 7 skipped (S21 baseline 352+7 → S22 close 376+7 unfast; +24 new tests in the unfast subset — 7 retrieval-result value-object unit tests, 5 embedder QUERY-task and embed_query unit tests, 5 wrapper traverse_from_seed unit tests, 5 neo4j_traverse adapter unit tests, 5 pgvector_search adapter unit tests; minus 3 net from existing test refactor — `_FakeEmbedder` and `_RaisingEmbedder` extended for protocol conformance with the two-method port). The four slow integration files (test_retrieval_e2e: 6 tests, test_extract_e2e: 2 tests, test_extractor_cost_capture_e2e: 1 test, test_embedder_cost_capture_e2e: 2 tests) all pass when run; total close = 387 passed + 7 skipped. `uv run lint-imports` → 19 contracts kept, 0 broken (S21 baseline 19 → S22 close 19; no new contracts — the retrieval adapter directory does not need its own confined contract because it consumes the existing wrapper rather than introducing a new vendor surface). `make migrate` runs idempotently; no new migrations at S22. AST enforcement tests pass — the retrieval adapter imports neo4j only via the wrapper, no raw `neo4j` driver import outside `contexts.ingestion.adapters.outbound.neo4j` and `ops.migrate_neo4j`. End-to-end live verified: empty-tenant `padhanam ingest search "anything" --tenant-id a --limit 5` returned `(no results)`; empty-tenant `padhanam ingest traverse "anything" --tenant-id a --depth 2` returned `(no results)`; full pipeline verified through the test_retrieval_e2e suite (~10 minutes for the suite — full ingestion pipeline plus query-embedding plus retrieval per test). EXPLAIN check: against an empty corpus with `enable_seqscan=off`, the planner picks `ix_sources_tenant_state` first (index on the small sources table) and joins via `chunks_source_chunk_index_unique`; once the chunks table is populated the HNSW index `chunks_embedding_hnsw_idx` becomes the primary ordering path (verified at S20 close per the same tuning probe). Browser interactive verification: not applicable at S22 — the retrieval CLI is shell-shaped per D60's CLI-surface commitment; the operator-visible signal is the printed result list and source-state filter behaviour, verified through the e2e test suite plus the live empty-tenant smoke. Image rebuilt once during the session (commit 6) and re-pinned at `sha256:c8d3fadbb47d0fd92a7d7a53d9d2918506e53fd0a0e4bab1cfaeb8693099280b`.

- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (per-source state filter cleanliness) — The filter held cleanly under both adapters with materially different shapes that surfaced the cross-store discipline at the right altitude. The pgvector adapter joins `chunks` to `sources` and filters `s.state = 'indexed'` in the WHERE clause; the join cost is negligible because sources is a small table and the planner picks `ix_sources_tenant_state` against the indexed-state filter cleanly. The Neo4j adapter cannot join across stores (Neo4j has no view of sources), so the readiness filter lands as a Cypher predicate against a chunk-id set computed by a pre-query against per-tenant Postgres — `ANY(cid IN reachable.source_chunk_ids WHERE cid IN $indexed_chunk_ids)`. The two-store call adds one Postgres roundtrip per traverse but matches the architectural shape (Postgres is source-of-truth for source state per D60; Neo4j is derivative). Build-time discovery worth recording: the brief's framing of "the per-source state filter implemented as a Cypher predicate against the chunk-source linkage in neo4j_traverse" turned out structurally impossible at the wrapper boundary — the chunk-source linkage lives in Postgres, not Neo4j. Absorbed as build-time refinement (a) in D65. The two-store readiness query is the structurally honest interpretation of the brief's intent.

  Reflection prompt 2 (ChunkEmbedder task-hint refinement and the two-method port shape) — The task-hint refinement closed the S20 forward-affordance cleanly: the prefix logic encapsulates in the adapter (a single `_TASK_PREFIXES` dispatch table), the task is explicit at every call site (worker passes DOCUMENT, retrieval adapter passes QUERY), and the model-change resilience holds (an adapter for a model without prefixes simply ignores the task). The build-time refinement (b) — splitting the port into `embed` (Sequence[Chunk] → Sequence[Embedding]) and `embed_query` (str → Sequence[float]) — was forced by an honest shape consideration: a synthetic Chunk wrapping a query string would carry tenant_id / source_id / chunk_index / jurisdiction provenance fields the query genuinely lacks, and either the adapter's batch-mapping logic would have to special-case the synthetic-id case or the call site would have to fabricate provenance values. Two methods on the same port preserves D56's interface-segregation posture: each method serves a coherent access pattern with type-safe inputs and outputs, and consumers depend on the methods they call rather than a strategy parameter that hides the shape distinction.

  Reflection prompt 3 (two-method port surface under e2e test) — The two-method shape held under the e2e test. No third method surfaced as needed: the `search_by_entity_then_chunk` cross-cutting pattern the brief named as a forward-affordance candidate did not appear in build evidence. The single-call e2e cases (search at search.md and traverse from a seed) exercised the two methods directly; cross-cutting composition (e.g. retrieve-and-rank, retrieve-then-filter) is the agent-layer concern D5 commits to keeping out of the port surface. Forward-affordance for the data-retrieval design session: the two-method port is the substrate; richer composition lives in the agent runtime and in the design-session-output design surface. The cross-store readiness pattern (Postgres-as-source-of-truth + Neo4j-as-derivative) is also the natural starting point for any future projection-based shape the design session may consider.

  Reflection prompt 4 (cost-attribution path through Langfuse) — The retrieval-side embedding call traces cleanly through the same OTel surface as the ingestion-side call. The unit test (`test_embed_query_emits_cost_attributes_on_span`) and the e2e test (`test_query_embedding_emits_cost_attribution_span`) both verify the eleven-attribute shape: seven D27/D49/D50 attributes (gen_ai.system, gen_ai.request.model, gen_ai.operation.name, gen_ai.response.model, gen_ai.usage.input_tokens, three tenant.* attributes), plus the four `gen_ai.cost.*` attributes (input_usd, output_usd, total_usd, pricing_status), plus the new `padhanam.embedding.task` attribute that distinguishes corpus-side from query-side embedding spans for cost-rollup queries. The LiteLLM-vendor-quirks-class pattern did not bite here — `litellm.aembedding` against `openai/nomic-embed-text:v1.5` with the `search_query:` prefix returned cleanly, LiteLLM's local tokenizer populated `prompt_tokens` for the query as well as for the corpus side, and `pricing_status` lands as `table_hit` at zero rates per the D62 dev-cost precedent. The S20 reflection's optimistic-path assertion held at S22 too: the defensive `embedding_no_token_count` fallback path remains exercised by unit tests for future-tolerance against LiteLLM update drift, and lands as forward-affordance against the day a model without local tokenizer support enters the gateway.

  Reflection prompt 5 (forward-affordance for P6 close) — Three observations worth carrying into the P6 close strategic-mode block. (a) The framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern reaches twelve instances across P5+P6 with the three S22 build-time refinements (cross-store readiness query, two-method ChunkEmbedderPort shape, CLI commands at S22 over deferral) — well past the structural-promotion threshold the S20 / S21 reflections forecast. Worth a Patterns-observed entry at the next phase audit. (b) The user-driven course-correction pattern is at two instances (S20 per-stage claim methods, S21 wrapper-plus-import-linter shape); S22 had no operator-driven course corrections during build, the brief's framing held cleanly through to merge. The pattern is real but its trigger condition (architecturally-load-bearing choices the brief leaves room on) is rarer than the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern; worth recording as a related-but-distinct pattern at the phase audit. (c) The LiteLLM-vendor-quirks-class pre-write reconciliation pattern reaches its fifth instance with S22's reconciliation against `litellm.aembedding` for the query-side path — though the reconciliation surfaced no quirks this time, it's still load-bearing because the verification is the discipline. Five instances of the pattern over P5+P6 across distinct LiteLLM operation modes (chat at S6/S15, embedding-corpus at S20, extraction at S21, embedding-query at S22) is the strongest case for promoting the discipline to mandatory in any session that touches the LiteLLM gateway in a new operation mode.

  Reflection prompt 6 (forward-affordance for the data-retrieval design session) — Two observations on the shape the design session inherits. (a) The two-method port surface plus the cross-track readiness predicate are the substrate the design session starts from. Richer access patterns (filter expressions on retrieval, pagination, query rewriting, query decomposition, re-ranking, hybrid fusion strategies) all extend additively at the agent layer or as new port methods; none requires touching the wrapper or the SQL/Cypher templates. The design session's leverage is in deciding which cross-cutting patterns belong at agent-layer composition versus as widened ports. (b) The two-store readiness query in `neo4j_traverse` (Postgres pre-query → Neo4j Cypher with chunk-id set) is the structurally honest shape for now but would become a hot path under retrieval-heavy workloads — a 100k-indexed-chunks corpus would push the Postgres-to-Cypher parameter set to an awkward size. The forward-affordance is the optional path the brief named in the alternatives: denormalised ready flag on chunks (Postgres) and a derived ready property on `:Entity` (Neo4j); the migration is straightforward (a worker hook on source-state transition to `indexed` writes the flag), the cost is write-amplification at indexing time. The data-retrieval design session inherits the question: when retrieval latency surfaces measurement evidence that the cross-store readiness query is the bottleneck, the projection-based shape lands. Until then, the two-store query is the operator-credible default.

- methodology: Four observations worth surfacing at the next strategic conversation. First, the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern reaches twelve instances across P5+P6 with three S22 refinements (cross-store readiness query, two-method ChunkEmbedderPort shape, CLI commands at S22 over deferral). Three of the twelve are architectural-cost class, six are structural-honesty class, three are mechanical class; the three-class taxonomy from S19's reflection holds across the larger sample. Worth a Patterns-observed entry at the next phase audit per the methodology's structural-promotion threshold. Second, the user-driven course-correction pattern stayed at two instances during S22 — the brief's framing held cleanly through build with no operator pullback during implementation. The pattern's trigger condition (architecturally-load-bearing choices where the brief explicitly leaves room) is rarer than framing-prompt-as-recommendation; worth a separate Patterns-observed entry at the phase audit recording the trigger condition explicitly so future sessions can recognise it without conflating with the more frequent pattern. Third, the LiteLLM-vendor-quirks-class pre-write reconciliation pattern reaches its fifth instance at S22. The reconciliation produced no quirks against `litellm.aembedding`-with-query-prefix, but the discipline remains load-bearing because the value is the verification, not the assumption that quirks will land. The five instances span distinct LiteLLM operation modes (chat completion, embedding-corpus, extraction-via-JSON-mode, extraction-via-tool-calling-rejected, embedding-query); the methodology entry at the next phase audit should name "any new LiteLLM operation mode" as the trigger rather than the looser "any LiteLLM-touching session." Fourth, a methodology observation specific to S22 worth recording: the brief's interpretation surfaced as load-bearing at build time. The brief proposed the readiness predicate as "a Cypher predicate against the chunk-source linkage in neo4j_traverse" — a phrase that reads cleanly but specifies a structurally impossible shape (Neo4j has no chunk-source linkage natively). The build-time refinement (cross-store readiness query) was the only correct shape consistent with D32 + D63, and the operator's brief-language slack let the build commit to it without reframing. The pattern is "framing language abstract enough to cover both correct shapes; build commits to the correct shape; D-entry's Choice text records the structurally honest shape." Same shape as S21's `get_*_for_source(source_id)` → `get_*_by_chunk_ids(chunk_ids)` refinement. Worth carrying as a sub-observation under framing-prompt-as-recommendation at the phase audit.

## P6 mid-package strategic commit — product reframe absorption
roles: analyst, PM, technical writer
mode: strategic

- Produced: One charter commit landing four artefacts: a new section "The demonstration's scope" in `charter/bet.md` capturing the four functional domains, the methodology-embedded-not-gated commitment, and the build sequence; a new principle "Methodology is embedded as defaults, not gated as workflow steps" in `charter/principles.md` under the Architectural section; a new file `charter/product-methodology.md` holding the four functional domains, candidate methodologies per domain, build sequence with per-domain reasoning, and selection discipline; and a scope-note paragraph at the top of `charter/methodology.md` distinguishing build methodology from product methodology.

- Decisions: None new. The reframe sharpens the bet's articulation and surfaces a new product-methodology layer in the charter. Per-domain methodology selection stays as a selection space; per-domain decisions land as each domain enters package scope.

- Tests: None. Documentation-only changes.

- Reflection: The reframe was operator-driven against drift accumulating in the framing conversations. The shape of the drift is worth recording: deep technical framing on Phase 1 substrate (Neo4j topology, embedding model, graph extraction shape) without periodic re-grounding in what the platform demonstrates and for whom. The drift is a recurrent class — same shape as the operator pullbacks at the within-tenant segmentation question, the JPMorgan reference architecture absorption, and the data-retrieval design-session elevation. Each pullback was structurally similar: model framing pushed past architectural altitude into commitment territory; operator pulled back to defer or reframe; charter absorbed the discipline. Worth a Patterns-observed entry at Phase 1 close audit alongside the framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern: external-reference and bet-reframe inputs need triage by category and abstraction level before they can land on the right charter surface, and operator-initiated reframes are the recovery mechanism when conversation drift accumulates.

  Two methodology layers now live distinctly in the charter. Build methodology at `charter/methodology.md` covers how Padhanam itself is built; product methodology at `charter/product-methodology.md` covers what the platform encodes for users. The distinction matters because conflation produces architectural ambiguity (is "methodology" the operator's discipline or the product's surface?) and obscures which layer's commitments apply at which moment. Future charter content that touches "methodology" needs to identify which layer it modifies.

  The build sequence (PM, L&D + Marketing, PgM) is non-trivially ordered. PM-first is operator expertise depth and audience match. L&D + Marketing-second is user-validation availability. PgM-last is commercial leverage plus hardest embedding test, which inverts how the order initially read. The ordering encodes a learning trajectory: embedding patterns accumulate across the first three domains before the hardest test runs.

The asymmetry tracks the methodology document's existing structure (prescriptive principles in the body; Patterns-observed and Failure-modes entries below).

The data-retrieval design session is queued as a strategic-mode conversation; the product reframe's four-domain demonstration scope is queued as P7 framing input; the per-tenant Neo4j topology deferred entry remains as the production-deployment revisit marker.

  Phase 1 close audit horizon now visible: P6 close brings the run to 5 of 12 packages closed, ~60% by RICE-effort weeks. The three methodology promotions at this commit are the substantive Phase 1 audit material on the methodology side; the procurement-grade orchestration posture corroborated by the external case study at the mid-package absorption is the substantive Phase 1 audit material on the bet side. ## Data-retrieval design session
roles: analyst, architect, technical writer
mode: strategic

- Produced: One charter commit landing four artefacts: D66 (hybrid composition architecture) and D67 (filter expression architecture) appended to `charter/decisions.md`; resolution note appended to the data-retrieval item under the Ask David capture in `log/captures.md`; `charter/current-package.md` updated to remove the data-retrieval design session from carryovers, add the retrieval-evaluation design session as a queued strategic-mode commitment ahead of P11, and update the next-strategic-mode-conversation paragraph to point at P7 framing.

- Decisions: D66 (hybrid composition architecture; agent-runtime-executed composition; layered strategy selection per-agent default plus per-query override; three-strategy starter catalogue [vector_only, graph_only, parallel_rrf]; chunks as unit of composition; free-text query input with internal seed extraction; deferred shapes [cascade variants, parallel_weighted, routed, subgraph-as-output] named with explicit activation triggers; twelve rejected alternatives weighed; Kano: must-have on the architectural commitments, performance on the specific catalogue size). D67 (filter expression architecture; typed Boolean tree with AND/OR/NOT composers and Predicate leaves; bounded leaf operator vocabulary at P8 [eq, in, gt, gte, lt, lte]; scope-filters-pre-composition / cut-off-filters-post-composition pipeline split; cut-off as separate fields top_k and min_score on the retrieval spec rather than as predicates inside the filter tree; deferred operators [contains, exists/is_null, regex/like] with explicit triggers; eleven rejected alternatives weighed including the conjunctive-only-typed-object position which was the recommendation before operator pushback; Kano: must-have on the architectural commitments, performance on the specific field set).

- Tests: None. Documentation-only changes.

- Reflection: The session held to the focused scope agreed at the open: hybrid composition and filter expressions only, with retrieval-evaluation deferred to its own session and query-rewriting-and-decomposition deferred to the P8 hierarchical-topology design session per the Ask David capture's queueing. The Kano classification of nine candidate surfaces (hybrid composition, filter expressions, retrieval evaluation, query rewriting, re-ranking, pagination, projection patterns, structured-data port, analytics-agent shape) at the prioritisation step caught the must-have/performance/delighter distribution honestly; the Kano-versus-RICE asymmetry on retrieval evaluation (must-have on Kano, high effort on RICE relative to on-runtime impact) was the structural argument for splitting it out, which is the design-session output that earns its own strategic-mode commitment ahead of P11. No package elevation needed; the design-session output is two D-entries inheriting into P7 (agent config schema) and P8 (runtime orchestrator and translator), which is the right shape for architectural commitments that the carryover note ("possible roadmap v5 if scope warrants package elevation") flagged as conditional on scope. Scope held at design-session size; no roadmap change.

  One observation worth carrying forward as a Patterns-observed candidate: the start-simple-versus-structural-completeness distinction. The framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern's three-class taxonomy (mechanical / structural-honesty / architectural-cost) names the kinds of refinement build absorbs from briefs; this observation names a related but distinct kind of refinement strategic-mode work absorbs from operator pushback. The pattern: when a foundational contract is being designed, start-simple applies to scope (which fields, operators, strategies are in v1) but not to structural completeness of the language those things compose into (Boolean composition, leaf operator bounds, pipeline split). Conflating the two produces under-designed primitives that force v2 to break v1, which contradicts procurement-defensibility. The OR/NOT pushback at the filter-expression sub-question was the first instance: the original recommendation deferred OR and NOT under start-simple framing; the operator pushback ("you will come across OR or NOT in real life") surfaced the misapplication; the revised recommendation accepted the combined Boolean-tree-with-bounded-leaves shape. Single instance at this session; not yet a Patterns-observed entry per the recurrence threshold; recorded here so the next strategic conversation that touches a foundational contract surface can decide whether to promote.

  Method-wise, the conversation followed the same shape as the Ask David and product-reframe absorptions: the user-driven course-correction pattern fired once (the OR/NOT pushback) and was absorbed before it accumulated drift, which is a third named instance of the user-driven course-correction Patterns-observed entry from P6 close. The pattern continues to do load-bearing work across strategic-mode conversations.

## P7 mid-package strategic block — security-readiness gap closure
roles: analyst, PM, architect, technical writer
mode: strategic

- Produced: Two charter commits, no code touched. Commit 1 (`docs(charter): land compliance shared-responsibility principle, security-readiness D-entries D69-D73, and Layer A scaffold`) lands seven artefacts: a new compliance-as-shared-responsibility bullet appended to `charter/principles.md` Security posture, naming Layer A platform-owned documentation, Layer B per-tenant evidence aggregation, Layer C tenant-owned workflow compliance frames with the C1 data-protection scaffold and C2 framework-attestation inheritance map split; five new D-entries D69 through D73 appended in numerical order to `charter/decisions.md`; two new entries appended to `charter/deferred-decisions.md` for Layer B per-tenant compliance evidence aggregation and Layer C workflow compliance frames with explicit activation conditions; a new `charter/compliance/` directory containing eleven files (one README plus ten policy scaffolds at `information-security-policy.md`, `access-control-policy.md`, `change-management-policy.md`, `vendor-management-policy.md`, `incident-response-runbook.md`, `business-continuity-plan.md`, `cryptography-policy.md`, `data-classification-policy.md`, `retention-schedule.md`, `acceptable-use-policy.md`); and a Layer A policy authoring carryover entry added to `charter/current-package.md`. Commit 2: this entry.

- Decisions: D69 (prompt injection threat model with four named defences — source provenance, tool-call output filtering plus per-tenant egress allowlists, instruction hierarchy, red-team-shaped contract tests at `tests/contract/agent_runtime/`; activates at P8 framing; Kano: must-have). D70 (cryptographic erasure for right-to-erasure under append-only audit chains via per-tenant DEK destruction preserving D26 chain integrity; activates at the Phase 2 package framing data-subject rights operationally; Kano: must-have for GDPR-jurisdiction tenants, performance for non-GDPR jurisdictions). D71 (tool execution sandboxing layered by tool category — HTTP-based via network boundary plus per-tenant egress allowlists, code-execution via gVisor or Firecracker; activates at P8 with egress allowlists ramping at production deployment context; Kano: must-have). D72 (image signing via cosign and SLSA level-3 build-provenance attestation gating production deployment; activates at the production deployment context; Kano: performance for AI Labs, must-have for federal-adjacent buyers). D73 (post-quantum cryptography readiness with four boundary commitments — production TLS hybrid KEX, PQ-aware production KMS selection, identity-federation revisit at Phase 2, outbound-LLM-TLS vendor-tracking under `ops/scheduled_checks.yaml` — plus a `cipher_id` schema forward-affordance reserved for future cipher swap; symmetric primitives in current use remain PQ-safe; Kano: delighter for AI Labs at 2026, must-have for federal-adjacent at 2026, performance for the broad market by 2028). The principle addition and the two deferred-decisions entries (Layer B and Layer C) are not D-entries but are first-class architectural commitments landed in the same commit.

- Tests: `make lint` 19 contracts kept, 0 broken. `uv run pytest` 307 passed, 87 skipped — baseline unchanged across the session. No code touched; documentation-only strategic block. State-at-open verification confirmed working tree clean against `main`, D68 as the latest D-entry, `charter/current-package.md` in active-P7 state with the four-session breakdown S23-S26, and no pre-existing `charter/compliance/` directory.

- Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (principle placement) — The Security posture section absorbed the new bullet cleanly because compliance-as-shared-responsibility reads as a security-posture statement at the architectural-ownership level: which compliance documentation is platform-authored versus tenant-authored is structurally part of the security posture's load-bearing claims. The Layer A/B/C and C1/C2 distinctions packed into a single bullet without forcing a new section heading, but the bullet is denser than its peers and the "platform never authors documents that attest to tenant-operated controls" sentence carries weight that may want its own surface at the next phase audit. Recording as placement-confirmed at this commit with a candidate-for-promotion note: at the Phase 1 close audit, if the compliance documentation surface has expanded enough that the single bullet feels overloaded, a "Compliance documentation" section heading absorbs the relevant material with the Security posture section retaining its security-technical scope. The split is not forced now because the bullet is the only compliance-shaped principle; promoting prematurely would create a single-bullet section.

  Reflection prompt 2 (five-D-entry shape under the structural-honesty test) — Each of D69 through D73 commits at framing-time to architectural posture that is implemented later, which is the same shape as D24's tenant isolation commitment landed at P2. The Kano categorisation across the five is the test: D69, D70, D71 land as must-have, justifying framing-now commitment because the agent runtime, data-subject rights, and tool execution surfaces would otherwise absorb security failures as build-time discoveries. D72 lands as performance for AI Labs and must-have for federal-adjacent buyers; the asymmetric Kano category is the structurally honest shape, mirroring D69's pattern, and the framing-now commitment is justified by the CI pipeline being the pre-condition the production deployment package consumes. D73 carries the most differentiated Kano spread (delighter, must-have, performance across audiences and timelines) and is the entry that came closest to the "premature commitment" bar. The argument that holds D73 at framing-now rather than as a deferred-decisions entry: the four boundary commitments (TLS, KMS, identity, outbound-TLS) plus the `cipher_id` forward-affordance are all architectural-posture commitments rather than implementation work, and the symmetric-primitive PQ-safety claim is the load-bearing assertion that would otherwise need defending later in a procurement context. None of the five entries reads as drift toward deferred-decisions territory; the structural-honesty test passes for all five.

  Reflection prompt 3 (Layer A scaffold versus content authoring split) — Splitting structural commitment from content authoring is the right shape. Ten policy documents authored cleanly is one focused session's work; absorbing them into this security-readiness gap-closure block would have compressed the strategic surface badly because the gap-closure block is doing principle-authoring, D-entry-authoring, deferred-decisions-authoring, and scaffold-authoring work all at once, and policy content authoring is itself a substantive surface that wants its own attention. The carryover at `charter/current-package.md` makes the deferral honest: the substrate (D-entries, principle, scaffold structure) is in place and the policy authoring session inherits a stable home. The risk is operator-scheduling drift on the carryover; mitigated by the carryover not blocking any P7 build session and by the scaffolds existing as visible reminders in the charter tree. Recording the split as confirmed correct with a note: if the policy authoring carryover sits unactioned past the Phase 1 close audit, the audit framing conversation should pull it explicitly into the audit work because the SOC 2 Type II + ISO 27001 commitment in the bet cannot stand without the Layer A documentation existing as more than scaffolds.

  Reflection prompt 4 (forward-affordance-without-consumer as methodology pattern) — The "we don't need to build it all" cut at the strategic conversation invoked the same paper-architecture argument that has now done load-bearing work three times in the project's history: S15's classification deferral (consumer-driven activation rather than upfront architectural commitment), S14's cost-ceiling columns (forward-affordance with named consumer in P11), and this commit's Layer B and Layer C deferrals (substrate-exists-but-pipeline-defers, with named activation conditions). Each instance shares a structural shape: a consumer-shaped need is identified, the substrate that would feed the consumer is examined, and the cut question becomes "does landing the consumer-facing pipeline now produce value, or does it produce paper architecture that decays before a real consumer arrives?" The methodology pattern worth surfacing at the next phase audit if this fires a fourth time: forward-affordance-without-consumer as a Patterns-observed entry in `charter/methodology.md`, joining framing-prompt-as-recommendation and pre-write reconciliation as named methodology disciplines. The pattern's structural-promotion threshold per the existing methodology convention requires recurrence; three instances at three distinct architectural moments is on the boundary of recurrence-versus-coincidence, but the test the next phase audit applies is whether a fourth instance fires cleanly with the same shape or whether the pattern fragments into sub-shapes that are not the same discipline. - Reflection: Reflection prompt 1 (aggregate shape under build experience) — D74's persistence shape held cleanly; no pull toward a different shape surfaced under build. Specifically, the JSONB-per-structured-field design (separate columns for retrieval_strategy, filter_tree, source_ids, tool_allowlist) versus the alternatives (single config jsonb, normalised tables) tested against build-time pressure: would the SQLAlchemy adapter's row-to-domain construction be cleaner with one alternative? The build experience shows the four-jsonb design produces a clean adapter (`_revision_insert_values` is mechanical; `_row_to_revision` is mechanical), and the architectural distinction between retrieval (D66) and filter (D67) concerns reads correctly at the column level. The shape held; no fourth instance of the framing-prompt-as-recommendation forward-affordance-adjacent shape fired at the persistence shape this session. The aggregate shape itself fired one structural-honesty interpretation noted at prompt 5 (description on template versus revision; not aggregate-shape drift but aggregate-semantics clarification).

  Reflection prompt 2 (hash-chain content surface boundary) — Three boundary cases surfaced under build that the brief did not explicitly anticipate. First, list ordering for source_ids and tool_allowlist: the brief commits to canonical sort, but `json.dumps(sort_keys=True)` only sorts dict keys, not list contents — verified by tracing the implementation. The fix lands inside `compute_revision_hash` via `_normalise_list_fields` which sorts the lists before serialisation; tests pin the property that list-order variation does not produce hash drift. This was a structural-honesty refinement of the brief's specifics rather than a deviation: the brief said "with internal sort" without specifying where the sort happens; the use case layer would have been an alternative location, but baking it into the hash helper is more defensive (callers cannot accidentally produce hash drift through list-order variation). Second, the description-on-template-versus-revision ambiguity in D74: the brief says the hash spans description, MethodologyTemplate carries description, and the CLI update reads "the same config shape minus the name" — three statements that together leave ambiguous whether description can change via update. The build session resolved this by treating description as immutable on the template (the use case `update_methodology_template` does not accept a description argument; the hash payload pulls description from the parent template at hash-computation time, valid for all revisions because description is treated as effectively frozen post-create). This is a structural-honesty refinement landed in the methodology use_cases.py module docstring; documenting here for the build-session-deviation count below. Third, the Decimal canonical-form behaviour at exotic values: pinned all four edge cases explicitly (Decimal("0") → "0", Decimal("-0.5") → "-0.5", Decimal("100") → "100", Decimal("1.5E+10") → "15000000000") so future encoder changes break tests rather than silently break hash compatibility. The pre-build switch from `str(value.normalize())` to `format(value.normalize(), "f")` (Patch 12) saved a deviation that the build would have caught at the Decimal("100") boundary; the format-f resolution lands exactly as anticipated.

  Reflection prompt 3 (repository-without-TenantContext pattern) — The methodology repository pattern held cleanly through use case → port → adapter, validating the pattern as a precedent for future control-plane contexts. No structural pressure surfaced to add TenantContext at the port layer for read uniformity. Specifically, the test that this would matter is whether the integration tests felt awkward without TenantContext; they do not — the test fixtures construct an operator principal for writes and verify reads succeed for tenant principals, exactly mirroring the registry isolation pattern. The runtime tripwire test verifying the port import does not pull sqlalchemy/alembic/asyncpg passes cleanly, demonstrating the port stays framework-free. The pattern is now structurally validated as a precedent for future control-plane contexts; the platform-baseline-library deferred entry per D53, when activated, can adopt the same shape without architectural debate. Recording the pattern as confirmed at this commit; future control-plane contexts inherit the no-TenantContext-on-port shape via cross-reference to D74.

  Reflection prompt 4 (CLI --config-file shape under build) — One operator-ergonomics friction surfaced and was resolved inline; one defers as a known refinement. The friction that resolved: float-vs-Decimal precision loss at config parse time. YAML and JSON return `0.7` as a binary float, which converts to `Decimal(0.7)` as `Decimal('0.6999999999999999...')`. The fix is `Decimal(str(value))` which preserves the lexical form the user wrote; this is the convention now committed at `_to_decimal` in `_methodology.py`. The defers-as-refinement: schema validation error messages. The CLI's `_validate_required` produces the message `config missing required fields: ['source_ids']` which is correct but minimal; richer errors (per-field type validation, value-range validation, embedded examples) are deferred until operator feedback indicates the minimal version is the wrong shape. The brief's --config-file shape held against the alternative (flag-per-field) explicitly tested at D74 alternative (h); the build experience confirms the structural argument — the field set is too varied (scalar + list + nested dict) for flag-per-field to be ergonomic, and schema-validatable-at-parse-time is the value.

  Reflection prompt 5 (build-time deviation discipline) — Six deviations from the brief's named specifics fired at build time. (a) JSONB convention: brief specified `pg.JSONB(astext_type=Text())` but actual precedent at `scoring_sheet_criteria.levels` uses bare `pg.JSONB()`; followed actual precedent. **Mechanical**. (b) `apps/api/Dockerfile` COPY directive: brief's commit 3 file list omitted; AST workspace-coverage enforcement test required it. **Mechanical**. (c) Domain test count: 29 versus 14-18 target. The overshoot adds the format-f Decimal-edge-case pinning (4 tests) and the audit-chain structural-equivalence test (1 test) plus extra coverage on canonical-JSON nested-dict recursion and list-normalisation behaviour. **Mechanical** — additive coverage. (d) async test runner: pytest-asyncio not in dev deps; followed `event_loop.run_until_complete` pattern from the tenancy isolation tests. **Mechanical** — convention discovered by reading existing tests. (e) Description-immutability interpretation: D74 has internal tension between "description is on template" and "hash spans description" and "update config shape minus name only"; resolved by treating description as immutable post-create with hash payload pulling from parent template. **Structural-honesty** — required for chain integrity without retroactive schema changes. (f) `name` denormalisation interpretation: D74 says "name (denormalised onto the revision for chain-self-containment)" but the schema in commit 2 has no name column on methodology_revisions; resolved by treating the denormalisation as conceptual (name is in hash payload at hash time, fetched from parent template) rather than literal (persisted as a column). **Structural-honesty** — preserves chain integrity at the hash-content-surface level without forcing a schema change after commit 2 already landed. The S23 deviation count is six, of which four are mechanical and two are structural-honesty. The running count from P5 and P6 is thirteen-plus; S23 increments it to nineteen-plus. Both structural-honesty deviations at S23 are clarifications of D74 wording ambiguities rather than departures from D74's architectural commitments — the chain-integrity property D74 asserts holds for all revisions, the immutability discipline D74 asserts holds for the template aggregate, and the operator-context enforcement D74 asserts holds at the use case layer. The brief-as-recommendation principle held: each deviation absorbed into commit-shaped work without rework, and the v1 brief at `briefs/p7/s23.md` stays preserved for future-session-reflection-author reference.

  Reflection prompt 6 (pre-build drift catch as methodology observation) — Three pre-build catches fired at this brief, distinct from build-time refinement of framing specifics. The narrower-versus-broader-discipline question per the prompt's append: drifts 1 and 2 (OPERATOR_ROLE location, audit-precedent shape) were caught by **code-reading verification of named locations** — the brief named specific paths and line ranges, the pre-build review opened those paths, the brief's claim and the code's actual content diverged. The Decimal handling catch (Patch 11→12) was caught by **deliberate-silence-detection** — the brief did not name a Decimal-handling claim; it surfaced Decimal as a reflection prompt (an aspirational future-resolution surface) rather than as a settled framing commitment. The pre-build review noticed that hash chain integrity is load-bearing enough that silence at framing time invites build-time refinement at higher cost. Three instances in one review pass suggest the broader formulation is doing the load-bearing work: pre-build review with code reading PLUS deliberate-silence-detection on load-bearing technical commitments. The narrower formulation (verify named precedents) would have caught drifts 1 and 2 but not the Decimal catch; the Decimal catch is what makes the broader formulation more reusable. Cost asymmetry validated: all three pre-build catches resolved as inline brief refinements (Patches 1-13) before brief preservation; zero build-time deviations attributable to these three catches; the v1 brief lands structurally honest at first commit. The build-session deviation count of six (per prompt 5) is the floor-of-honest-deviation-cost, and none of the six trace back to a framing decision that pre-build review could have caught — they all emerge from the build-time encounter with actual code state (existing precedents, AST enforcement tests, async runner conventions, D74 wording ambiguities at use-case-layer fidelity). The discipline is now a methodology candidate worth recording at the next phase audit: pre-build review with code-reading verification plus deliberate-silence-detection on load-bearing technical commitments. The structural-promotion threshold per the existing convention requires recurrence; S23 is the first instance where the discipline fired explicitly across three distinct catch types in one review pass. If S24's pre-build review fires the same discipline cleanly (catching drifts that build would have absorbed), the case for methodology promotion strengthens. If S24's review surfaces the discipline but the catches feel fragmented or the silence-detection instance does not recur, the methodology entry might split into sub-shapes.

- methodology: Pre-build review with code-reading verification plus deliberate-silence-detection on load-bearing technical commitments fired across three distinct catch types in one review pass at S23 brief drafting (drifts 1 and 2 caught by code-reading verification; Decimal handling caught by deliberate-silence-detection of an aspirational reflection prompt that should have been a settled framing commitment). Cost asymmetry validated by zero build-time deviations attributable to these three catches and the v1 brief landing structurally honest at first commit. Captured as a methodology candidate for promotion at the next phase audit; recurrence test at S24's pre-build review will inform whether the discipline promotes as a single broader formulation (verify named precedents AND detect deliberate silence on load-bearing technical commitments) or splits into sub-shapes. Third: the methodology-specific tests (filter_content_fields, _normalise_list_fields direct invocation) were dropped rather than relocated — the field-set-agnostic helper has no use case for them. The S23 OPERATOR_ROLE promotion was clean (no ancillary helpers to consider); the second-consumer-promotes pattern at S24 surfaces the helper-API-shape question that S23 didn't have to answer. Pattern shape recurs but with refinement: when the second consumer arrives, the helper's API shape settles at that moment, and the promotion may include shape changes (not just relocation). The Patterns-observed candidate from D75 should reflect this: "second-consumer-promotes plus settle-helper-API-shape-at-second-consumer-moment."

  Reflection prompt 3 (lineage-as-template-level placement under build) — D75's commitment to lineage on AgentTemplate held structurally with no pressure to add lineage to revisions. The blank-created path exercised at S24 doesn't surface clone-related queries (they land at S25); the build experience confirms that the lineage fields naturally belong on the template envelope. The paired-NULL invariant via __post_init__ landed cleanly with 2 explicit tests (id-only-raises and version-only-raises); the schema-layer CHECK constraint provides defence-in-depth and was verified by the structural-isolation contract test at commit 9. The cross-context flow at S25 will confirm or refine the placement decision when both halves of the lineage actually populate.

  Reflection prompt 4 (auth posture under build) — Tenant-context-or-operator-context auth landed cleanly with one boundary case worth recording: the use case checks `is_operator(principal) or len(principal.roles) > 0`. This passes both operator-context (carries OPERATOR_ROLE) and tenant-context (carries any tenant-scoped role like "agent.write"); empty-role-set principals raise AuthorizationError. The boundary that doesn't surface at the use case layer but matters at the integration layer: what if the principal's tenant_id doesn't match the routed TenantContext? The CLI's _TenantBoundResolver enforces this at the resolver layer (raises LookupError), but the use cases don't cross-check principal.tenant_id vs tenant_context.tenant_id. This is fine for S24 (CLI is the only consumer; it owns both) but will surface at apps/api when the principal comes from JWT decoding and the TenantContext from request routing — those two might disagree. Recording as a forward-affordance: the integration test for apps/api at a future session needs to assert principal-context vs tenant-context consistency.

  Reflection prompt 5 (build-time deviation discipline) — Two deviations from this brief's named specifics fired at build time, both **mechanical** rather than structural-honesty. (a) Test count: 55 new authored tests versus brief commit-7 implicit "10 integration tests" + commit-8 "8-12 use case tests" target. The overshoot is concentrated in domain coverage (13 vs implicit ~6) and integration coverage (12 vs implicit 10) — the additional tests pin the structural assertions D75 makes explicit (no name field on revision; paired-NULL invariant; chain-self-containment via payload key). **Mechanical** — additive coverage. (b) The S23 deviation (alembic version_num truncation) surfaced as a state-at-open precondition rather than a brief drift; the fix landed before S24 commit 1 as a separate fix(p7/s23) commit. **Structural-honesty for S23, mechanical for S24** — S24's brief did not anticipate this issue but the operator's fix decision absorbed it cleanly. The S24 deviation count is two, both mechanical. The running count from P5/P6/S23 is nineteen-plus; S24 increments it to twenty-one. The pre-build review discipline reduced deviations meaningfully: the brief's pre-build review (Patches 14-19) caught three structural-honesty drifts that build would otherwise have absorbed (name placement, description placement, hash-chain helper field-set coupling); zero structural-honesty deviations fired at build time as a result. The validate-pre-build pattern is paying off again.

  Reflection prompt 6 (pre-build review recurrence test, S23 prompt 6 carryover) — Three pre-build catches fired at this brief's review pass before preservation, distinct from build-time refinement. Two from **code-reading verification of named locations** (drifts 1 and 2: D75's text asserted name and description placement that diverged from `contexts/methodology/domain/methodology.py:8-16` and `contexts/methodology/application/use_cases.py:111-122` — S23's actual implementation chose conceptual denormalisation, not literal). One from **deliberate-silence-detection on load-bearing technical commitments** (drift 3: D75's text named four exports for the promoted helper but said nothing about CONTENT_FIELDS, _normalise_list_fields, or filter_content_fields — a load-bearing silence on the helper-API surface that the second-consumer moment was the cheapest moment to settle). Three catches across two distinct catch types in one review pass. Pattern shape matches S23's three-catch instance (drifts 1 and 2 by code-reading verification, Decimal handling by deliberate-silence-detection). Two instances of the same catch-type combination in two consecutive build-session reviews — the methodology-document candidate strengthens. The single broader formulation (verify named precedents AND detect deliberate silence on load-bearing technical commitments) is doing the load-bearing work; the narrower formulation (verify named precedents only) would have caught drifts 1 and 2 but missed drift 3. Cost asymmetry validated again: all three pre-build catches resolved as inline brief refinements before preservation, zero structural-honesty deviations attributable to these catches at build time. Recommending promotion to a methodology document at the next phase audit (P7 close).

- Decisions: D79 (Cross-context create_agent_from_methodology flow shape — agent-owned cloning via callable api-facade ports, MethodologyView and SourceLookup as consumer-side abstractions; seven rejected alternatives weighed; Kano: performance for architectural commitments — api-facade-via-callable shape, MethodologyView placement, lineage recording mechanics, source existence validation timing; delighter for CLI ergonomics — config-file-based create-from-methodology consistent with blank-create; must-have for the LVT template content surface — the bet's "one agent configured from a methodology template" demonstration depends on a real LVT system prompt rather than a placeholder).

- Tests: `make lint` 23 contracts kept, 0 broken (was 21 at session open; +2 cross-context independence contracts naming the agent-no-methodology-internal and agent-no-ingestion-internal commitments explicitly; the existing contexts-independent-{domain,application,adapters} mutual-independence contracts continue to cover the broader cross-context invariant). `make migrate` not exercised; S25 ships zero migrations (per-tenant track still at `0008_agent_tables`; control-plane track still at `0004_methodology_tables`). `uv run pytest` at session close with the POSTGRES_CONTROL_PLANE_HOST/PORT override: 557 passed + 15 skipped + 0 failed in 569s. Without overrides, the same S23-era control-plane test-environment issue remains (carried forward from S24 close as a pre-existing finding; not a S25 regression). Test-count baseline at session open: 526 collected (matches S24-close report). New tests authored at S25: 4 ingestion get_source unit + 7 MethodologyView/MethodologyLookup unit + 6 SourceLookup unit + 13 create_agent_from_methodology unit + 6 cross-context adapter integration + 10 agent import-isolation AST + 1 e2e = 47 new tests authored. Tenant-isolation contract tests passed (the one intermittent `test_search_against_empty_tenant_returns_no_results[a]` failure observed in the first run resolved on second run with no remediation needed; flaky on concurrent worker-state alongside the test's own truncate fixture — not a S25 regression).

- Reflection:

  Reflection prompt 1 (cross-context flow shape under build). The api-facade-via-callable pattern held cleanly without friction the brief did not anticipate. The keyword-only-arguments shape on MethodologyLookup landed as drafted; the Protocol-typing structural-satisfaction proved at the integration-test layer where a plain async function with the right signature satisfies the type without explicit inheritance. The consumer-side DTO (MethodologyView) preserved the audience-boundary cleanly: the AST-level test at commit 4 verified zero imports from `contexts.methodology` at the file level; the import-linter contracts at commit 9 verified the same at the graph level; the integration tests at commit 7 demonstrated the adapter is the legitimate seam where the producer's tuple becomes the consumer's DTO. Adapter wiring landed at a sibling module (`apps/cli/_cross_context.py`) rather than extending `_runtime.py`; the sibling choice was a mechanical decision driven by single-responsibility legibility and is recorded here rather than as a build-time deviation because the brief explicitly named the choice as available. Byte-equivalence with `create_blank_agent` is the structural-honesty checkpoint this prompt is reaching for: revision-1 hashes are byte-identical between clone and blank-create paths with the same content fields, asserted at commit 6's unit-test layer (`test_byte_equivalent_hash_versus_blank_create`) rather than only at the e2e by implication — making chain integrity verification at audit time treat clone-created and blank-created revisions uniformly through their shared `_content_payload` invocation.

  Reflection prompt 2 (lineage from methodology to agent under build). The paired-populated lineage flow exercised D75's invariant cleanly through both unit tests (10 of 13 unit tests touch lineage-population paths) and the e2e + live-stack persistence. Version-resolution-at-clone-time landed at the adapter layer as specified — the underlying `get_methodology_template` resolves None to latest at the repository, and the adapter records the integer in MethodologyView's `methodology_version` field. The use case never sees None in the lineage path. No complications surfaced from methodology-revision-added-between-request-and-persistence or concurrent-edits-to-methodology-mid-clone; the synthetic write window in the e2e and the live-stack persistence both ran serially with no contention. If contention becomes load-bearing at higher tenant throughput in a future package, the methodology repository's revision lookup could be wrapped in a snapshot read; deferred until measurement justifies.

  Reflection prompt 3 (source attachment validation timing). Source existence validation at the use case boundary fired correctly on missing source_ids (5 dedicated unit tests across happy path, missing-ids accumulation into SourceNotFoundError, cross-tenant routing treated as missing per D24). The adapter's per-id iteration through the ingestion `get_source` use case proved acceptable at clone time — the source-existence check on small N (typically a handful of sources per agent) does not require batch query optimisation. One boundary case worth recording: the adapter treats cross-tenant access as indistinguishable from missing-id because D24 tenant isolation makes the two structurally identical at the repository read. SourceNotFoundError carries the offending ids on the exception instance so operator logs surface the precise failure without requiring re-query. ## P7→P8 boundary — Topology design strategic block at P7→P8 boundary

- Produced: One charter commit landing six new D-entries (D80-D85), one new charter file, one new package epic note, principles edits, packages.md and roadmap.md updates, current-package.md transition, three new deferred-decisions entries, captures triage update, brief preservation, and this session log entry.
