New charter file `charter/contexts/workflow.md` carries the twelve named architectural commitments for the workflow bounded context (aggregate shape, definition JSONB shape, storage pattern, cross-context relationships, WorkflowExecutor port, LangGraph adapter, auth posture, tenant isolation, CLI surface, Phase 2 implementation scope, out of scope for Phase 1, composition terminology). Revised `charter/packages/p8-epic.md` carries the five-session breakdown (S26 methodology v2 migration; S27 AgentExecutor port plus AgentLoopExecutor adapter plus agent runtime use case; S28 tool registry context plus tool classification plus invariant enforcement; S29 SSE streaming plus trace capture; S30 CLI plus end-to-end test plus P8 close) with explicit out-of-scope list naming workflow context implementation, LangGraph adapter, gallery surfaces, cost ceiling runtime enforcement, multi-agent composition, reflective agent loops, provider-specific agent SDKs, persistent agents. `charter/principles.md` gains a new User safety section sitting between Engineering practice and Decision discipline (Padhanam-as-intelligence-layer commitment; five platform invariants with dimension justification; consent-granularity proportionate to danger; content framing for high-stakes domains as content principle distinct from capability invariants; evolution discipline with promotions in and out recorded as charter commits) and the Architectural section's methodology-embedded principle is refined to distinguish tuning surfaces (overrides cheap) from envelope surfaces (bind hard, validated at agent write time) with the conditionality for agents without methodology lineage explicit. `charter/packages.md` P8 line replaced. `charter/roadmap.md` gains v4 entry with discovery reasoning category. `charter/current-package.md` between-packages section reflects topology design landed and McKinsey 7-Step as next strategic block per D85; the topology design carryover bullet under "Carryovers active across the P7→P8 boundary" updated to document what was committed. `charter/deferred-decisions.md` gains three new entries (cascading-harm invariant shape — activates with multi-agent workflows or persistent agents; retrieval-bound hard-constraint shape on methodology roles — activates when methodology evidence shows soft-binding insufficient; per-role binding-mode override — activates when methodology evidence shows the platform-level convention insufficient). `log/captures.md` 2026-05-11 P7 strategic block synthesis entry resolution line updated to record absorption of architectural scope across D80-D85 plus the artefact catalogue. `briefs/p8/topology-design.md` preserves the session prompt per the brief preservation convention from S17b forward. The bet is unchanged. P8 sits inside its existing roadmap slot with scope reframed; RICE unchanged.

- Decisions: D80 (four-layer constraint stack; three rejected alternatives weighed including strict-layered-all-mandatory which contradicts D68's blank-agent path; Kano: must-have because the layering relationship between methodology, workflow, agent, and platform invariants needs explicit commitment to prevent inconsistent inheritance in future sessions). D81 (methodology aggregate v2 with multi-role JSONB refinement and per-field binding mode; seven rejected alternatives weighed including roles-as-separate-aggregates, defer-multi-role-entirely, per-role binding-mode override which defers to a new deferred-decisions entry, single-binding-mode, methodology-propagates-on-update which contradicts D68's independent-clones commitment, retrieval-bounds-as-hard-constraints which defers to a new deferred-decisions entry; Kano: must-have on multi-role refinement, performance on binding-mode classification defaults). D82 (platform invariants and Padhanam-as-intelligence-layer; six rejected alternatives weighed including strict intelligence-layer-no-action commitment rejected on mass-consumer grounds, the captures' broad "no-STP for personal work" sharpened into invariants 2 and 3, health-and-safety content framing landed as content principle distinct from capability invariants, cascading-harm invariant deferred to a new deferred-decisions entry; Kano: must-have for procurement-grade audit defence and consumer-grade safety floor). D83 (workflow as architectural primitive at `contexts/workflow/` with Phase 2 implementation; five rejected alternatives weighed including workflow-as-refinement-of-agent-context, Phase-1 workflow implementation rejected on 3x scope grounds, LangGraph-specific definition shape rejected on orchestrator-portability grounds, methodology-and-workflow merged, cross-mode portability between workflow and agent; Kano: must-have on architectural commitments, performance on JSONB definition shape and CLI surface). D84 (P8 agent runtime with hand-rolled AgentLoopExecutor; four rejected alternatives weighed including LangGraph-at-P8 which doubles scope, OpenAI Agents SDK on provider-coupling grounds, workflow context at P8 without LangGraph as partial commitment, agent runtime defers to Phase 2 which contradicts bet's success criterion 2; Kano: performance on scope choice and adapter shape). D85 (McKinsey 7-Step methodology authoring placement as separate strategic block post-topology pre-P8 build; four rejected alternatives weighed including author-before-topology against undecided contract, author-during-topology doubling session scope, author-after-P8-complete missing the v2 exercise opportunity, defer-to-Phase-2 which misclassifies methodology-template work as workflow work; Kano: performance for placement choice).

- Tests: n/a. Documentation-only commit; `make lint` and `uv run pytest` not required.

- Reflection:

  Reflection prompt 1 (four-layer constraint stack with conditional methodology and workflow layers — does the captures' "each layer constrains layers below" framing resolve cleanly or surface latent tension). The four-layer stack resolves the captures' framing without surfacing latent tension at any boundary. The conditionality framing at the methodology and workflow layers is the load-bearing refinement: agents that do not adopt a methodology skip the methodology layer; agents not invoked within a workflow skip the workflow layer. Without conditionality, "each layer constrains layers below" would imply mandatory layers, contradicting D68's blank-agent path. The platform invariants layer is unconditional by construction (every entity in the system binds to it). The agent layer is per-instance and always present. The conditional layers in the middle accommodate the existing optionality (blank-create per D68) and the new optionality (agent invocation outside a workflow per D83). The architectural relationship is now explicit at D80 in a way it was not before; future sessions inheriting methodology, workflow, and agent contexts cannot resolve the layering inconsistently without contradicting a committed D-entry. The captures' synthesis framing "each layer constrains layers below" maps onto the new commitment as "each applicable upper layer constrains layers below for entities to which the upper layer applies," which is a tighter and more honest statement.

  Reflection prompt 2 (methodology v2 multi-role refinement — does it create downstream complexity at S26 migration the topology session did not anticipate). The S26 migration shape is straightforward but not trivial. The LVT template carries flat content fields at P7 close (system_prompt, source_ids, tool_allowlist, retrieval_strategy, filter_tree, top_k, min_score, model_selection); the migration wraps these in a single-element `roles` array with `role_name = "LVTGuide"` and adds null `source_filter` and `cost_ceiling`. The methodology hash chain re-anchors at genesis at migration time with explicit Alembic commentary. The agent context is unaffected at the content-field level per D75's flat-content-fields commitment; the agent's `source_methodology_role_name` field is the only new agent-context column, back-filled as "LVTGuide" for the PM agent. The migration touches one methodology template, one methodology revision, one agent template, and one agent revision (the PM agent has one revision at P7 close). Tenant-isolation contract tests need an update for the new role-bundle shape but the test harness pattern is the same as S23/S24. The complexity that the topology session did not fully anticipate is the methodology-hash-chain re-anchoring: re-anchoring at genesis is the simplest path but breaks any external system that depends on the prior hash, and Phase 1 has no such system (the audit chain at `contexts/audit/` is per-tenant and writes to per-tenant audit_records, which are separate from methodology revision hashes). The re-anchoring is structurally honest at S26 because methodology templates are control-plane-scoped and not part of any per-tenant audit chain; the methodology aggregate's own hash chain restarts at v2 with the migration commentary preserving auditability of the migration event itself.

  Reflection prompt 3 (platform-invariants five-set danger-targeted framing — procurement-defensible and consumer-credible at the same time). The five-set framing is both procurement-defensible and consumer-credible without compromise on either. Procurement-defensibility shape: each invariant maps to specific user-safety dimensions (the six dimensions from the captures' framing), the set is versioned in `charter/principles.md` per the existing append-only principle, and the dynamic-capability-posture framing distinguishes "what the platform does not enable today" from "permanent architectural commitments." Procurement audiences see a defended posture at a point in time with an evolution trajectory; this is stronger than either a fixed-set-forever posture (which over-commits) or an unenumerated discretion (which is undefensible). Consumer-credibility shape: the five invariants are danger-targeted at specific consumer-harm classes (financial harm, reputational harm via outbound communication, legal harm via terms acceptance, data destruction within Padhanam, privacy harm via tool-path leakage) rather than abstract principles. The consent-granularity principle resolves the tension between consumer expectations (auto-update calendar, document generation, draft creation as routine reversible actions with standing consent) and safety floor (per-transaction confirmation for financial; per-invocation for outbound communication; explicit user action for legal commitments). Padhanam-as-intelligence-layer is the commercial positioning that the consent-granularity principle accommodates; it differentiates from autonomous-agent platforms without forbidding all action. The five invariants are mechanically enforceable at the tool layer per D82's three-layer-enforcement framing; this matters because audit-grade readers see through performative principles (per D76's refinement to the forking principle).

  Reflection prompt 4 (McKinsey 7-Step placement per D85 — confirm). Confirmed. The 7-Step methodology authoring lands as a separate strategic block, post-topology-design and pre-P8 build sessions. The block produces one MethodologyTemplate row on control plane with one MethodologyRevision carrying seven role elements (ProblemFramer, Disaggregator, Prioritiser, Planner, Analyst, Synthesiser, Communicator). Each role's constraint bundle is authored against the methodology aggregate v2 shape per D81. Placement decisions weighed in D85's alternatives-considered: bundling into the topology session was rejected on scope-doubling grounds; authoring before the topology session was rejected on architecture-undecided grounds; authoring after P8 build complete was rejected on missed-opportunity grounds (the 7-Step exercises the multi-role surface before Phase 2 implementation work begins and may surface practical authoring friction with the role bundle JSONB shape). The placement is structurally consistent with the architectural-decision-work versus methodology-template-work distinction and operator capacity per the all-or-nothing posture.

  D-number renumbering note. The prompt as authored numbered the six new D-entries D79-D84; at execution the next available D-number was D80 because D79 was taken by the S25 close commit on cross-context create_agent_from_methodology. The entries landed as D80-D85 with cascading cross-reference updates throughout the strategic commit (decisions.md internal refs, workflow.md, p8-epic.md, principles.md, packages.md, roadmap.md, current-package.md, deferred-decisions.md, captures.md, and this session log entry). The renumbering is mechanical; the content is preserved verbatim from the prompt. The brief preservation at `briefs/p8/topology-design.md` notes the renumbering explicitly.

- methodology: Strategic-block-shaped session producing six D-entries plus a new charter file plus a new package epic note in a single commit. The framing-prompt-as-recommendation pattern held across the prompt's authoring (six D-entries' full text supplied as drafts with body content verbatim; renumbering applied mechanically). The D-number-conflict detection at session open (D79 already taken by S25) fired the verify-against-current-sources pattern symmetrically at strategic-mode session open, mirroring the pattern's prior firings at build-time brief review and system-touching-commit-time; second named instance at strategic-mode altitude (first was the brief reconciliation against decisions.md format style at the consumer-direction strategic block which corrected the bulleted template to the heading style). The pattern's recurrence test extends to a fourth context (build-brief review, source-document review, system-touching-commit-time, strategic-mode session open) and supports the phase-audit-promotion candidacy recommended at S25 close. No new methodology candidates surfaced from this strategic block; the session's load-bearing work was D-entry authoring rather than pattern observation.

## Strategic-mode: P8 role-first refinement and McKinsey 7-Step brief (2026-05-11)

roles: PM, architect

Single strategic-mode commit landing D86 (role-first refinement revising parts of D80, D81), principles additions (coach consistency, light UX, safety surface, phase-audit UX-and-safety verification), four deferred-decisions entries (learning store, revision mode, output aggregates, skills aggregate), S26 split into S26a and S26b, McKinsey 7-Step brief at briefs/p8/mckinsey-7-step.md, and current-package.md update.

The role-first refinement surfaced during a McKinsey 7-Step methodology authoring conversation. The operator's pushback on early framing (methodology-first; persona-shaped roles; methodology-defines-skills) iteratively refined the architecture toward role-first, methodology-as-playbook, skills-as-orthogonal capability. The pattern observed: methodology authoring exposes architectural assumptions that initial framing did not surface; brief-first authoring before code-time commits is the right shape.

The structural enforcement commitments (schema lacks the offending fields; aggregates separated to enforce concerns) align with the platform's existing mechanical-discipline posture. The risk surfaced earlier in the conversation that author discipline would be the load-bearing mechanism is replaced by architecture as the enforcement; authoring templates and a Phase 2 optional linter handle residual risk in role free-text fields.

Worth noting at the next phase audit: the methodology-authoring-exposes-architecture pattern as a Patterns-observed candidate. The S25 cross-context commit and this session's role-first refinement both surfaced during methodology-related work after architectural framing had already landed.

- Produced: Six commits closing the control-plane half of S26a per D86 (the original brief covered the full S26a scope; pre-write reconciliation split at the brief's commit-4-to-5 boundary into S26a-1 control-plane work and S26a-2 per-tenant work). Commit 3 (`feat(p8/s26a-1): add Role aggregate within methodology context`, 53bd4ed) shipped the role aggregate with full hexagonal layout: `contexts/methodology/domain/role.py` (RoleTemplate + RoleRevision frozen dataclasses), `contexts/methodology/ports/role_repository.py` (RoleRepositoryPort Protocol with five methods, no TenantContext per D33 control-plane scope), `contexts/methodology/application/use_cases.py` extended with five role use cases (create / get / list / update / retire) with a dedicated `_role_content_payload` helper kept separate from methodology's because the next commit diverges their content surfaces, and `contexts/methodology/adapters/outbound/postgres/role_repository.py` (RolePostgresRepository against the control-plane Postgres instance, mirroring MethodologyPostgresRepository's privileged_action-event-emitting shape from D74); 25 unit tests + 9 integration tests + api re-exports; import-linter's 23 contracts kept. Commit 4 (`feat(p8/s26a-1): refactor methodology_revisions to use role_refs`, 06ed41a) landed the methodology v3 refactor: Alembic revision 0006_methodology_role_refs (control plane) extracts each existing methodology revision's bundle into a freshly-minted role row pair, re-anchors methodology hashes against the new content surface (name, description, role_refs sorted by role_id), drops the bundle columns, tightens role_refs to NOT NULL; `MethodologyRevision` loses the bundle fields and gains role_refs: tuple[RoleRef, ...]; new RoleRef frozen dataclass; methodology use cases simplified to accept role_refs; `MethodologyView` shape stays identical (consumer surface) but is populated by the role-aware `MethodologyLookupAdapter` at `apps/cli/_cross_context.py` which closes over both methodology and role repositories and resolves role_refs[0] to a role revision at lookup time; `apps/cli/_agent.py` builds both repos at command construction and disposes both at exit; `apps/cli/_methodology.py`'s config shape changes from the nine-field bundle to role_refs; methodology domain + use-case + adapter integration + tenant isolation + CLI integration + cross-context adapters tests refactored; the S25 docker-based clone-and-edit e2e marked skip pending S26a-2's role CLI; 157 methodology/role/agent/CLI tests pass after the refactor. Commit 5 (`feat(p8/s26a-1): split LVT methodology into LVT + LVTGuide role`, b891024) landed Alembic 0007_lvt_split data-only migration (renames the auto-migrated LVTRole to LVTGuide with a canonical description, re-anchors the role's revision-1 hash because name + description span its hash payload per D74's chain-self-containment; no-op for fresh DBs and test environments that truncate the tables) plus a focused integration test at `tests/integration/contexts/methodology/test_lvt_round_trip.py` exercising the full role-first composition end-to-end through the MethodologyLookupAdapter resolution. This session-log entry is commit 6.

- Decisions: D86 (role-first architectural model — already landed at strategic-mode commit bc39515 ahead of build; build-mode session implements against it). No new D-entries this session.

- Tests: 157 methodology/role/agent/CLI/tenant-isolation tests pass after the refactor (429 unit tests across the whole codebase; the 9 broader-suite failures in tests/integration/contexts/ingestion and tests/integration/evaluation are pre-existing Neo4j/LiteLLM environmental issues unrelated to S26a-1). Three control-plane Alembic migrations (0005, 0006, 0007) verified forward and back against the live control-plane Postgres. Import-linter's 23 contracts kept; AST enforcement passes (9 tests).

- methodology: brief-first authoring is becoming a productive pattern. The S26a-1 brief landed at strategic-mode commit `bc39515` ahead of build; the role aggregate's shape, the methodology refactor's content surface, the migration sequence, and the LVT split commitment were all clarified before any code was written. Build-time deviations (the three pre-write reconciliations) lived in the session-log reflection per the methodology document's "no in-place brief edits" rule, leaving the brief stable and the deviations auditable.

Reflection (four prompts per the brief):

1. **Migration discipline.** First v1-to-v3 schema jump (skipping the never-built v2 from D81). The constraint-bundle-to-role extraction was lossless for the in-flight single-role case: NULL fields normalised through the prior schema's NOT NULL coverage, empty arrays stayed empty, the migration's per-methodology auto-named role row carried the full bundle verbatim. The migration order (role tables → methodology role_refs → LVT split) avoided foreign key issues because role rows landed before the methodology_revisions UPDATE that references them; the LVT rename came last because by then the role row was structurally stable. Hash re-anchoring at the migration boundary is the right shape for content-surface refactors: both methodology revisions (now spanning role_refs instead of the bundle) and role revisions (after LVT rename) get recomputed against the new payload, so chain integrity is byte-equivalent to what a use-case-driven path would produce. Multi-revision rename — the role's revision-1 only recompute case is acceptable at Phase 1 because the auto-migrated role has exactly revision 1; future renames of a role with multiple revisions would need to walk the chain and recompute forward.

2. **Cross-context port pattern at scale.** Agent context now has cross-context ports for both methodology (`MethodologyLookup` per D79) and source (`SourceLookup` per D79); S26a-2 will add a third (`RoleLookup`). The api-facade-via-callable pattern held up at the second consumer (the role-aware MethodologyLookupAdapter at apps/cli/_cross_context.py still takes the consumer's principal and returns a consumer-shaped DTO; the adapter just now closes over two repositories instead of one). Consumer-side DTO discipline (MethodologyView stays in agent's application layer) avoided cross-context type imports cleanly; the adapter does the methodology+role join at the wiring layer, keeping the agent use case shape-stable. The pattern is shaping up as a P8 invariant worth pinning at the phase audit: "consumer contexts define their own consumer-shaped DTOs; producer contexts expose use cases at api.py; wiring adapters at apps/cli translate producer aggregates to consumer DTOs."

3. **D86 idealization versus implementation.** D86 specified `source_filter` and `cost_ceiling` on the role bundle. S26a-1 implemented with the existing `source_ids` (mirroring D74's methodology shape) and omitted `cost_ceiling` (forward-affordance lives at tenant-registry level per D41). This is the second instance of architectural-idealization-vs-implementation-reality after S23's D74 jsonb-shape reconciliation (the brief specified `JSONB(astext_type=sa.Text())` but the actual code precedent used the bare form). Both instances had the same shape: the D-entry described what the architecture should commit to in concept; the implementation used what was already in place to avoid introducing concepts without consumers. Worth tracking at the next phase audit as a Patterns-observed candidate if a third instance fires; the pattern would promote to a principle: "D-entry choice text is the architectural commitment; mechanical-form details defer to existing precedent unless the D-entry explicitly contradicts them."

4. **Brief-first authoring proving valuable.** The McKinsey 7-Step brief at `briefs/p8/mckinsey-7-step.md` from strategic-mode commit `bc39515` is the reference for S26b's authoring against the role-first model. The brief's existence reduced uncertainty in S26a-1: D86's role-first refinement (specifying that roles are first-class with constraint bundle and methodologies compose them via role_refs) was already concrete by build time, so the question "what does the role aggregate carry?" had a settled answer. The brief did NOT reduce all uncertainty — the field-name reconciliation (`source_filter` vs `source_ids`) needed build-time decision because D86's text described an ideal, not the implementation. Brief-first is proving its value, but its productive shape is at the architectural-commitment level (which aggregates exist, which constraints they carry conceptually, how they compose) rather than at the field-name level. Promoting this observation to methodology: the brief settles concept; pre-write reconciliation settles mechanics.




## P8 — S26a-2 — Agent role lineage, role lookup, role CLI; S26a closes
roles: architect, engineer, PM
mode: build

- Produced: Seven commits closing the per-tenant + cross-plane half of S26a per D86. Commit 2 (`feat(p8/s26a-2): add source_role_id lineage to agent_templates`, bba68d4) added the per-tenant Alembic revision 0009_agent_role_lineage with a cross-plane backfill that opens a sync sqlalchemy engine against the control-plane Postgres from inside the tenant migration to read methodology revisions' role_refs and populate the tenant's role lineage pair; forward-and-back-verified against tenant a's live Postgres. The PM agent's role lineage came down NULL because its methodology revision was missing on the control plane in the current dev-stack state (orphaned-lineage path, structurally sound under the three-valid-state invariant). Commit 3 (`feat(p8/s26a-2): agent domain gains role lineage; MethodologyView carries resolved role`, 4fdc43b) extended `AgentTemplate` with the two new fields and the D86 paired-NULL invariant enforced independently of D75's methodology pair; extended `MethodologyView` with role_id + role_version + the adapter populates them by reading the resolved role on role_refs[0]; `create_agent_from_methodology` now populates both lineage pairs from a single cross-context hop; AgentPostgresRepository's Core Table + insert values + materialiser cover the new columns; CLI surface (human + JSON renderers) shows both new fields. Commit 4 (`feat(p8/s26a-2): RoleLookup port, create_agent_from_role, RoleLookupAdapter`, a65d35c) added the second cross-context port at `contexts/agent/application/ports/role_lookup.py` mirroring MethodologyLookup's shape (api-facade-via-callable per D17), a RoleView consumer-side DTO, the `create_agent_from_role` use case persisting role-pair-populated-methodology-pair-NULL agents per D86's first-class-role posture, and a RoleLookupAdapter wrapping `get_role_template`. Commit 5 (`feat(p8/s26a-2): padhanam role CLI namespace and agent create-from-role`, 8cacc56) shipped the five `padhanam role` commands (create, get, list, update, archive) and `padhanam agent create-from-role`; verified end-to-end against the live tenant a stack (role create + list + get JSON, agent create-from-role landing UUID with role lineage populated and methodology lineage NULL). Commit 6 (`chore(p8/s26a-2): restore docker-based clone-and-edit e2e against the role-aware shape`, 4a39f78) rewrote `tests/e2e/agent/test_create_from_methodology_flow.py` end-to-end: the test now creates an LVTGuide role + LVT methodology + ingests two sources + drains to indexed + clones an agent from the methodology (both lineage pairs populate) + clones a second agent directly from the role (role pair only) + edits the methodology-cloned agent + asserts chain advancement and template independence + cleans up. 103s end-to-end against the live Compose stack; previously-skipped test runs and passes. This session-log entry is commit 7.

- Decisions: No new D-entries. S26a-2 implemented against D86's role-first model already landed at S26a-1 framing.

- Tests: 561 unit + integration + tenant-isolation + AST tests pass alongside 23 import-linter contracts kept. 12 unit tests + 3 adapter integration tests added for `create_agent_from_role` and `RoleLookup`/`RoleView`; 4 domain-invariant tests added covering the role-pair paired-NULL invariant; 1 tenant-isolation contract test covering the new column; 1 repository integration test covering the third valid lineage state. The docker-based e2e moved from @pytest.mark.skip to passing in 103s.

- methodology: pre-write reconciliation discipline did substantive load-bearing work this session. The brief's eighth pre-write reconciliation step ("Verify the `MethodologyView` from S26a-1 already carries the resolved role's `role_id` and `role_version`") surfaced the gap at brief-review time (the view did NOT carry these); the gap was promoted to a user-question, the user selected the extension path, and the session proceeded against the corrected assumption without rework. This is the second instance of pre-write reconciliation catching a load-bearing brief misalignment (S26a-1's three reconciliations were the first). The discipline is shaping up as a methodology-document Patterns-observed candidate worth tracking at phase audit time; promotion threshold is one further instance per the existing methodology-promotion convention.

Reflection (four prompts per the brief):

1. **Paired-NULL invariant at two pairs.** D75 introduced paired-null for methodology lineage; S26a-2 extends to role lineage as a second independent pair. The invariant structure generalises cleanly: one CHECK constraint per pair at the schema layer (`agent_templates_lineage_paired_null` for methodology, `agent_templates_role_lineage_paired_null` for role), one `__post_init__` clause per pair at the domain layer, one tenant-isolation contract test per pair at the schema-isolation layer. The three-valid-state matrix (both NULL, both populated, role-only) is small enough to enumerate in prose and in test parametrisation; the fourth combination (methodology populated, role NULL) is structurally impossible because methodology-based creation always resolves at least one role per D86 — the brief flagged this and the schema doesn't add a redundant CHECK to prevent it. A third lineage pair (e.g., skills at Phase 2) would extend the pattern to three independent pairs with three CHECK constraints, three `__post_init__` clauses, and a 2^3 valid-state matrix. The pattern accommodates a third pair without restructuring; whether the eight-state matrix remains audit-friendly is the open question. The complexity ceiling is not at three pairs but at the point where the cross-pair relationships become non-independent (e.g., if a skills pair could only populate when a role pair is populated, that's a tightening invariant requiring its own CHECK and its own `__post_init__` clause). For now, the pattern is robust at two pairs and remains compositional.

2. **Cross-context ports at second consumer.** Agent context now hosts two cross-context lookup ports: `MethodologyLookup` (D79, S25) and `RoleLookup` (S26a-2). Both implement the api-facade-via-callable pattern per D17; both have consumer-side DTOs (`MethodologyView`, `RoleView`) in the same `application/ports/` directory; both have adapters at `apps/cli/_cross_context.py` that close over a producer repository and synthesise a Protocol surface; both have AST-backstop tests asserting the port module does not import from `contexts.methodology` or `contexts.ingestion`. The second consumer reinforced the pattern's value: the wiring overhead at the CLI layer is modest (a few constructor lines per command), the consumer-side DTO discipline kept the agent context's import graph clean, and the structural symmetry made `RoleLookupAdapter` straightforward to author with `MethodologyLookupAdapter` as a reference. The structural duplication is visible but not yet costly: a shared "cross-context-lookup pattern" abstraction would compress two adapters into one but at the cost of obscuring per-port differences (single-hop role lookup vs join-resolution methodology lookup). A third port with the same shape — single-hop, version-resolving, consumer-DTO-shaped — would tip the balance toward abstraction. Promotion candidate for the Patterns-observed list if a third such port lands.

3. **Docker e2e restoration as integration check.** The S25 skip decorator was deferred-integration-commitment shaped: it carried a precise restoration trigger ("once `padhanam role` CLI becomes operator-callable") and a precise reason for the deferral (the prior test body wrote a pre-v3 methodology config). The restoration at S26a-2 surfaced two facts the unit tests could not: first, that the create-from-methodology flow actually populates both lineage pairs end-to-end against a live data plane (the unit tests asserted this against in-memory fakes but the integration check at the CLI + Postgres boundary is stricter); second, that the create-from-role flow produces a methodology-NULL agent against the live data plane. The pattern of "skip with a precise restoration trigger plus a precise reason" is shaping up as more useful than "delete and re-author later" because the trigger surfaces at the right session naturally (S26a-2's CLI work was the trigger), and the reason for the skip is preserved as code-located audit context rather than as a session-log paragraph that can be forgotten. The pattern is worth tracking as a methodology candidate if it fires again — e.g., if S28b adds a skipped e2e for tool-registry-aware agent invocation with a restoration trigger naming S29b's SSE streaming.

4. **S26a closure: the split-at-implementation-reality call held up.** S26a-1's framing decision to split S26a into control-plane and per-tenant halves yielded two sessions of comparable scope (S26a-1: six commits, three control-plane Alembic migrations, role aggregate authoring, methodology v3 refactor, LVT split, MethodologyLookupAdapter role-awareness, round-trip integration test; S26a-2: seven commits, per-tenant Alembic with cross-plane backfill, AgentTemplate role-lineage extension, MethodologyView extension, RoleLookup port + use case + adapter + CLI, padhanam agent create-from-role, docker e2e restoration). Neither session felt rushed nor padded. The cut point — the brief's commit-4-to-5 boundary between control-plane and per-tenant work — held up because per-tenant work depended on control-plane substrate (the methodology revision's role_refs needed to exist before the per-tenant migration's backfill could read it) but did not depend on control-plane substrate beyond what already existed in the database after S26a-1. The cut point's structural property was "the per-tenant work has a stable interface to the control-plane work, and the interface is the methodology revision's role_refs column." Future split decisions on similarly-shaped sessions should look for the same structural property: a stable interface between halves that's already in code at split time, not requiring coordination changes mid-split. The discipline generalises beyond control-plane-vs-per-tenant cuts; it applies wherever a session boundary can sit on a stable structural interface rather than on time-of-completion.




## P8 — S26b — McKinsey 7-Step methodology authoring with D87 override-mode substrate refinement

Commit 3 (`feat(p8/s26b): add 0008_create_mckinsey_7_step data migration on control plane`, a40e2a1) authored the Alembic data migration inserting seven `role_templates` plus seven `role_revisions` (revision 1 chained from `GENESIS_REVISION_HASH`) plus one `methodology_templates` plus one `methodology_revisions` (revision 1) on the control-plane track; each role carries the substrate-mapped bundle (`source_ids=[]`, `tool_allowlist=[]`, `retrieval_strategy={"strategy":"parallel_rrf","params":{}}`, `filter_tree={"node":{}}`, `top_k=8`, `min_score=Decimal("0.5")`, `model_selection="qwen2.5:7b"`) and the brief's function-focused system_prompt verbatim; the methodology revision's `role_refs` JSONB carries the seven role references in the brief's sequential order with each entry's `overrides` at the D87 structured shape `{"system_prompt": {"mode": "augment", "value": "<brief addition>"}}` verbatim from the brief's overrides table; forward-and-back-verified against live control-plane Postgres (head moved 0007_lvt_split → 0008_create_mckinsey_7_step; downgrade -1 dropped the eight rows by the migration's actor sentinel; re-apply head was idempotent via the name-guard). Commit 4 (`feat(p8/s26b): add integration test exercising McKinsey methodology resolution end-to-end`, 27202d2) landed `tests/integration/contexts/methodology/test_mckinsey_resolution.py` with five tests: methodology+seven-role-refs presence with names in brief order; structured augment-mode override verbatim per role with exactly one `system_prompt` key per role_ref; golden-hash equality between each role's persisted revision-1 hash and what `_role_content_payload + compute_revision_hash` would produce via the application-layer helper; golden-hash equality for the methodology's revision-1 hash over `(name, description, role_refs sorted by role_id, overrides canonically serialised)`; MethodologyLookupAdapter end-to-end resolution of the first role_ref (ProblemFramer per brief order). The same commit fixed four pre-existing methodology integration test fixtures (`test_lvt_round_trip.py`, `test_methodology_commands.py`, `test_repository.py`, `test_role_repository.py`) to scope truncation to test-authored rows (`created_by_user_id NOT LIKE 'migration:%'`); without the fix, the order in which integration tests run wiped the migration-owned McKinsey rows before the McKinsey test read them. The structural framing: migration-owned rows are session state; test-authored rows are per-test state. This session-log entry is commit 5.

- Decisions: D87 (override-mode space and structured on-disk overrides). The decision surfaced at the pre-write reconciliation moment when the McKinsey 7-Step brief's "system_prompt addition" semantics for each of the seven roles did not match D86's "soft fields replace" framing literally; the user-question moment elevated the choice from "encode mode in the migration's overrides shape" to "amend the substrate's binding-mode taxonomy with a structurally-honest mode space". Three alternatives weighed (keyed addition, flat field-keyed with implicit mode, hold-D86-and-rewrite-the-brief) and rejected; one fourth alternative (defer overrides entirely to S27b) rejected because the brief AC #7 names per-role overrides as load-bearing for content fidelity. Default modes committed per field at the substrate layer; admissibility validated at parse time; resolver semantics defer to S27b's agent runtime. Kano: must-have for the brief's authoring fidelity at the first authoring-evidence moment; performance for the architectural commitment.

- Tests: 599 tests pass across unit + contract + methodology integration + CLI integration + AST enforcement at session close. Test-count delta from S26b commit 1 framing baseline: 24 new D87 substrate unit tests at commit 2 (17 in `test_overrides.py` covering the per-field default-mode table, admissibility-pair parametrisation, parser projection from flat to structured, structured pass-through, drop-None-values, unknown-field and unknown-mode rejection, treat-non-structured-as-flat semantics; 3 in `test_use_cases.py` covering empty-overrides-canonicalise-to-None for byte-stability, populated-overrides-pass-through-canonical-form, populated-vs-empty hash distinction; 4 in `test_methodology.py` for the refactored RoleRef defaulting to empty dict); 5 new McKinsey integration tests at commit 4; four pre-existing methodology integration fixtures updated to scope truncation to non-migration actors. `make migrate` head moved 0007_lvt_split → 0008_create_mckinsey_7_step (forward and back verified against live control-plane Postgres at 127.0.0.1:5433). McKinsey methodology revision-1 hash and seven role revision-1 hashes computed inline via `compute_revision_hash` against the canonical content payloads from the application-layer helpers; the integration test's golden-hash assertions confirm byte-equivalence between the migration-computed and use-case-computed hashes. Import-linter: 23 contracts kept, 0 broken. AST enforcement: 9 tests pass.

- methodology: D87 emerged through pre-write reconciliation, not through up-front strategic design. The McKinsey brief's "system_prompt addition" semantics for each of the seven roles surfaced a load-bearing gap in D86's "soft fields replace" framing when Claude Code drafted the user-question on override shape. The user-question moment elevated the choice from "migration shape" to "substrate commitment", and the operator selected the structured-per-field-with-explicit-mode option that became D87. The pattern observed: pre-write reconciliation can surface architectural gaps the original strategic framing missed, and the user-question discipline turns the gap into a charter-grade refinement rather than an in-flight workaround. This is the third instance of the pre-write-reconciliation-as-architectural-discovery pattern (S26a-1 surfaced three reconciliations resolved at brief-time; S26a-2 surfaced the MethodologyView-extension reconciliation; S26b surfaces D87 as a full new D-entry from a single user-question moment). Recurrence threshold satisfied at three instances; recommend formal phase-audit promotion to `charter/methodology.md` at Phase 1 close.

Reflection (five prompts per the brief):

1. **D87 surfacing through brief reconciliation.** D87 surfaced exactly where the brief said it would — at pre-write reconciliation, before any migration code was written. The McKinsey brief's per-role "system_prompt addition" wording read as augment-mode-on-soft-field, but D86's sub-commitment (b) committed soft fields to replace. The mismatch had three resolution paths (encode addition in keyed names; hold D86 and rewrite brief to use full replacement prompts; refine D86 with a richer mode space). The user-question pattern surfaced the three options as Option A / B / C and asked the operator to choose; Option B was the structurally-honest choice that became D87. The pattern observed: committing architectural decisions ahead of authoring evidence is not the same as committing them in a way that survives first authoring evidence. D86 was structurally sound (role-first model, methodology-as-playbook, skills-as-orthogonal-capability all held) but its sub-commitments on binding mode were thinner than the strategic-block framing acknowledged. The light-UX-as-authoring-discipline principle from `charter/principles.md` did the load-bearing work at the user-question moment: the third option's reasoning ("doubles authoring cost on every methodology and breaks the role-first model's clean separation") was the deciding consideration. Discipline pattern: when first authoring evidence surfaces a gap, treat the user-question as a charter-grade refinement opportunity rather than an in-flight workaround.

2. **Content authoring against a stable substrate, refined mid-session.** S26b started in the original prompt as content-only authoring against a stable substrate; D87's surfacing turned it into content authoring plus a substrate refinement. The five-commit shape kept the refinement scope disciplined: commit 1 lands D87 charter-side; commit 2 lands D87 code-side (substrate plus parser plus encoder plus materialiser plus 24 unit tests); commit 3 lands the McKinsey content as a data migration; commit 4 lands the integration test asserting the content; commit 5 closes. The cut point between substrate refinement (commits 1–2) and content authoring (commits 3–4) held cleanly because the substrate work was independently testable via the LVT byte-equivalence assertion (empty overrides canonicalise to `null` in canonical form regardless of whether any methodology populates them). The fixture-update side-quest at commit 4 (four integration test fixtures updated to scope truncation to non-migration actors) was unforeseen at brief time but is a structurally honest finding: it surfaces a previously-implicit testing-pattern weakness (test fixtures wiping migration-owned rows) that the McKinsey integration test exposed only because the migration owns persistent content rather than test-authored content. The substrate's authoring affordances held at the seven-role scale; the parser projection accepted both flat and structured authoring inputs cleanly; the canonical encoder preserved byte-stability for the no-op case. No scope creep into adjacent architectural work — the refinement stayed within D86's role-first model without re-litigating the four-layer constraint stack or the workflow-as-Phase-2 commitment.

3. **Role authoring discipline at scale.** The brief's seven function-focused system prompts held against the temptation to include procedural content. Each role's system_prompt describes function (job, inputs, outputs, responsibilities) without methodology-specific procedure (SCQ, MECE, impact-tractability matrix, pyramid principle). The procedural content lives in the methodology's overrides, not in the role's system_prompt. The augment-mode-as-default for system_prompt earned its keep here: it encourages the structural separation between standalone role identity (the function the role does) and methodology overlay (the technique the methodology brings). A future methodology re-using ProblemFramer (e.g., a Toyota Production System variant of problem-solving with A3-shaped framing rather than SCQ) can compose the same role with its own augment-mode addition; the role's standalone identity survives. The per-role overrides specialise without diluting: at adopt-from-role-directly time (no methodology context), the agent inherits the function-focused prompt and the McKinsey-specific specialisation never reaches it. The augment-mode commitment is the architectural enforcement of the structural separation D86 sub-commitment (e) named.

4. **Hash computation in migrations and serialisation determinism.** The byte-equivalence check between migration-computed and use-case-computed hashes surfaced one important property: canonical JSON serialisation is content-determined, not call-path-determined. The migration's inlined `_role_content_payload` and `_methodology_content_payload` helpers produce byte-equivalent canonical JSON to the application-layer helpers at `contexts/methodology/application/use_cases.py` for any input the integration test reads back from Postgres. The LVT no-op case (empty overrides) hashed byte-equivalent because the canonical encoder maps empty `dict` to `None` in the canonical form, matching the pre-D87 LVT row's `null` serialisation. The McKinsey populated case hashed byte-equivalent because the structured `{mode, value}` form serialises deterministically via the canonical encoder's sorted-keys-and-format-f convention. One subtlety: the migration's `uuid4()` for role and methodology IDs means re-running the migration after downgrade produces fresh UUIDs and therefore a different methodology hash (the methodology hash payload includes the resolved role_ids). Role content hashes are stable across cycles because the role payload doesn't include role_id. The integration test handles this correctly by reading role_ids back from the persisted rows before recomputing — the assertion is "given the actual persisted state, the canonical-JSON-derived hash matches" rather than "the hash is some fixed golden value". The serialisation is platform-stable across the migration boundary in the strict sense that matters for chain integrity: the persisted hash equals what the use-case path would compute for the persisted content.

5. **Workflow specification without runtime.** The methodology declares a sequential workflow per D83 (ProblemFramer → Disaggregator → ... → Communicator) but the workflow context is Phase 2 implementation; the role_refs ordering is structurally sequential but not runtime-executed. The brief's workflow specification translated cleanly into the methodology's storage shape because the storage carries the role references in authoring order without committing to any runtime semantics — the storage is honest about what it represents (a sequence of role references) without claiming what it does (workflow execution). The augment-mode-defers-to-resolver pattern (S27b consumes the override) is the same shape at the constraint-bundle level: S26b commits storage and validation; S27b commits resolution. The broader framing — substrate stores authored content; consumer layer resolves at runtime — holds up at first scale. The structural property that makes this work: the substrate's storage is content-determined, not consumer-determined. A methodology with seven roles and seven augment-mode overrides has a deterministic on-disk shape (and a deterministic content hash) regardless of whether any consumer ever reads it. The asymmetry between storage-now and resolution-later is honest at the layer boundary and does not produce friction at the authoring boundary. It would produce friction if the storage shape required consumer commitments to be definitionally complete (e.g., if the JSONB schema had to specify the runtime composition algorithm) — but the D87 shape is consumer-agnostic at storage time. The pattern generalises: any substrate that captures authoring intent without committing to consumer semantics buys decoupling at the cost of one additional layer (the resolver) that has to be authored when the consumer surfaces. For S26b the cost is acceptable; for unbounded deferral of consumer layers, the cost compounds (Phase 2 will need to commit on the resolver semantics).

Commit 4 (`feat(p8/s27b): add D87 override-mode resolver at contexts/agent/application/composition.py`, 0020ebf) shipped `compose_effective_constraint_bundle` plus 20 unit tests covering every (field, mode) admissibility per D87 plus the LVT no-overrides path plus the McKinsey ProblemFramer augment case plus malformed-entry defence; the two-newline separator constant `SYSTEM_PROMPT_AUGMENT_SEPARATOR` lives in the module. Commit 5 (`feat(p8/s27b): add AgentLoopExecutor adapter; widen AuditPort.emit to return persisted event; surface per-call cost on Completion`, d66f763) shipped `AgentLoopExecutor` at `contexts/agent/adapters/outbound/agent_loop_executor.py` implementing the hand-rolled LLM-with-tool-loop with `MAX_ITERATIONS=10` and retrieval-as-only-callable per D88, plus widened `AuditPort.emit` to return `AuditEvent` so the agent runtime captures the persisted event's authoritative `this_event_hash` for surfacing on `AgentResult.audit_start_hash + audit_end_hash`, plus extended `Completion` with `cost_usd: Decimal` populated from the existing `cost_for` pricing-table lookup in the LiteLLM adapter. Commit 6 (`feat(p8/s27b): add invoke_agent use case at contexts/agent/application/use_cases.py`, 7a0bbaa) shipped `invoke_agent` with three lineage paths (blank-created uses agent revision content as role view; role-cloned re-fetches via RoleLookup; methodology-cloned re-fetches role plus pulls per-role overrides via MethodologyOverridesLookup) plus auth posture matching the existing D75 CRUD use cases (tenant-context-or-operator-context) plus 5 unit tests. Commit 7 (`feat(p8/s27b): wire apps/cli adapters for new ports plus tenant-isolation contract extension for the agent runtime audit path`, 618cf63) wired `AgentRetrievalClientAdapter` (translating role's `retrieval_strategy.primary` into ingestion `search_vector` or `traverse_graph` with `min_score` post-filtering) and `MethodologyOverridesLookupAdapter` (scanning methodology revision's `role_refs` for matching `role_id`) at `apps/cli/_cross_context.py`, plus a tenant-isolation contract test at `tests/contract/tenant_isolation/test_agent_isolation.py::test_agent_invocation_audit_isolated_per_tenant` exercising AgentLoopExecutor against the synthetic dual-tenant Postgres infrastructure with the real PostgresAuditAdapter. Commit 8 (`feat(p8/s27b): add integration test exercising McKinsey ProblemFramer agent end-to-end against live stack`, 530162d) landed `tests/integration/contexts/agent/test_invoke_agent_end_to_end.py` running in-container via `docker compose exec`; the test drives `create_agent_from_methodology` against the live McKinsey methodology, then `invoke_agent` against the real LiteLLM gateway + Qwen 2.5 7B via Ollama per D15, asserts the augment composition (role base + "\n\n" + SCQ override) reaches the LLM in the system message, asserts non-empty content + `termination_reason="content"` + cost parses as Decimal, asserts two audit rows (start + end) with intact hash chain matching the AgentResult's surfaced hashes; the same commit updated `compose.yaml`'s padhanam-api digest pin from `sha256:e65afe7a...` to `sha256:108f92b9...` reflecting the rebuilt image carrying the agent runtime code. This session-log entry is commit 9.

- Decisions: D88 (Agent runtime architecture — AgentExecutor port, AgentLoopExecutor adapter, tool-call extension to InferencePort, agent-context retrieval and overrides ports, D87 resolver at agent context, retrieval-as-only-callable at Phase 1). D88 absorbs four architectural sub-choices resolved at the pre-write user-question moment: (1) extending the existing InferencePort for tool calls rather than duplicating the LLM seam at the agent context; (2) defining an agent-context `AgentRetrievalClient` Protocol with strategy translation at the wiring layer rather than direct cross-context import; (3) defining a new runtime-time `MethodologyOverridesLookup` port distinct from the clone-time `MethodologyLookup`; (4) the two-newline augment separator for `system_prompt`, with the reflection prompt at session close as the forcing function for richer framing if LLM behaviour demands. The composition resolver placement at agent context (not methodology context) follows the cross-context port pattern established at S26a-2. Alternatives considered and rejected per D88: LangGraph at P8 (deferred to Phase 2 per D84); provider-specific agent SDKs; no-tool-loop at S27b; D87 resolver at methodology context; new agent-context-shaped tool-aware chat port duplicating the LLM seam; AgentLoopExecutor consuming the ingestion RetrievalClient directly; extending MethodologyLookup to take role_id for runtime; extending MethodologyView to carry the full role_refs list; richer augment-separator framing. Kano: must-have for the bet's success criterion 2 at Phase 1 close (one agent running); performance on the inference context extension (the tool-call shape generalises beyond agent runtime) and the three agent-context ports (the workflow context at Phase 2 reuses); delighter on the consumer-port-plus-wiring-adapter pattern's third reinforcement (MethodologyLookup at S26a-1, RoleLookup at S26a-2, AgentRetrievalClient + MethodologyOverridesLookup at S27b).

- Tests: 619 tests pass across unit + tenant-isolation contract + AST enforcement at session close (up from 599 at S26b close; +20 net after the test rationalisation in commit 2 retired one fake-port test pattern). Plus the new integration test `test_invoke_agent_end_to_end.py` passes against the live stack with a real Qwen 2.5 7B call via Ollama. New test surfaces this session: 6 LiteLLM tool-call tests + 1 request_completion threading test (commit 2); 8 executor DTO tests + 3 effective bundle tests (commit 3); 20 D87 composition resolver tests (commit 4); 10 AgentLoopExecutor unit tests (commit 5); 5 invoke_agent unit tests (commit 6); 7 cross-context adapter integration tests + 1 tenant-isolation contract test for agent.invoke audit chain isolation (commit 7); 1 end-to-end integration test against the live LiteLLM gateway (commit 8). Import-linter: 23 contracts kept, 0 broken (no new contracts needed — the agent context's internal layers continue to import nothing from contexts.methodology or contexts.ingestion; AgentRetrievalClient + MethodologyOverridesLookup follow the consumer-port-plus-wiring-adapter pattern that the AST enforcement test backstops at `tests/unit/contexts/agent/test_import_isolation.py`). AST enforcement: 9 tests pass; the new `contexts/agent/adapters/outbound/agent_loop_executor.py` imports from `contexts/inference/domain` and `contexts/inference/ports` and `contexts/audit/domain` which are legitimate inbound dependencies the agent context's adapter layer is allowed to take. The augment composition's golden assertion in the integration test confirms byte-level: the LiteLLM-bound system message contains the ProblemFramer role's function-focused base prompt + `"\n\n"` + the McKinsey SCQ override verbatim from the methodology revision's `role_refs` entry — the D87 resolver semantics deferred at S26b have now executed end-to-end at first authoring-evidence-meets-runtime.

- methodology: Pre-write reconciliation surfaced four structural mismatches against the prompt's substrate assumptions, each promoted to a charter-grade D88 sub-choice via the user-question pattern: (1) `InferencePort` was sync without tool-call surface (vs. the prompt's "if the current adapter is too thin... surface to user-question"); (2) `RetrievalClient` was split (search_vector + traverse_graph) per D5/D65's explicit "hybrid is an agent-layer concern" rather than the prompt's assumed unified `retrieve()`; (3) `MethodologyView` carried only `role_refs[0]`'s content at clone time rather than the per-role overrides invoke_agent needs at runtime; (4) augment-separator confirmation. The pattern observed: the brief's "promote structural mismatches to user-questions rather than guessing" discipline produced four pre-code architectural commitments captured in D88, which then constrained the eight-commit code work into a clean structural shape that the integration test exercised end-to-end on first attempt against the live stack. This is the fourth instance of pre-write-reconciliation-as-architectural-discovery (S26a-1 brief-time three reconciliations; S26a-2 MethodologyView extension; S26b D87 emergence; S27b D88 emergence) — the discipline pattern is now load-bearing for the methodology-implementer mode and warrants formal phase-audit promotion to `charter/methodology.md`.

Reflection (three prompts per the brief):

1. **D87 resolver under live composition.** The two-newline separator produced a useful effective prompt for the LiteLLM call against Qwen 2.5 7B. The integration test's assertion (role base appears before SCQ override, joined by `"\n\n"`, both fragments distinct and identifiable) confirmed the structural shape held; the LLM produced a non-empty content response that terminated at iteration 1 (no tool calls because tool_allowlist is empty per the McKinsey role bundle), suggesting the augmented prompt was directly actionable. No evidence yet that richer framing (named sections, XML wrapping) is needed; the operator can collect richer evidence at S30b's end-to-end demo where multiple McKinsey roles get exercised against operator-uploaded sources. The composition algorithm held up at the first authoring-evidence-meets-runtime moment: augment for `system_prompt` concatenates cleanly; the LVT no-overrides path returns role base unchanged; the McKinsey-with-overrides path produces a coherent composed prompt. The structural property that worked: composition operates per-field rather than per-bundle, so a methodology overriding `system_prompt` only doesn't touch `tool_allowlist`, `retrieval_strategy`, etc. The honest framing: a single-role single-augment-field test exercises only a fraction of D87's mode space; the other modes (tighten on list-shaped, tighten on numeric, replace on retrieval_strategy) have unit-test coverage but not live-LLM coverage. The reflection prompt's "before McKinsey's other six roles get exercised at S30b's end-to-end demo" framing is the right cadence: substantive refinement of the algorithm should wait for the multi-role evidence S30b produces.

2. **Tool-loop control flow with retrieval-as-only-callable.** The hand-rolled loop's control-flow shape held up cleanly at the first live invocation. Branching on `completion.tool_calls` empty-vs-populated produced unambiguous termination on the McKinsey case (the role's empty `tool_allowlist` after composition meant the executor registered no tools with the InferencePort, so the model produced content on first turn and the loop terminated with `TerminationReason.CONTENT`). The unknown-tool branch (covered at unit-test level) and the max-iterations branch (also unit-test) are structural defences not exercised by the McKinsey path. The extension points S28b's tool registry should preserve: (a) per-tool result formatting — currently the retrieval tool's chunks format via `_format_chunks_as_tool_result`; the registry will need an equivalent per-tool formatter (or a default formatter consumers can override); (b) classification-driven invariant checks per D82 — the current loop has no place to inject per-tool invariants (financial-execution authorization, outbound-communication consent, etc.); S28b will need an injection point before tool invocation; (c) the iteration cap as a per-role concern — the universal `MAX_ITERATIONS = 10` will need lifting to role-configurable when a methodology demands long horizons. The loop's structural cleanliness comes from the early-termination flag plus the four-string `TerminationReason` enum: every termination mode is named and surfaced on `AgentResult`, so audit-payload composition is uniform. No structural restructuring needed before S28b; the substrate is ready for tool-registry generalisation.

3. **Cost and audit at invocation level.** The OTel span aggregation pattern produced per-invocation cost numbers that match the LiteLLM-side calculation byte-equivalent — the per-call `cost_usd` on `Completion` is the same Decimal that the OTel span's `gen_ai.cost.total_usd` attribute carries (both come from the same `cost_for` lookup). The agent runtime's aggregation is a straightforward Decimal sum across per-call costs; no discrepancies surfaced. For the Qwen 2.5 7B dev path, all costs are zero per D62 (Ollama-served zero-rate pricing); the structure is correct and the assertion in the integration test passed with `Decimal("0") >= Decimal("0")`. The cost-query path from D57 will need extension at a future session to read per-invocation aggregates from the trace store (Langfuse); D88 names this as a follow-on but defers the specific extension shape. The two-row audit pattern (start, end) proved adequate at the integration-test scale: one row at invocation entry with the input hash plus lineage metadata, one row at invocation exit with the response hash plus termination plus cost. Hash chain integrity confirmed by the chained-from-genesis property: the start row's `previous_event_hash = "0"*64` (genesis sentinel because the test's tenant alpha was freshly migrated at session start), the end row's `previous_event_hash = start.this_event_hash`. Intermediate events (per-tool-call, per-iteration) were not needed because the McKinsey path is single-turn; multi-iteration paths will surface in evidence later, and the audit's two-row pattern is the right baseline. The `AgentResult.audit_start_hash` and `audit_end_hash` fields are now load-bearing for downstream auditors to deep-link from the agent response to the chain rows — this is the load-bearing reason for D88's widening of `AuditPort.emit` to return the persisted event.

## P8 — S28b — Tool registry context, classification taxonomy, two-thin-ports replacement of retrieval branch, BC stub at revision creation
roles: architect, engineer
mode: build

- Correction to S27b session log entry. S27b's entry claimed "the bet's success criterion 2 at Phase 1 close (one agent running) is met." The actual criterion language reads "one agent can be configured, run, audited, and optimised through the platform's own tooling." S27b proves run and audit. Configuration via platform CLI lands at S30b. Optimisation surface remains gated on P11. Recording this correction here per session-log append-only discipline; the criterion in `phase-1-prd.md` stands as the Phase 1 close target and the close audit makes the real call.

- Produced: Ten commits closing S28b per the brief. Commit 1 (`docs(p8/s28b)`, 77b7930) landed D89 at `charter/decisions.md` covering tool registry aggregate + classification-to-invariant mapping + two-thin-ports replacement of retrieval branch + BC stub at revision creation + Phase 1 authoring prohibition on high-classification tools; three deferred-decisions entries at `charter/deferred-decisions.md` (per-invocation confirmation pathway; rich BC testing; automated adoption flow on BC pass); `charter/packages/p8-epic.md` S28b line rewrite plus S30b drafted-artifact sentence; `charter/current-package.md` transition to S28b in flight; brief preserved at `briefs/p8/s28b.md`. Commit 2 (`feat(p8/s28b)`, 9b400d1) shipped `Tool` + `ToolRevision` aggregates at `contexts/tools/domain/tool.py` with the six-category D89 Classification enum + PHASE_1_PROHIBITED + PHASE_1_VISIBLE sets + ToolDefinition VO; `ToolRepositoryPort` Protocol + `RoleToolBinding` DTO; `ToolPostgresRepository` adapter against the control-plane Postgres instance; Alembic 0009_create_tools_tables (control plane) creating `tools` + `tool_revisions` tables with classification CHECK constraint + bc_result forward-affordance column, seeding the retrieval tool at the well-known UUID `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001` (revision 1 at UUID `...0002`) so platform-managed role allowlists have a durable anchor; charter/schema.md "Tool registry (control plane)" section; 15 unit tests + 9 integration tests against live control-plane Postgres; 24 import-linter contracts kept (was 23). Commit 3 (`feat(p8/s28b)`, feef2fa) shipped `tool_invocation_service.py` with `list_visible_definitions` (filters by PHASE_1_VISIBLE_CLASSIFICATIONS) and `check_invocation_admissibility` (defensive invariant check returning InvocationAdmissibility with the three-to-three invariant_index per D89's mapping); five CRUD use cases with operator-context auth; create_tool rejects high classifications with ClassificationProhibitedError naming the per-invocation confirmation pathway deferred-decisions entry; 30 new unit tests. Commit 4 (`refactor(p8/s28b)`, 1335600) migrated role.tool_allowlist from `tuple[str, ...]` to `tuple[ToolAllowlistEntry, ...]`. `ToolAllowlistEntry` lands in `shared_kernel/types.py` because both methodology and agent contexts reference the exact shape. Two Alembic migrations forward-and-back-verified: control-plane 0010_role_tool_allowlist_pin (recomputes role revision hashes per D74's chain-self-containment) and per-tenant 0010_agent_tool_allowlist_pin (cross-plane backfill via sync engine against control plane mirroring S26a-2's pattern). Cascade through agent context: MethodologyView, RoleView, EffectiveConstraintBundle, composition.py (tighten intersects by tool_id), agent use case _content_payload, agent postgres adapter, AgentLoopExecutor's retrieval check shape. CLI parser at `apps/cli/_role.py:_to_tool_allowlist` accepts both legacy string-shape entries (resolved via `_NAMED_TOOL_PINS` for "retrieval") and explicit dict-shape entries. 11 unit + integration tests updated from string allowlists to ToolAllowlistEntry tuples. Commit 5 (`refactor(p8/s28b)`, 1fe0f22) shipped the two thin ports: `ToolDefinitionsLookup` Protocol (takes allowlist tuple → inference-context ToolDefinition tuple) and `ToolInvoker` Protocol (takes ToolCall + tenant_context → ToolInvocationResult carrying outcome + payload + invariant_index). `TerminationReason` enum gains `INVARIANT_BLOCKED`. `AgentInvocationContext` gains `tool_definitions: tuple[ToolDefinition, ...]` at the ports layer rather than on EffectiveConstraintBundle in domain because the agent domain cannot import inference.ToolDefinition per the cross-context independence contract — the resolution surfaced when `lint-imports` broke at the first attempt. AgentLoopExecutor refactor: constructor takes tool_invoker; loop body reads `context.tool_definitions` for the LiteLLM `tools` parameter; dispatches each tool_call through `self._tool_invoker`; branches on ToolInvocationResult.outcome. invoke_agent use case wires the new ports. 8 new executor unit tests + 6 invoke_agent test updates + 1 contract test update. Commit 6 (`feat(p8/s28b)`, 60723b8) shipped the schema-diff BC stub at `contexts/tools/application/backward_compatibility.py`: BCResult carries PASSED/FAILED outcome plus reason string; check_revision_compatibility compares parameters and returns schemas with conservative narrowing-fails-widening-passes semantics; create_tool_revision use case runs the BC check against the predecessor revision and stores the result on `ToolRevision.bc_result`; ToolPostgresRepository.list_roles_using_tool computes `RoleToolBinding.can_auto_adopt` by walking the BC chain between current and latest. 13 new unit tests. Commit 7 (`feat(p8/s28b)`, b4d6795) wired the two new ports at `apps/cli/_cross_context.py`: ToolDefinitionsLookupAdapter translates tools-context ToolDefinition to inference-context ToolDefinition; ToolInvokerAdapter does two-step dispatch (defensive invariant check via tools-context invocation service + Phase 1 retrieval routing to AgentRetrievalClient). Retrieval-specific helpers relocated here from the executor; 6 new integration tests with in-memory fakes. Commit 8 (`feat(p8/s28b)`, 20755d7) shipped the `padhanam tool create | get | list` CLI at `apps/cli/_tool.py` with config-file authoring; create rejects financial / communication / legal with exit code 2 and the prohibition message naming the per-invocation confirmation pathway deferred-decisions entry; 8 integration tests against live control plane. Commit 9 (`test(p8/s28b)`, b5d6fb1) added `test_tool_invocation_threads_tenant_context_correctly_per_tenant` to the agent isolation contract test file. The brief's commit-9 framing presumed per-tenant tool storage ("tenant A's tools not visible from tenant B"); the implemented test reflects the D89 control-plane resolution with storage-level isolation pushed to the Phase 2 deferred-decisions entry. This session-log entry is commit 10.

- Decisions: D89 (Tool registry aggregate, classification-to-invariant mapping, two-thin-ports replacement of retrieval branch, backward-compatibility stub at revision creation, Phase 1 authoring prohibition on high-classification tools). D89 absorbs the control-plane storage resolution as alternative (h) per the pre-write reconciliation user-question: tools live alongside methodologies and roles on control plane rather than per-tenant, with per-tenant tool authoring deferred to Phase 2 per the customer-deployment-evidence trajectory. Three new deferred-decisions entries: per-invocation human-in-the-loop confirmation pathway (activation: first high-classification tool authored), rich BC testing for tool revisions (activation: second revision producing false BC result), automated adoption flow for BC-passed revisions (activation: first BC-passed revision in production). Kano: must-have on tool aggregate + classification taxonomy + invariant mapping at invocation boundary + two-thin-ports replacement (held in code at Phase 1); performance on BC test mechanism (refines at Phase 2 with consumer evidence); delighter on the two-thin-ports pattern's third reinforcement establishing Phase 1 norm.

- Tests: 709 tests pass at session close (up from 619 at S27b close; +90 net). Composition: 596 unit + 12 enforcement at the AST layer + 14 apps/cli integration (including 6 cross-context adapter tests + 8 padhanam tool CLI tests) + 12 contract tests (including 1 new tool-invocation tenant-isolation test) + ~75 other integration. Four Alembic migrations forward-and-back-verified twice each: control-plane 0009_create_tools_tables (creates tools + tool_revisions; seeds retrieval at fixed UUID); control-plane 0010_role_tool_allowlist_pin (converts role_revisions.tool_allowlist + recomputes role hashes); per-tenant 0010_agent_tool_allowlist_pin (converts agent_revisions.tool_allowlist with cross-plane lookup against control-plane tools table on downgrade). Import-linter: 24 contracts kept (was 23 at S27b); new layers-tools hexagonal contract; cross-context independence contracts gained `contexts.tools.{domain,application,adapters}` modules. AST enforcement: 9 tests pass, auto-discovers the new context via the workspace manifest. Workspace registration: `contexts/tools` added to uv workspace members + `[tool.uv.sources]` + the `apps/api/Dockerfile` COPY directives.

- methodology: Pre-write reconciliation surfaced a load-bearing architectural tension at session-open: the prompt's commit 2 said tools live in per-tenant Postgres, but commit 4's allowlist tuple-shape pin lives on `role_revisions` in control plane. Cross-plane reference (control-plane role.tool_allowlist → per-tenant tool UUIDs) requires either same-UUID seeding across all tenants (write-to-all-tenants CLI without precedent) or name-resolution-at-runtime (defeats the tuple-pin shape). The user-question moment surfaced three resolution candidates with previews; the operator chose control-plane storage (alongside methodologies and roles per the existing template-precedent), with per-tenant tool authoring deferred to Phase 2. D89's Appendix A text in the brief was adjusted at commit 1 to absorb the choice as alternative (h) and to add the explicit "Storage location" section. This is the fifth instance of pre-write-reconciliation-as-architectural-discovery (S26a-1 brief-time three reconciliations; S26a-2 MethodologyView extension; S26b D87 emergence; S27b D88 emergence; S28b D89 storage-location resolution) — the discipline pattern is now load-bearing for the methodology-implementer mode and warrants formal phase-audit promotion to `charter/methodology.md`.

Reflection (three prompts per the brief):

1. **Classification-to-invariant mapping at first runtime exercise.** The three-to-three mapping (financial → 1, communication → 2, legal → 3) is committed at D89 but tested without a real high-classification tool shipped. The defensive invariant check at invocation felt necessary for two reasons that the writing surfaced: (a) defence-in-depth — the ToolDefinitionsLookup adapter applies PHASE_1_VISIBLE_CLASSIFICATIONS filtering at the visible-tools surface, but a future bypass catches at the invariant boundary instead of allowing a high-classification call to slip through; (b) the seam is a fixed point — the activating session for the per-invocation confirmation pathway deferred-decisions entry can refactor the defensive check into the pause-and-confirm point rather than restructuring the executor, because the structural commitment to "invocation goes through ToolInvoker and INVARIANT_BLOCKED is a TerminationReason" is in code. The absence of a Phase 1 high-classification tool means the three shape candidates in the confirmation-pathway deferred-decisions entry (hand-off / pause-and-confirm / queue-and-resume) remain unrefined; the activating session will produce the consumer evidence (which surface — CLI, future UI, API client — is the actual user environment; whether the action is reversible if mis-authorised; how long the user needs to review) that drives the shape choice. The deferred-decisions entry's three candidates are well-shaped for the choice as-written; refinement before the activating session would be premature paper architecture.

2. **Two thin ports versus one fat registry port at first reinforcement.** S26a-1 (MethodologyLookup), S26a-2 (RoleLookup), S27b (AgentRetrievalClient + MethodologyOverridesLookup) each split related concerns into separate ports. S28b reinforces the pattern with `ToolDefinitionsLookup` and `ToolInvoker`. The split felt right at the AgentLoopExecutor's refactor for a structural reason that became clear during implementation: the read shape (what tools are available, formatted for the LLM) and the action shape (invoke this specific tool_call with this argument payload) have different evolution velocities. The read shape evolves with classification policy. The action shape evolves with tool-specific dispatch. Collapsing into one ToolRegistry port would couple the agent loop's stability to both evolution velocities simultaneously; a change to classification policy would touch the action surface, and a new tool registration would touch the read surface. The two-thin-ports split surfaced this naturally because the AgentLoopExecutor's body reads `context.tool_definitions` once (at LiteLLM-call construction) and calls `self._tool_invoker` per tool_call (at dispatch). Different read/write rates → different ports. The pattern is now a Phase 1 norm worth promoting to `charter/methodology.md` at the next phase audit: "for any consumer port that surfaces a read shape and an action shape with different evolution velocities, split into two thin ports rather than collapsing into one fat registry."

3. **Schema-diff BC stub at first revision creation.** The stub ran exactly twice in this session — both at commit 6's unit tests against a fake repository, never against a real second revision in the live DB (the session authored zero tool revisions beyond the migration-seeded retrieval-revision-1). The stub's behaviour at unit-test exercise was clean: pass cases (identical schemas, optional-field addition, type widening to nullable variant) and fail cases (removed fields, newly-required, type narrowing, returns narrowing) all produced the expected BCOutcome. The rich-BC-testing deferred-decisions entry's activation trigger ("second tool revision in a tenant produces a false positive or false negative from the schema-diff stub") feels well-calibrated for the right reason: the stub covers structural shape diff but not semantic behavioural compatibility; the first real evidence will be a revision where the BC stub said "passed" but a consumer broke, or said "failed" but the consumer didn't care about the structural change. Either evidence shape drives the same architectural refinement (contract tests per tool / scenario-based regression / type-evolution rules). The BCResult shape (passed/failed with reason) is the right signal carrier for the adoption flow Phase 2 will build because the rendering at the binding-adoption UX is binary-with-explanation: "tool X has 3 revisions ahead of your binding; auto-adopt safe? No — reason: parameter 'limit' became required at revision 2." The integration with the role-tool query surface (`list_roles_using_tool.can_auto_adopt`) showed that the BCResult shape suffices for the query's computation — walking the chain and short-circuiting on the first FAILED outcome is O(N) and the reason field surfaces at the UX layer per binding. A richer result type (e.g., a structured per-property diff) would over-commit on the rendering surface ahead of consumer evidence. The stub-plus-result-shape commitment is the right Phase 1 substrate.
